# CHANNELS.md — the divergence ledger

Every **intended** difference between `bpcl_contact_center` (CC) and `bpcl_showroom_crc` (CRC).

> **The rule:** a difference between the two channels that is not recorded here is a drift bug.
> New intended difference → add a row in the same edit that creates it.

Status of this file: **partial.** §2 is confirmed and complete for the two policy axes. §3 is a
verified defect list. §4 is the ~2,800 lines of unclassified divergence still to audit.

---

## 1. What must never differ

Listed in [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md) §4 — topology, handoff contract, tool-turn rule, invisible
switching, emergency override, LPG domain facts, voice/TTS rules, complaint discipline.

Control case: `emergencyAgent` differs by only 34 lines across channels (265 vs 266), and those are
persona lines only. That is roughly what a correctly-maintained shared agent should look like.

---

## 2. Intended differences

### ID-02 · `getConsumerDetails` introduced itself as an A I nobody asked about — the ID-01 "only when asked" guard never reached it, `Default` or `unregisteredComplaintAgent` — CRC only, CC pending (2026-09-30)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Axis 2 (persona) wording, shared-truth intent. Completes the self-intro sweep in `75269a9`. **CC not ported** (CC has no ID-01 yet). |
| **Source** | CRC call, `getConsumerDetails`' first line, unasked: *"जी, मैं वाणी हूँ, भारत पेट्रोलियम के भारत गैस C R C की Virtual Voice A I Assistant। कृपया अपना registered मोबाइल नंबर बताइये।"* The consumer had already heard `Default`'s greeting with her name. |
| **Diagnosis** | `75269a9` added one sentence to every post-switch ID-01 block: *"This answer exists only for that question … never as an opener, never after a switch, never tacked onto an answer."* It missed five files: `getConsumerDetailsLive.txt` (deployed), `getConsumerDetails.txt`, `getConsumerDetails-unreg.txt`, `Default.txt` and `unregisteredComplaintAgent.txt`. Without it, ID-01 is a quoted, ready-made Hindi line with no limit on when to say it. The Live and unreg variants also only said *"do not introduce yourself"* and never said what that covers. |
| **The rule now** | ① All five files carry the guard. In the getConsumerDetails files it also says the answer is never folded into the number request. `Default`'s version exempts its own opening greeting, which already says her name. ② The Live and unreg variants spell out the no-intro rule (never her name, Vaani, Voice Assistant, A I, or where she works unless just asked) and quote the live-call line as WRONG. `getConsumerDetails.txt` adds that line to its WRONG first lines. |
| **Deliberately NOT changed** | `Default`'s greeting (*"नमस्ते, मेरा नाम वाणी है …"*). The truthful answer when the consumer does ask. `unregisteredComplaintAgent` was converted from "I" to "You" in the same pass, pronouns only, with no rule changed. Her spoken Hindi lines and spelled abbreviations (A I, I S I, I V R S, I D) are untouched. |
| **⚠️ Open** | `vaaniQA` has no check for an identity line the consumer did not ask for. **CC not ported.** |

### HOFF-03 · The consumer's own request rides in `Context`; a second problem is kept as "consumer also says"; the escalation handoff no longer orders a complaint — CRC only, CC pending (2026-09-29)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth** (the handoff contract). `Default`, `routingAgent`, and the ten complaint-registering agents. Completes HOFF-02. **CC not ported.** |
| **Client framing** | `handoffSummary` has two parts. `Default` writes the first, always in the fixed `Intent. Context. Please help consumer with.` shape. The platform appends the second, the system record, which differs per agent. Every change here is to the **words inside Default's three slots**. Routing is untouched: the destination is `agentName`, plus the platform's family resolution. |
| **The rule now** | ① **`Default` build rule:** what the consumer explicitly asked for (a complaint, a person, a callback, a number) goes into `Context` in their words, e.g. *"consumer asked to register a complaint"*. It never goes in NEXT_ACTION and never changes where the call is routed. ② **Two problems:** routing stays exactly as today, and the problem the chosen agent does not own goes into `Context` as *"consumer also says …"*. Context parts are separated by semicolons. ③ **Escalation handoff** (`Default` COMPLAINT ESCALATION, `routingAgent` MODE A): *"Please register a complaint and tell consumer the team will contact them"* is gone. The request is in `Context`, the next action is neutral, and it still routes to `genericInfoComplaintAgent`, which registers first and transfers by its own rules. HOFF-02's "one exception" is removed. `Default` line 504 no longer quotes the old line. `routingAgent`'s format rule gets the same Intent/NEXT_ACTION/Context rules. ④ **Receiving agents (ten):** a new **THE CONSUMER'S OWN REQUEST IN handoffSummary** rule. A request already made is never asked for again. A complaint request is honoured once the record has been stated, if the problem still stands: the call alone, with no text, and no "do you want it?". A person request follows SENIOR TEAM TRANSFER as before. |
| **Worked example (29-09 call)** | Default's part would read: *"Intent: late delivery by distributor. Context: consumer says Morsa Gas Agency delivers late every time; consumer also says bookings do not show in the system; consumer asked for the sales officer's number. Please help consumer with booking status and delivery."* |
| **Deliberately NOT changed** | The parked `newConnectionAgent.txt` (still holds the old escalation line; not deployed). `genericInfoComplaintAgent`'s guard that the old line never overrides its GATE A/B (harmless, and it covers any stale summary). `unregisteredComplaintAgent`, which receives no `Default` handoff. |
| **⚠️ Open** | **CC not ported.** |

### HOFF-02 · `Default`'s handoff never says "complaint" in its Intent or its next-action line — CRC only, CC pending (2026-09-29)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth** (the handoff contract). `Default`. Follows BNE-04. Numbered HOFF-02 because HOFF-01 exists on the multilingual branch. **CC not ported.** |
| **Source** | The 29-09 14:10 call behind BNE-04. The consumer asked for the sales officer's number, then said the distributor delivers late every time and bookings do not show in the system. They never said "complaint". `Default` wrote *"Intent: booking and delivery complaint … Please help consumer with booking and delivery complaint."* |
| **Diagnosis** | `Default` line 503 already says "complaint" is an action word, never a topic, and line 504 says a register instruction tells the receiving agent the work is already decided. Its own examples taught the opposite: 16 example handoffs put "complaint" in the Intent or in "Please help consumer with". The receiving agent reads either as a decision to register. |
| **The rule now** | The STAGE 3 build rule: INTENT names the problem the consumer has, never the word "complaint". NEXT_ACTION names what they need help with, never "complaint" and never "register". The next agent, which holds the record, decides whether a complaint is registered. All 16 examples rewritten (e.g. *"Intent: recurring rude behaviour by delivery person … Please help consumer with the delivery person's behaviour."*). Three examples whose Context stated the consumer's account as a plain fact now start "consumer says" (CPL-27). |
| **Follow-up** | The escalation handoff, the consumer's own requests and the two-problem case are settled in HOFF-03. |

### BNE-04 · `bookingNonEligibilityAgent` offered a complaint on its first turn because the handoff said "complaint", then typed the call out with a stage direction and an invented number — CRC only, CC pending (2026-09-29)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth** (TOOL_CALLING.md rule 2). `bookingNonEligibilityAgent`. **CC not ported.** |
| **Source** | Live call 29-09-2026 14:12. Handoff from `Default`: *"Intent: booking and delivery complaint. Context: consumer says distributor (…) does not deliver on time and bookings are not appearing in the system. Please help consumer with booking and delivery complaint."*, followed by the system record *"LPG refill was booked on 2026-09-25 … planned for Delivery"*. Turn 1 stated the open order correctly, then added that she was registering a complaint "so our team can investigate" and asked permission. Turn 2, on the consumer's "हाँ, मेरा booking भी हुआ है": the tool name spoken, a stage direction *"(शिकायत दर्ज करने के बाद)"*, an invented 10-digit number, a team-contact promise and the close question. No tool call. Client confirmed the platform schema is the trimmed two-key one, the same as every other agent. |
| **Diagnosis** | The TOOL_CALLING §4 shape checks all pass for this file. The failure is in the flow. ① **The handoff's "complaint" read as an instruction.** Both the Intent and the "Please help consumer with" line said complaint, and nothing told the agent that this was the previous agent's label rather than a decision. ② **Two rules licensed a first-turn complaint.** The opening pattern's *"Give the resolution path immediately — what the consumer should do next"* and THE ONE CARVE-OUT's *"register directly"* (a cylinder not delivered is on its list). Both collided with OPEN ORDER's own *"nothing to fix, no complaint"*. ③ **Step 6 fired on a relayed claim.** "Bookings are not appearing" arrived in Context and read as a data dispute, although the system sentence right below showed the booking. Step 6 also said *"on that same turn"* without *"the call alone"*. Once turn 1 had announced the complaint, turn 2 typed it out (rule 2). |
| **The rule now** | ① New **THE PREVIOUS AGENT'S WORDS NEVER DECIDE A COMPLAINT** under TWO KINDS OF TEXT. Intent and "Please help consumer with" are a topic label. The first turn is the block and its resolution, and nothing about a complaint. What the consumer says next decides Step 6, Step 6A or the carve-out, and each is the call alone. ② Opening pattern item 3: the resolution path is the block's own line, never a complaint, and never mentioned on the first turn. ③ Carve-out: still direct, now *"the call alone, with no text"*, applying after the first turn has stated the record. ④ Step 6: a dispute is the consumer disputing the record *after* it was stated. A Context claim the record contradicts is answered by the record. The call is on the turn they dispute it, alone, with no text. |
| **⚠️ Open** | **`Default`'s handoff wording** is the upstream cause (the complaint labels are fixed by HOFF-02; the rest is open): its examples put "complaint" in Intent (10 places) and in "Please help consumer with" (14 places), it merged two problems into one handoff, and its escalation handoff scripts *"tell consumer the team will contact them"*. Under discussion with the client, not changed here. **CC not ported.** |

### NUM-08 · A real complaint number read out as an amount ("एक करोड़ सत्ताईस लाख …") — the general number rule sent IDs to Hindi words — CRC only, CC pending (2026-09-28)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — NUM-01 compliance. The eleven CRC agents that speak a complaint number. **CC not ported.** |
| **Source** | Live call 28-09-2026 16:40, `postDeliveryAgent`. The tool was called correctly and returned a real number (12701511, the backend's 8-digit shape). Vaani said *"आपकी शिकायत संख्या एक करोड़ सत्ताईस लाख एक हज़ार पाँच सौ ग्यारह है"*. |
| **Diagnosis** | Three places disagreed. ① The general rule near the top of five agents (the four delivery agents and genericInfo): *"I never speak dates, times, money, counts, **or IDs** as raw digits. I always convert into **natural Hindi words**."* A complaint number is an ID, and its "natural Hindi words" are an amount, so the output obeyed the first rule it read. ② `DIGIT WORDS`, nine lines later, had the right rule: English digit words with `" - "`, never one whole number. ③ The complaint-number exception in ten agents said *"I speak it **whole**, in ONE turn"*, and "whole" reads as "one whole number", the opposite of ②. The tool's message delivers the number as numerals, which leaves the model to pick a form. NUM-07 (22-09) fixed the same failure in `connectionServicesAgent` alone; it did not touch ①. |
| **The rule now** | ① In the five agents: dates, times, money and counts go to Hindi words, and a complaint number, phone number or any other long number is never turned into an amount (never करोड़, लाख, हज़ार or सौ); it goes one digit word at a time per LONG NUMBER DELIVERY. ② *"speak it whole, in ONE turn"* → *"speak all its digits in ONE turn the first time, one digit word at a time with `" - "` between them per DIGIT WORDS, never as one whole number or an amount"* (ten agents). `unregisteredComplaintAgent`'s *"in ONE go, the whole thing"* got the same rewrite. ③ `COMPLAINT TOOL` step 3 in all eleven: the number arrives in the message as numerals, each digit is its own English digit word with `" - "`, never read as a whole and never as an amount. |
| **Deliberately NOT changed** | The same call's *"एस एम एस (S M S)"* double form (client: not in scope). The *"Every number becomes Hindi words"* line in the address-reading blocks, which is scoped to addresses. `paymentAgent` and both booking agents already sent IDs and long numbers to LONG NUMBER DELIVERY in their general rule, so only ② and ③ landed there. |
| **⚠️ Open** | **CC not ported.** |

### CPL-27 · Complaint calls typed out as text with invented keys and numbers; the empathy rule's "next step" became the registering line; the consumer's own date read back as the system booking date — CRC only, CC pending (2026-09-28)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. The eleven CRC agents that register a complaint, the six delivery/booking agents that read a booking date from `handoffSummary`, plus `Default` and `routingAgent`. **CC not ported.** |
| **Source** | Four CRC calls on 28-09-2026 (`genericInfoComplaintAgent` ×3, `activeDeliveryAgent` ×1). (1) 14:54 genericInfo spoke the tool name aloud, in backticks, and the reason phrases *"Rude Behavior"* / *"Cylinder Delivery"*, with no call. (2) 15:02–15:08 and 10:08–10:13 genericInfo, 14:23 activeDelivery: every tool (`bpcl_create_complaint`, `calltransfer`, `callhangup`) written out as text in `(tool(key=value…))` form after a sentence announcing it, then a complaint number spoken with no Result. Client confirmed every number was invented. (3) 14:23 activeDelivery spoke the booking date as *15 September*. The handoff held Default's *"Context: consumer booked and confirmed on 15th September"* **and** the backend's *"LPG refill was booked on 2026-09-26 …"*. |
| **Diagnosis — not a regression** | No prompt change since CPL-25 (18-09) touches genericInfo's complaint path; `75269a9` (25-09) changed one ID-01 line there. The same shapes are on record since CPL-11/12 (07-08), CPL-17 (18-08), TOOL-06 (14-09), CPL-21 (16-09), CPL-23 (18-09) and CPL-26 (21-09). |
| **Diagnosis — the invented keys are the platform's** | The typed-out complaint calls carry `caseId`, `caseNumber`, `error`, `errorCode` and `validatedContactNumber`; `callhangup` carries `closeReason`/`reason`/`trigger`, and `calltransfer` carries `preToolMessage`/`callTransferReason`. None of these is in any CRC agent prompt (`preToolMessage` left them at TOOL-07). They come from the platform's tool definitions. PARAM-01 (03-09) saw only `caseId`/`caseNumber`, so the definitions grew after that. Their values are placeholders (*123*, *12345*, *9999999999*, *1234567890*) in the prompt's spoken number-word form. The spoken complaint numbers (*456789* after `caseNumber="four hundred fifty-six"`, *9466521346* then *…347* after caseId 123 then 124) match none of the 10 real case numbers in the 1,609-row contact report, which are all 8-digit `1248xxxx`. **Platform fix (client, in progress):** expose only `complaintSummary` + `complaintReason` on `bpcl_create_complaint`, and trim `calltransfer` / `callhangup` the same way. |
| **Diagnosis — the prompt side** | ① **The empathy rule demanded the announcement.** CLS-03's *"every warm sentence carries a fact, an answer, or a concrete next step"* met TOOL-06's no-text complaint turn. On a grievance the only next step available is *"I am registering it"*, so the model says that. Once that sentence exists, it types the call out after it instead of invoking it (CPL-19's mechanism). ② **genericInfo line 196 scripted it outright:** *"I tell them what the record says and that I am registering exactly that mismatch."* ③ **The line-1 directive (CPL-25) was the only backticked tool name** in the ten agents, and it was spoken with its backticks. ④ **`handoffSummary` holds two kinds of text and the agents trusted both equally.** Line 13 said *"I treat everything it states as established truth"*. Default wrote the consumer's claim as a plain fact (*"consumer booked and confirmed"*, its own handoff examples ×3), so the consumer's date and the system's date competed, and nothing ranked them. |
| **The rule now** | ① Every complaint-registering agent (the ten plus `unregisteredComplaintAgent`): directly after the empathy rule, *a complaint I am about to register is never that next step*. The complaint turn is the call alone, and the warmth goes on the next turn with the tool's message. ② genericInfo line 196: tell them what the record says and nothing about a complaint; the mismatch is registered per COMPLAINT TOOL on a turn of its own. ③ Line-1 directive: backticks removed, **tool name kept** (client — it demonstrably helps). `subsidyAgent`'s one other backtick (around `others`) went too; no backtick remains in a live CRC agent prompt. ④ The four delivery agents and both booking agents: *"the system record in it"* is established truth, and a new **handoffSummary CARRIES TWO KINDS OF TEXT** rule says the Context line is the consumer's account, the backend's status sentences are the record, and **the system sentence wins on disagreement**. A date only in Context is never spoken as the booking date. activeDelivery's and postDelivery's BOOKING DATE and bookingNonEligibility's LAST BOOKING DATE now name the system sentence as their source. `Default` (STAGE 3 build rule and five examples) and `routingAgent` (format rule) write consumer facts as *"consumer says …"*, never as a bare *"booked on"*, which is how the system states the real date. The example uses a placeholder, not a date. |
| **Deliberately NOT changed** | The tool-recovery rule and line 1's *"If no Result arrives, invoke it again"*. They cause a double call only when a Result really is late, and the double `calltransfer` on 28-09 came from a typed-out first attempt, which never ran. `vaaniQA` and the parked `newConnectionAgent.txt` keep their backticks. |
| **What separates the working tool from the broken ones (client, 28-09)** | `switchagent` is invoked correctly in every agent, leaves included, so neither the history theory first recorded here nor TOOL-01's "tool-selection load" explains the other three. The difference is **prompt-schema agreement**. `switchagent` is the only tool whose prompt `Parameters:` block names exactly the keys the model is asked to fill (`agentName`, `handoffSummary`), each with its value, and the only one whose typed-out form has never been seen. The other three all ask for keys the prompt never teaches or tells the model not to fill: `bpcl_create_complaint` (seven keys against two taught), `calltransfer` (`callTransferReason`, `preToolMessage`; the prompt has **no** `Parameters:` block for it), `callhangup` (`closeReason`, `reason`, `trigger`, `preToolMessage`; its `Parameters:` block holds the spoken closing line, not a parameter, and every typed-out hangup put that line into `preToolMessage`). The typed-out calls fill **every** schema key with placeholders, so the model is visibly trying to satisfy a schema the prompt contradicts. |
| **✅ Confirmed working (client, 2026-09-29)** | After this row and NUM-08, every CRC tool is invoked correctly: `bpcl_create_complaint`, `calltransfer`, `callhangup`, `switchagent`. None is typed out as text or narrated. The method is written down as the reference in [TOOL_CALLING.md](TOOL_CALLING.md); future tool-calling issues start there. |
| **⚠️ Open** | **Platform (client, in progress):** each tool's schema must hold exactly the keys its prompt teaches. That means `bpcl_create_complaint` down to its two, and `calltransfer` / `callhangup` trimmed the same way, or their required keys taught in the prompt with values the way `switchagent`'s are. **Prompt follow-up once the schemas are final:** give `calltransfer` and `callhangup` a `Parameters:` block that states their real keys (or says plainly that they take none), matching `switchagent`'s. If typed-out calls persist after both, CPL-19's shape (the registering sentence inside the call) is the next thing to try. **CC not ported.** |

### UNREG-03 · Two lookups, then silence; `unregisteredComplaintAgent` registers through `bpcl_create_unregistered_complaint` — the IVRS unregistered flow ported to CRC in Hindi — CRC only (2026-09-28)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Escalation. Client instruction. **CRC only; does not port to CC**, which has no `unregisteredComplaintAgent` and whose `getConsumerDetails` still escalates by `calltransfer` on the no-data path. The reference is the multilingual IVRS pair on branch `multilingual-vijay-contact-center` (`bpcl_prompts_multilingual/bpcl_ivrs_support/`: `getConsumerDetailsLive.txt`, `unregisteredComplaintAgent.txt`), written for Hindi with CRC's channel layer kept. |
| **Supersedes** | UNREG-01 and UNREG-02, which live **only on the unmerged `unreg` branch** (never landed on `main`) and are superseded rather than merged. Where they differ: UNREG-02 kept a refusal ending with `calltransfer`/`callhangup` and deleted `getConsumerDetails-unreg.txt`; registered through `bpcl_create_complaint` with a third `ConsumerDetailsConsumerName` parameter; spelled the PIN parameter `Pincode`; made the district gate retry without limit; had no `updateContact` and no `switchagent`. All six are reversed here. Its tested Hindi PIN/district wording was reused. |
| **`getConsumerDetails-unreg.txt` — rewritten from `getConsumerDetailsLive.txt`** | The unregistered-number stage now runs the deployed Live loop (running number, `validatecontactno` on every digit turn, the `Next`-field states, `validatedContactNumber`), with STEP 7's one second chance. **It owns no ending**: `calltransfer` and `callhangup` are removed, and after the second empty fetch it produces nothing — the platform switches to `unregisteredComplaintAgent`. `getConsumerDetailsLive.txt` and `getConsumerDetails.txt` are **not changed** and keep their `calltransfer` ending. |
| **The platform switches on two failed fetches and nothing else** | Confirmed with the client: the switch fires only after `bpcl_fetch_all_api` has failed twice. So every road to the silent ending must pass through two fetches, or the consumer is left in dead air. (1) **"No registered number" / refusal** → asked, without a cap, for whichever number they use (the one they are calling from, a family member's) — no ending of its own. Client rationale: unregistered consumers mostly ask about new connections and are sent to `newConnectionAgent` from `Default`, so this path is rare. (2) **No second number, or the same number again** → the first confirmed number is silently re-validated and fetched as the second attempt, with no re-confirmation. (3) **An incomplete fetch that survives its one repair** counts as a failed fetch for that number. A third fetch never runs. |
| **`unregisteredComplaintAgent` — the IVRS tool chain** | Tools: `updateContact`, `get_pincode_data`, `bpcl_create_unregistered_complaint`, `calltransfer`, `switchagent`, `callhangup`. `bpcl_create_complaint` is now **forbidden** to it. Order: offer-and-name in one turn → name read back once in Devanagari → `updateContact` (`ConsumerDetailsConsumerName`) → PIN code → `get_pincode_data` (**`pinCode`**) → district confirmed aloud from the Result → `bpcl_create_unregistered_complaint` with exactly three parameters: `ConsumerDetailsConsumerName`, `complaintReason`, `complaintSummary`. **`complaintReason` is the IVRS nine-value list, copied exactly** — no `others`; a ZIP grievance is `10 kg Bharat Gas Lite Zip cylinder`, anything else unmatched `14.2 kg Subsidized domestic LPG connection`. A district that will not resolve gets **one more try**, then no complaint and the agent stays on the line (the IVRS rule, replacing UNREG-02's unlimited retry). |
| **Where the issue comes from** | `{{handoffSummary}}` is Default's summary. Order: handoffSummary → what the consumer said earlier in the call (the transcript is visible) → only then one question. |
| **CRC's one difference from IVRS: `calltransfer`** | Kept, as in the branch's multilingual CRC port: T1–T4 per SENIOR TEAM TRANSFER, never volunteered except T3 on a failed unregistered complaint, once per call and surviving a switch (XFER-05). IVRS holds none. |
| **`switchagent` → `routingAgent` only** | New to CRC's agent (it held none). Only for a **new** topic, after its own work is finished, heard out first: answerable from SECTION 4 → answer; a different grievance → register; another domain, or wanting to actually book/apply for ZIP or a new connection → switch with a meaning-anchor line and a handoffSummary that says the consumer is unregistered. Otherwise the close question, and on a no, `callhangup`. Client accepted that a specialist may receive a consumer with no record — the case is rare. A gas hazard stays **inline** (1906 + safety steps), never a switch. |
| **Kept from CRC, unchanged** | SECTION 2 (Hindi speaking rules, English digit words per NUM-01, the CRC office pair TTS block) and **SECTION 4, the CRC knowledge base, carried over verbatim** by client instruction — including the Mini-plus-ZIP offer on the new-connection hold and CRC's ZIP block. |
| **Tool-turn shape** | CRC TOOL-06/07/08, not IVRS's: `updateContact`, `get_pincode_data`, the complaint and `calltransfer` are the call alone; `switchagent` and `callhangup` carry their line as text on the same turn; **no `preToolMessage` anywhere**. IVRS's own TOOL 5 contradicted its SECTION 9 on `callhangup`; the CRC shape resolves it. |
| **QA side** | `vaaniQA` F8/F9 (confirmed-correct), **C27** (capture: re-asking after "no registered number" is correct; a third number, any line at the no-record moment, or a transfer/offer during capture is not) and **C28** (the no-record complaint shape; asking for a mobile number again is a violation). C1/C5a/C5c/C5j lose the getConsumerDetails connect carve-out; A11-MOB and C23 gain the end of re-asking once two numbers failed; every `bpcl_create_complaint` rule applies equally to `bpcl_create_unregistered_complaint`. |
| **⚠️ Open — noted, not fixed** | SECTION 4 of `unregisteredComplaintAgent` predates ZIP-04 and still carries what ZIP-04 deleted channel-wide: the ₹2,700 + 18% GST cylinder price, the ₹250 regulator, `commercial-lpg.in`, "10 kg" only, and "a domestic product". Kept verbatim per the client's instruction for this change; the `unreg` branch has a ZIP-04-compliant version to port from. |

### TOOL-01 · Every tool turn is one sentence, then the tool, then wait for the Result — `preToolMessage` retired channel-wide — CRC only, CC pending (2026-09-08)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**, and it **reverses CPL-19** three weeks after that entry shipped. Landed in **CRC only**, in every tool-capable agent except `Default`; **CC not ported**. |
| **Source** | Client report, this session: the narration failure is happening on **every tool, in every agent** — not only `bpcl_create_complaint`. Observed transcript: Vaani spoke *"एक मिनट रुकिए। मैं आपकी शिकायत रजिस्टर कर रही हूँ।"* with no tool call, then invented a complaint number on the next turn. Client also reports `callhangup` and `calltransfer` failing the same way: text generated, tool not called. |
| **What this invalidates** | CPL-19's whole rationale was that `callhangup` and `calltransfer` **never fail**, because their line lives in `preToolMessage` and is unreachable without the call. That was **absence of reports, not absence of failures** — a narrated `callhangup` produces dead air, not a wrong sentence, so nobody filed it. The model can generate `preToolMessage` content as its own text. **No channel is safe, and `preToolMessage` protects nothing.** |
| **The evidence that picked the fix** | One agent has never been observed to narrate: **`Default`**. It holds **one** tool with every other tool forbidden by name, its tool turn is reached **positionally** (`switchagent` is allowed ONLY in STAGE 3, so arriving there *is* the call), its text is capped at **one sentence** with "end generation, nothing after the tool call", and its whole contract sits in **one contiguous block**. Every specialist holds **four** tools, each gated behind fresh per-turn judgement, with the contract restated in five scattered places. The failure tracks **tool-selection load and rule dispersion**, not the speech channel. |
| **Platform correction — sequential, not parallel** | Client confirmed this session that the platform does **not** dispatch the tool while the spoken line plays: the line is spoken, *then* the call runs. The `PARALLEL EXECUTION` paragraph in ten prompts asserted the opposite and was **false**; it is rewritten as `SEQUENTIAL EXECUTION` in every file. This also removes CPL-19's last justification — the split into two utterances was chosen purely on latency, and under sequential dispatch both utterances play before the call either way, so the split bought nothing and only created the collapse. |
| **The contract that shipped** | **ONE** — write ONE natural Hindi sentence as own text. **TWO** — invoke the tool on that same turn. **THREE** — stop generating and **wait for the Result**; what is said next is decided by that Result and by nothing else. Identical for all four tools. **No tool takes a `preToolMessage` any more** — not `switchagent`, not `bpcl_create_complaint`, not `calltransfer`, not `callhangup`. The parameter is now named in these prompts only in order to forbid it. |
| **The complaint sentence — client-specified** | The consumer **must** be told a complaint is being registered; that is not optional and it is now the agent's own text that tells them. The worked line, client's own wording: `"जी, सिलेंडर अभी तक नहीं आया — एक मिनट रुकिए, मैं आपकी शिकायत रजिस्टर कर रही हूँ।"` One sentence, problem named and registration announced together. |
| **CPL-19 rules retired** | The fixed `preToolMessage` `"मैं आपकी शिकायत रजिस्टर कर रही हूँ।"` is **deleted from every file** — it survived nowhere except inside WRONG-labelled examples. **THE WAIT CUE IS TERMINAL** is retired: the wait cue is now medial, as it is in `Default`'s anchors. The **TENSE GATE** is re-aimed from the tool's sentence at the agent's own. |
| **Why the fixed sentence became the problem** | CPL-19 claimed it "cannot be spoken at all unless the tool is invoked." That holds only if the sentence exists nowhere but the parameter. It was printed **4–8 times per file** in neutral instructional prose, making it the most-reinforced Hindi sentence in the file — the one sentence the agent must never say. The model never had to compose it; it copied it. Same mechanism CPL-11 proved and CPL-17 was reverted for. **New standing rule: a sentence the agent is forbidden to speak appears only inside a WRONG-labelled example, never in neutral voice.** |
| **Deliberately net-negative** | Nineteen CPL entries each answered a recurrence by adding prose to the file whose size and dispersion is the cause. This pass **removes** words from every file it touches (−149 to −379 words each). Adding another rule was treated as the option of last resort. |
| **`vaaniQA` updated in the same pass** | Left stale it would have flagged the new correct behaviour and spiked the dashboards, exactly as CPL-19 warned. **C3g** rewritten: a registering turn is now ONE utterance, not two, and the single sentence is not an incomplete turn — what is checked is the OUTCOME on the next turn. **C3g-TAIL** re-aimed from "a clause after the wait cue" to "a second sentence on the turn at all". **C5d** rewritten: hearing the transfer sentence once, spoken by Vaani, is now correct. **C25 inverted** — a *reworded* registering sentence is no longer a violation, because Vaani composes it herself now; what is checked is that the one sentence carries both halves and that a tool call went with it. **C26** generalised from the wait cue to every tool: a closing line with no `callhangup`, a transfer line with no `calltransfer`, a "देखती हूँ" with no `switchagent` — one defect in different words. |
| **Files (18)** | The ten registering agents, plus `routingAgent`, `emergencyAgent`, `newConnectionAgent`, `newConnectionAgent_onHold`, `getConsumerDetails`, `getConsumerDetailsLive`, `getConsumerDetails_MultiToolVersion` — `postCallAnalysis/vaaniQA.txt`, and this ledger. `Default` is **untouched**: it is the reference implementation this pass copies. |
| **⚠️ Platform dependency** | The platform must accept `calltransfer` and `callhangup` **with no `preToolMessage`**, taking the spoken line from the agent's text instead. XFER-03 currently makes `preToolMessage` `calltransfer`'s only parameter. **If the platform rejects a `calltransfer` call with no parameters, this pass breaks transfers** — confirm before deploying. |
| **⚠️ Open — this is prompt-only, and prompt-only may not be enough** | Client asked for a prompt-only fix for now. Three things still stand: **①** there is **no measurement** — nineteen entries and no denominator; a platform log of "text matched a tool-implying pattern and no tool call was emitted" would say whether any of this helped. **②** the only *guarantee* is a platform guard that rejects such a turn before audio plays. **③** the specialists still hold **four** tools against `Default`'s one; **XFER-03**, which deleted `callTransferAgent` and gave `calltransfer` to thirteen agents, raised exactly the selection load this entry blames — worth reopening. Also unported to CC, along with everything CPL-08 onward. |
| **⚠️ Not fixed here** | The **Result field is still unnamed** — every file says only "the Result field", so "if I cannot point to it there, I speak none" has nothing to point at. Naming it needs the platform's response schema. |

---

### TOOL-02 · The registering line is one short fixed sentence, no wait cue — the cause is interruption and the token cap, not wording — CRC only, CC pending (2026-09-09)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**, a direct follow-up to TOOL-01 landed the same week. **CRC only**; CC still carries the pre-TOOL-01 contract. |
| **Source** | A live `paymentAgent` call, 09-09-2026, plus client answers on the platform. Vaani spoke *"जी, आपकी शिकायत दर्ज की जा रही है। एक मिनट रुकिए।"* and then **wrote the tool call out as spoken text** — `bpcl_create_complaint(complaintReason="others", complaintSummary="Consumer paid ten rupees via PhonePe U P I on twelfth May…` — **cut off mid-parameter**. Ten of her turns in that four-minute call end mid-sentence or mid-word. |
| **What the transcript proved** | Every CRC prompt had already been purged of `name(param="value")` syntax by CPL-18, and re-checked in this session: **nothing in the prompts demonstrates that shape**. She produced it from her own priors. So the "we teach the bug" mechanism (CPL-11, CPL-17) is **ruled out** for this failure, and no further wording will reliably stop it. |
| **The three platform facts, client-confirmed 09-09-2026** | ① **Barge-in is implemented and cancels the generation.** ② **`max_output_tokens` was 150** (Gemini 3.1 Flash Lite) — Devanagari is token-expensive, and a Hindi sentence plus a composed `complaintSummary` lands at or past that ceiling. ③ **Tool calling is native, not text-parsed** — so a call appearing in the spoken channel is the model using the wrong channel, not the protocol working as designed. |
| **The mechanism — this is the actual root cause** | The output stream is `[spoken text tokens] → [tool call tokens]`. **The tool call is always last, so it is always the casualty** — of a barge-in cancelling the turn, or of the token cap truncating it. The client's own observation closes the loop: the registering line appeared **three times** in that call *because the customer kept interrupting*. She tried to register three times and the call died mid-stream every time. |
| **Why `Default` never fails — corrected from TOOL-01** | TOOL-01 attributed it to holding one tool and a positional trigger. The truer reason is simpler: **`Default`'s line is one short sentence**, so the tool call is reached in well under a second of speech, before a human can realistically interrupt. **Window length, not tool count.** |
| **The change** | The registering turn's spoken text is now **one short fixed sentence and nothing else**: `"मैं आपकी शिकायत रजिस्टर कर रही हूँ।"` **`"एक मिनट रुकिए"` is deleted from every complaint path** — client instruction: the tool answers in milliseconds, so there is nothing to wait through, and a wait cue is pure window. The problem restatement is deleted too. Rationale stated in the prompts in the terms that actually apply: *every extra word spoken before the tool call is a word the turn can be cut off in.* |
| **`complaintSummary` absorbs what the speech dropped** | Client instruction: **one sentence, but a complete one.** With nothing descriptive spoken any more, `complaintSummary` is the **only** record the team ever sees — so it must carry what happened, to what, and any figure, date, payment mode or name the consumer gave, actionable cold without calling them back. The old *"in one or two lines"* wording is replaced in all ten agents; a bare label like "payment issue" is explicitly forbidden. |
| **`NO FILLER TURN` ban deleted (reverses CPL-16 Fix 2)** | Client: **fillers are fully allowed**, `"जी, मैं"` included. CPL-16 banned them on the theory that a filler is the audible signature of a missing tool call — but that is a *diagnostic tell*, not a reason to forbid natural speech on a voice call, and it was making `vaaniQA` score allowed behaviour as a violation. Removed from all ten agents; `vaaniQA`'s allowance widened from two tokens to any brief acknowledgement, with an explicit note that a filler is **not** evidence of a missing tool call — C3g and C26 judge that by the missing **outcome**. |
| **`vaaniQA` re-aimed again** | **C3g** — the registering turn is now a bare fixed sentence; do not report it as abrupt. **C3g-TAIL** — anything before *or* after it is the violation, because extra words widen the interruption window. **C25** — a reworded line drops to low severity and is explicitly **not** proof the tool did not fire. **C26** — adds that missed calls **cluster around interruptions**, so check every turn where she was cut off, and that a tool call written out as speech is the same defect caught in the act. |
| **Client action taken in parallel** | `max_output_tokens` raised **150 → 350**. |
| **⚠️ Open — the fix that matters most is still platform-side** | **Barge-in should stop the TTS playback, not the LLM generation.** Today an interruption does not merely cut Vaani off; it silently destroys the tool call she was in the middle of making. Until that changes, shortening the spoken line only narrows the window — it does not close it. Also still open: logging `finishReason` per turn (`MAX_TOKENS` / `STOP` / cancelled) would give the first real denominator this defect has ever had, across twenty-one ledger entries. |
| **⚠️ Also unaddressed** | The **Result field is still unnamed** in all ten agents (TOOL-01's open item). And nothing prevents the model writing the call into the content channel — that is a model-behaviour limit of Flash Lite, not something a prompt can guarantee away. |
| **Extended to every tool, 2026-09-09 (same session)** | Client instruction: apply the same shape to **all tools in all agents except `Default`**. Every tool-capable agent now carries one **THE SHORTER MY/YOUR SENTENCE** paragraph stating the mechanism plainly — *the spoken text comes out before the tool call, so the tool call is the last thing in the turn and the first thing lost when a turn is cut short; the two things that cut a turn short are the consumer speaking over her and the turn running out of room; either way it is the tool call that dies while the consumer has already heard it was happening.* It closes by naming the recovery: a turn that died means the tool did not run, so invoke it first next turn rather than re-speaking the line. Per-tool, `calltransfer`, `callhangup` and `switchagent` each gained an explicit **nothing in front of it, nothing after it** constraint on their one sentence. **Seventeen agents** carry it — the ten registering agents plus `routingAgent`, `emergencyAgent`, `newConnectionAgent`, `newConnectionAgent_onHold`, `getConsumerDetails`, `getConsumerDetailsLive`, `getConsumerDetails_MultiToolVersion`. |
| **The forbidden phrase is not named — corrected 2026-09-09, client** | The first version of this change stated the rule as a prohibition that **quoted the phrase**: *"You do NOT add `एक मिनट रुकिए`"*. That is the same mistake TOOL-01 was written to stop and CPL-17 was reverted for — **a sentence printed in the prompt is a sentence the model can reach for**, and a prohibition is still a printing. Client caught it: *"Agent should not know that there is a line एक मिनट रुकिए which I do not have to speak."* The rule is now purely positive and names nothing: **NOTHING ELSE IS SPOKEN ON THIS TURN** — one sentence is the complete spoken part, nothing in front of it and nothing after it. The phrase, and the term "wait cue" itself, are **deleted from every live agent prompt**, including the recovery enumerations and the WRONG examples that had listed it as a thing not to do. The only surviving instances anywhere are `getConsumerDetails*`'s own fetch line, which she *is* meant to speak, and `vaaniQA`, which is a post-call reviewer and never speaks to a consumer. |
| **Not changed, deliberately** | `getConsumerDetails*`'s fetch line `"जी, एक मिनट रुकिए, में आपकी डिटेल्स चेक करती हूँ।"` keeps its wait cue: it is already one short sentence, and unlike a complaint write it fronts a real API round-trip that the consumer does wait through. `callhangup`'s closing line stays two sentences because it is the fixed brand phrase — **noted as a residual risk**: it is the longest spoken line still sitting in front of a tool call, so a `callhangup` is the most truncation-exposed call left. `getConsumerDetailsLive`'s STEP 5 keeps its deliberate **two-output** split (speech alone, then the tool alone); that trades the truncation window for an extra turn boundary and has not been re-evaluated against this evidence. |
| **Files (20)** | The ten registering agents, `routingAgent`, `emergencyAgent`, `newConnectionAgent`, `newConnectionAgent_onHold`, the three `getConsumerDetails` variants, `postCallAnalysis/vaaniQA.txt`, and this ledger. `Default` untouched. `getConsumerDetails*`'s own fetch-turn wait cue is deliberately left alone — not instructed, and its tool round-trip is a real API call rather than a complaint write. |

---

### TOOL-03 · Tool turns rebuilt to Default's shape — one block, the stop after the call, contradictions removed — CRC only, CC pending (2026-09-11)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**, and a partial **reversal of TOOL-01 and TOOL-02's wording**. Landed in all **17** non-`Default` CRC agents. **CC not ported.** |
| **Source** | Two live calls, 11-09-2026. `calltransfer`: Vaani spoke `"आपकी कॉल ट्रांसफर की जा रही है।"` **ten times** with no call behind any of them, the customer silent or saying "Ok." each time; then spoke the closing line and `callhangup` did not fire either. `bpcl_create_complaint`: the fixed sentence four times, no call. Short line, no barge-in, 350 tokens — **TOOL-02's window/truncation theory does not explain these.** |
| **Client facts, 11-09-2026** | All tools (`bpcl_create_complaint`, `calltransfer`, `callhangup`) **are declared to the agents**, and **in some calls she calls them correctly** — so this is intermittent model behaviour, and the client's position is that **the prompt is the cause.** The audit below confirmed it. |
| **Finding 1 — the prompt told her to stop BEFORE the tool call (introduced by TOOL-02)** | `Default`, the one agent that never fails, says *"call the tool, end generation. **Nothing after the tool call.**"* — the stop lands **after** the call. TOOL-02 wrote, in 1–8 places per agent: *"That sentence is the **WHOLE turn** — nothing in front of it, **nothing after it**"*, *"that line is the **whole turn**… **nothing after it**"*, *"CORRECT, AND THIS IS THE **ENTIRE TURN**: your text is…"*. In the output stream **the tool call is the thing after the sentence**, so a small model reads these as *finish once the sentence is written* — exactly the observed output. The transfer version even read *"ONE: write the sentence. That sentence is the WHOLE turn… TWO: invoke calltransfer"* in a single line. |
| **Finding 2 — the tool rule was scattered and restated ~15 times per agent** | In `paymentAgent` it lived at ten separate places across 350 lines, each worded slightly differently. `FAILED TURN` appeared 5–6× per specialist against `Default`'s 1; `RECOVERY` 6–8× against 4. Many restatements were added by TOOL-01/TOOL-02 themselves — **nineteen-plus entries of prose fixes had grown the problem.** |
| **Finding 3 — live contradictions no earlier sweep caught** | `newConnectionAgent` + `_onHold`: *"write the closing line as your own text, no preToolMessage"* **and** *"For callhangup, generate ZERO conversational text — the platform plays the preToolMessage."* `_onHold` line 93 contradicted itself: *"pass NO preToolMessage… This is the ONE exception to the no-preToolMessage rule — you generate no spoken text."* `routingAgent`: *"write this sentence as your own text"* and, in the same line, *"If you also write that line as your own text the consumer hears it twice."* `getConsumerDetails`: *"That tool call is the entire turn"* followed by *"write this sentence as your text"*, and "transfer" still *"permitted inside that preToolMessage."* `getConsumerDetails` + `_MultiToolVersion` still asserted **PARALLEL** execution, which the client confirmed is false. |
| **The rebuild** | Each complaint agent's tool rules now live in **one block**, `HOW I/YOU TAKE A TOOL TURN — THE SAME WAY FOR EVERY TOOL`, in `Default`'s own shape: *write the one line as text, call the tool on that same turn, end generation, nothing after the tool call.* Each tool gets **one line that shows the sentence and the call together** (`your text is "…" and calltransfer fires`) — the form `Default` uses for `switchagent`. The complaint turn and the transfer turn each shrink to a few lines stating the same shape. **One recovery rule** — each agent's existing per-turn TOOL RECOVERY check, which CPL-09 put in the decision procedure deliberately — and the block points to it instead of restating it. |
| **Deleted** | The three-steps preamble, the window paragraph (TOOL-02), `SEQUENTIAL EXECUTION` as a separate paragraph (its one fact — *the platform speaks the line, then runs the tool* — kept as a sentence in the block), the second `THE TOOL CALL IS THE ACTION — TRUE OF EVERY TOOL` restatement, `THE SECOND REGISTRATION IS WHERE I TYPE THE CALL`, `WHY IT HAPPENS HERE`, `HAVING CALLED THE TOOL SUCCESSFULLY…`, `NOTHING ELSE IS SPOKEN`, the six-line WRONG list, `MY/YOUR SENTENCE IS NOT THE REGISTRATION`, the per-block RECOVERY copies, and both `FAILED TURN` lines in every transfer block. All five contradictions above are resolved. |
| **Kept, unchanged** | The three spoken sentences exactly as the client decided (`मैं आपकी शिकायत रजिस्टर कर रही हूँ।`, the transfer line, the closing line); **no `preToolMessage` on any tool**; the one-complete-sentence `complaintSummary` rule; the Result branches (SUCCESS / FAILURE, the invented-number rule, two outcomes only); `ONE ATTEMPT PER CALL`; T1–T4; every agent's business logic. `getConsumerDetailsLive`'s deliberate two-output fetch is untouched. |
| **Result** | Every file shrank: **−2,032 to −2,343 words** per complaint agent, **−185 to −409** on the other seven. `FAILED TURN` per complaint agent: 5–6 → **0–1**. Stop-before-tool phrasings: 1–8 per file → **0**. |
| **Correction to TOOL-02** | TOOL-02 concluded that the mechanism was the truncation window, and that `Default` works because its line is short. These calls show that was at most part of it: a one-sentence line with no interruption still failed every time while the prompt around it said "the sentence is the whole turn". TOOL-02's short sentence stands; its explanation was incomplete, and **its wording caused part of the failure it was written to fix.** |
| **⚠️ Open** | Unmeasured, as every entry before it has been: logging `finishReason` and the raw model response on tool turns is still the only way to know whether this worked. Barge-in-stops-generation (TOOL-02) remains a platform item. The Result field is still unnamed. **CC not ported** — it has none of TOOL-01/02/03. |

---

### TOOL-04 · A spoken line is never a tool use — the once-only rules were locking tools after a single miss — CRC only, CC pending (2026-09-11)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. Landed in every CRC agent that holds `calltransfer` or `bpcl_create_complaint`, plus `vaaniQA`. **CC not ported** — and CC carries the same read-back rule. |
| **Source** | A `genericInfoComplaintAgent` call, 11-09-2026 14:00, after TOOL-03 shipped. The transfer line spoken **ten times**, the customer answering *"Ok."*, *"इंतज़ार कर रहा हूं"*, *"Transfer कीजिए"* — and then, at 14:03:07, *"मैंने अभी प्रयास किया था, लेकिन हमारी सीनियर टीम अभी उपलब्ध नहीं है"* with **no transfer ever having run**. |
| **The bug** | `THE ATTEMPT BELONGS TO THE CALL`, in **11 agents**: *"BEFORE I EVER INVOKE calltransfer I READ BACK THROUGH THE WHOLE CALL for **a transfer line or a transfer Result**. If **either** is there, the tool is finished for this call and I handle the consumer with the truth instead — **I already tried, and our team could not be reached.**"* It counted **her own spoken line** as an attempt. One missed call — for any reason — put the line in the transcript, and from that turn on the rule **forbade ever calling the tool**, while the recovery rule said *"a line with no Result means it never ran — call it now."* The one-attempt rule is the more emphatic of the two and sits beside the transfer turn, so it won. |
| **Why it looked systematic** | It turned every single miss into a **permanent lockout**. That is why these calls show ten identical misses in a row rather than an intermittent one: after the first, she was *forbidden* to succeed. And the 14:03:07 message was not a hallucination — it is the prompt's own instruction, near-verbatim (`IF THEY ASK ME TO TRY AGAIN` even scripts *"मैंने अभी try किया था, हमारी team अभी available नहीं है"*), firing because the line counted as the try. The 10:28 call's *"अभी हमारी senior team busy है"* is the same instruction. |
| **The complaint guards had it too — and one was worse** | `CALL ONCE, NEVER RETRY`: *"if I have already called it for this same issue, I do not call it again… **regardless of whether the earlier call succeeded or failed**. In that case I say **'आपकी complaint register हो गई है…'**."* After a missed registration her own registering line read as "already called", she was forbidden to call — and **told to say the complaint is registered**. A scripted false confirmation, triggered by a miss, and a match for the invented *"register हो गई है"* in the 04-09 transcript that opened this whole workstream. Even without a miss it was wrong: after a real **failed** call it still scripted *"registered"*. `THE TOOL IS DOWN` (*"has failed even once"*) and `SAME-ISSUE` (*"ALREADY registered"*) were ambiguous in the same way. |
| **The fix — one definition, and every guard keyed to it** | Added once, in each agent's tool block: *"**A TOOL HAS BEEN USED ONLY WHEN ITS RESULT IS IN THE TRANSCRIPT.** This decides every once-only rule in this prompt… My own spoken line is never a use of a tool. If I said a tool's line and no Result came back, that tool has NOT been used: it is still available, and my TOOL RECOVERY check calls it now."* Then: the read-back looks for **a calltransfer Result** only, and says outright that her own transfer line with no Result is **not** an attempt; `ONE ATTEMPT` / `NO SECOND OFFER` / `OFFER DIES` key on **a Result**; `CALL ONCE` keys on **a Result for this issue**, says *"registered"* **only if that Result carried a number**, and routes a numberless Result to `THE TOOL IS DOWN`; `THE TOOL IS DOWN` means **a failure Result**, explicitly not a line; `SAME-ISSUE` means **a number came back**. |
| **A second complaint guard, `notEligibleDeliveryAgent` only** | Its data-dispute registration path carried its own copy: *"if I have already called it for this issue, I do not call it again… regardless of whether the earlier call succeeded or failed."* Same lockout, same fix — keyed to a Result for this issue, with the spoken-line clause. *"regardless of whether the earlier call succeeded or failed"* now survives nowhere in a live prompt. |
| **Caught in verification** | Four you-voice agents (`bookingEligibleAgent`, `bookingNonEligibilityAgent`, `connectionServicesAgent`, `subsidyAgent`) had *"the consumer asking **me** to try again"* in `THE TOOL IS DOWN` — pre-existing in the prior commit, fixed here since the line was being edited anyway. And: the first scripted pass left `CALL ONCE` reading *"…not a call — TOOL RECOVERY calls it now. **Otherwise I say 'register हो गई है'**"* in nine agents — reintroducing the false confirmation. Repaired before commit; the success tail now sits on the success branch only. |
| **`vaaniQA` aligned** | **C18** — an attempt is an invocation that returned a Result; a busy/unavailable/"already tried" message with **no calltransfer Result in the call** is a **fabricated outcome**, reported at highest severity, with the tell spelled out (the line repeating between silence prompts, then a busy message with nothing behind it). **C5i** — the spent offer keys on a Result. **C3c-LOOP** — *failed* means a numberless Result; invoking after a missed line is correct, not a loop. **C3d** — *registered* means a number came back; answering a missed line with "register हो गई है" is a false confirmation under C26. |
| **What this does and does not fix** | It removes the **lockout** and the **scripted false outcomes** — so a first miss becomes recoverable instead of fatal. It does **not** explain the first miss itself; TOOL-03 is the attempt at that, and it remains unmeasured. |
| **⚠️ Open** | **CC has the identical read-back sentence** and should be ported before its next transfer change (§6 rule 4). `finishReason` / raw-response logging is still the only way to measure the first-miss rate. |

---

### TOOL-05 · When she registers: she decides, or she asked and got a yes — nothing in between — CRC only, CC pending (2026-09-11)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. All **10** CRC complaint agents plus `vaaniQA`. **CC not ported.** |
| **Source** | Continuing complaint-tool misses after TOOL-03/04. In the 11-09 10:29 call Vaani said *"प्रदीप जी, आपकी बात सही है, मैं आपकी शिकायत रजिस्टर कर रही हूँ।"* and then **waited** while the consumer kept adding detail — the sentence repeated, no call. |
| **The cause — she was being told to wait for a "yes" that no longer had a question** | The complaint rules sat in three sections that disagreed. `CONFIRM ONCE`: *issue clear → no confirmation turn at all.* The per-path steps: *"I **move to confirmation** in Section 7/8"* (32 pointers across the ten agents — **21** in `postDeliveryAgent` alone) and *"Step 3: I **confirm** the one-line summary once, **then** I call the tool."* And the tool turn itself: *"**When the consumer confirms** they want to register a complaint, I…"* — but CPL-05 had removed the confirmation question months ago. Pointed at a confirmation step that no longer existed, the only sentence she had was the registering sentence, so she **used it as the question** and held the tool for a yes. |
| **Three more reasons to keep waiting** | **Collection mode** — *"I continue **until I have all the details**, then I move to confirmation."* **TOOL-02's `complaintSummary` wording** — *"…**Anything left out here is lost**."* (18 places) — written to help the team, it read to a small model as a reason to keep gathering. **`paymentAgent`** asked *"क्या यह सही है?"* on **every** complaint, contradicting its own `CONFIRM ONCE`, and said only *"On confirmation, I call the tool"*. With about ten gates before the call and several of them saying "wait", she never had a clear moment to act. `Default`'s switch, which never fails, has one trigger and no wait. |
| **Also a stale contradiction in all ten** | `CONFIRM ONCE` still said *"The registering announcement is part of that same sentence — **I name the problem** and say I am registering it"* — but since TOOL-02 the sentence is fixed and names nothing. Removed. |
| **The rule — client decision, 11-09-2026** | One block in each agent, just above `THE TOOL TURN`: **`WHEN I REGISTER — TWO WAYS, AND ONLY TWO`.** **I DECIDE** (the normal way): once the problem is clear — she can name what went wrong, it is LPG, she cannot solve it, it is hers — she registers **that same turn**: the sentence, the call, end generation; no question, no waiting for a yes. **I ASKED** (only where the prompt tells her to ask): she waits for the answer; on a **clear yes that turn is the registering turn**; on a no, nothing is registered; if unclear, she answers and does not register yet. **ONLY A GENUINE PROBLEM** — client requirement: never on a vague or random sentence; if she cannot yet name what went wrong she asks one open question, and that question carries no registering sentence. **The registering sentence is never a question.** |
| **Edits** | Every *"move (directly) to confirmation…"* → *"register per WHEN I REGISTER"*; `Step 3` → *"Once the problem is clear, I register per WHEN I REGISTER"*; *"When the consumer confirms they want to register…"* → *"Once the problem is clear — or, where a question was asked, on the consumer's clear yes —"*, and *"the consumer's yes is the only confirmation needed"* deleted; collection mode → *"only until I know what went wrong"*; the `complaintSummary` line → *"Build it from what the consumer has already said — never ask more questions just to fill it."* |
| **`paymentAgent` — recommendation adopted** | The always-ask becomes ask-only-when-something-new-needs-checking, matching `connectionServicesAgent` and `subsidyAgent`; after a clear yes, *that turn is the registering turn*. Its *"पहले वाली complaint register कर दूँ, या पहले यह नई बात solve करें?"* choice question stays — a real ask, correctly handled as I ASKED. |
| **Caught in verification — a second family of wait instructions** | The first pass keyed on the "move to confirmation" wording and missed nine lines in the *consumer-asks-for-a-person* paths that said *"(I) confirm the one-line summary once, then call bpcl_create_complaint"* — one of them, in `activeDeliveryAgent`, said *"…then call bpcl_create_complaint **on a later turn**"*, a direct instruction to split the sentence from the call. All now read *"register per WHEN I/YOU REGISTER"* (the `activeDeliveryAgent` one adds *"the sentence and the call on the same turn"*). Those same paths asked *"आपकी समस्या क्या है, **मैं दर्ज कर देती हूँ**?"* — a clarifying question carrying a registration promise, which the new rule and the readiness gate both forbid — now *"आपकी समस्या क्या है, थोड़ा बताइए?"*. Also removed: *"The consumer's yes to registering is the only confirmation needed"* (`eligibleDeliveryAgent`, `postDeliveryAgent`), and `paymentAgent`'s *"until I have all the details, then… confirmation and tool call in Block 8"*. |
| **Left alone, deliberately** | The genuineness gates — `RESOLUTION LADDER`, the readiness gate, the LPG-scope check, *read the record first*, and `A COMPLAINT COMES AFTER THE ANSWER` (a real answer turn, not a limbo). `connectionServicesAgent` / `subsidyAgent`'s *"क्या यह सही है?"* → *On "हाँ"* sites, already correct I-ASKED shape. Every `switchagent` *"If the consumer confirms…"* line — a different flow. |
| **`vaaniQA`** | **C3g** now names the two moments and reports **a clear yes with no call that turn** (same miss as C26) and **the registering sentence used as a question** as violations. |
| **⚠️ Open** | Unmeasured, like every entry before it. If a failing call shows a consumer's clear *"हाँ, दर्ज कर दीजिए"* and still no call on the next turn, there is a cause this entry does not reach. **CC not ported.** |

### TOOL-06 · Tools that act are not announced — the complaint is the call alone, transfer and hangup speak through `preToolMessage` — CRC only (excl. `Default`), CC pending (2026-09-14)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. **15 CRC prompts**: the ten complaint agents, `routingAgent`, `newConnectionAgent_onHold`, and the three `getConsumerDetails` files. `Default` excluded by client; `emergencyAgent` has no complaint, transfer or hangup turn. Plus `vaaniQA` and `postCallAnalysisFlat`. The parked `newConnectionAgent.txt` untouched. **CC not ported.** |
| **Source** | Call 14-09-2026 11:10, `genericInfoComplaintAgent`, underweight grievance. 11:12:07 the registering sentence, 13 s of silence, a presence check, then complaint number *8-9-7-6-5-4-3-2-1* — **confirmed by the client as invented by the model**. 11:14:28 an unprompted connect offer stitched from the no-window failure script and the T3 offer script, with no complaint failure. 11:14:34 the transfer sentence with no `calltransfer`; 11:14:45 *"इस समय हमारी senior team उपलब्ध नहीं है"* with no Result behind it. |
| **Diagnosis** | TOOL-01 to TOOL-05 all kept a fixed sentence as the agent's own text beside the call. The sentence became what the model produced and the call became optional; every miss had the same shape — the line, no call. Check 0 recovery was keyed on that line and never named the transfer. The rare-branch failure lines were printed verbatim and got reached for by mood. **The tool returns in under 400 ms (client), so a silent tool turn costs nothing.** |
| **Client decision, 14-09-2026** | (1) **`bpcl_create_complaint` is the call alone** — `complaintSummary` + `complaintReason`, no text, no `preToolMessage`; the agent decides what to say from the Result. The registering sentence appears **nowhere** in the prompts — not as a line, not inside a prohibition — and the prompts carry no guidance about sentences at all, only: call the tool, read the Result, speak from it. (2) **`calltransfer` and `callhangup` carry their line in `preToolMessage`, no text** — neither has a later turn to speak on, and a line inside the call cannot be spoken without the call. **This reverses TOOL-01 for these two tools**, back to XFER-03's shape. (3) `switchagent` unchanged. |
| **Edits** | Tool block rewritten as *HOW I/YOU USE MY/YOUR TOOLS*. Every registering-sentence reference removed: tool turn, `WHEN I REGISTER`, `CONFIRM ONCE`, `CALL ONCE`, `THE TOOL IS DOWN`, `complaintSummary`, Section 8 *TURN ONE* / *TENSE GATE* / *NOTHING ELSE*, data-dispute and *On हाँ* sites, the readiness gate, and the retry-wording paragraph. Transfer turn, T3 yes, hangup definitions and every closing sequence moved to `preToolMessage`. **Check 0 / TOOL RECOVERY is state-based** — *reached the point of using a tool, no Result* — covers all four tools, and adds **A TOOL WITH NO RESULT HAS NO OUTCOME**. Outcome 2 gated to a turn with a `calltransfer` Result; its verbatim Hindi anchors, the no-window line, the try-again line and the T3 offer script replaced by meaning; the T3 offer names its one trigger; **DESPAIR IS NOT A REQUEST** added. `activeDeliveryAgent`'s underweight block — where the `calltransfer — AUTHORIZED` paragraph had been spliced over the *Reason: Less weight* tail — restored, the paragraph moved back beside its siblings. |
| **Analysers** | `vaaniQA` C3g, C3g-TAIL, C25, C26 now expect **no** registering sentence and look for a due complaint with no outcome; C5d expects the transfer sentence once, from `preToolMessage`; C3, C3c, C3c-LOOP, C3d, C3i, C5i, C17, C18 reworded to match. `postCallAnalysisFlat`'s `complaintFailed` no longer needs a "registering" sentence. `callTransferred` still reads the spoken transfer line, which the platform now speaks from `preToolMessage`. |
| **⚠️ Open** | The platform must speak a `preToolMessage` in full before it transfers or disconnects. `routingAgent`'s switchagent failure-retry still says `preToolMessage` in one place and own text in another (pre-existing, untouched). **CC not ported.** |

### TOOL-07 · No `preToolMessage` anywhere in CRC — the transfer is the call alone, the hangup line is text, the fetch is the call alone — CRC only (excl. `Default`), CC pending (2026-09-14)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. Same 15 CRC prompts as TOOL-06, plus `vaaniQA`, `postCallAnalysisFlat`, both `CLAUDE.md` files. **Supersedes TOOL-06's `preToolMessage` shape for `calltransfer` and `callhangup`.** **CC not ported.** |
| **Source** | Two calls on 14-09-2026 after TOOL-06. 13:51 — the transfer line spoken with **no `calltransfer` in the raw model output** (client-confirmed), then *"संपर्क नहीं हो पा रहा"* and *"मैंने पहले ही प्रयास किया था"* with no Result; the closing line heard twice — the raw output held **`callhangup` with the line as text *and* as `preToolMessage`** (client-confirmed). 14:01 — every tool fired, but the fetch line *"जी, एक मिनट रुकिए…"* was heard twice (`getConsumerDetailsLive`), the transfer line twice (text, then `preToolMessage`), and the closing line twice again. The complaint, called with no text, was clean in both. |
| **Diagnosis** | While a sentence sits anywhere in the prompt, this model speaks it — instead of the call (transfer, 13:51) or beside the `preToolMessage` that already carries it (hangup, both calls). A call with no text never doubled. `getConsumerDetailsLive`'s STEP 5 asked for two outputs in a row — speech, then the fetch — which cannot happen without a consumer turn between them, so the model merged them and the line came back when the Result arrived. |
| **Client decision, 14-09-2026** | (1) **`calltransfer` works exactly like the complaint tool**: the prompt says only to call it and speak from its Result — nothing about text or `preToolMessage`, and the transfer sentence is deleted everywhere, including the rule that let the word *transfer* live inside it. (2) **`callhangup`**: the closing line as the agent's **own text**, the call on that same turn, **no `preToolMessage`** — the call ends, so the line cannot be repeated; a goodbye with no call is recovered by calling `callhangup` without saying it again. (3) **`bpcl_fetch_all_api`** — in `getConsumerDetailsLive`, `getConsumerDetails` and `MultiToolVersion` — **works like the complaint tool**: the call alone, the *"एक मिनट रुकिए"* line deleted, the Result decides the next step. (4) The platform's transfer-failure message and the delivery agent's permission question were left out of scope. |
| **Analysers** | `vaaniQA` C5d: no announcement is correct; an announcement of her own, or a busy/unavailable message with no platform failure message before it, is a violation. C18 and C26 key on the platform failure message instead of a transfer sentence. `postCallAnalysisFlat` `callTransferred`: *yes* when the consumer asked for a person and the call left the agent's hands with no failure message — no longer the spoken line. |
| **Docs** | CRC `CLAUDE.md` now records `getConsumerDetailsLive.txt` as the deployed entry prompt (it said `getConsumerDetails.txt`). |
| **⚠️ Open** | The consumer hears no transfer announcement, by design. **CC not ported.** |

### CPL-26 · `paymentAgent` still ran a "collect for complaint" loop, and it ended in a claimed registration with an invented number — CRC only, CC pending (2026-09-22)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. `paymentAgent` only. Extends CPL-22/CPL-23. **CC not ported.** |
| **Source** | Live call 21-09-2026 19:33, a village-pickup / no-home-delivery grievance with an advance recorded. 19:33:17 *"आपकी शिकायत के लिए मैं इसे अभी रजिस्टर कर रही हूँ। … यह घटना किस तारीख को हुई थी?"*; 19:34:22 *"आपकी शिकायत मैंने दर्ज कर ली है। … नंबर है एक शून्य शून्य एक चार, छह शून्य सात सात"*. **No `bpcl_create_complaint` call**, and the number was spoken in Hindi digit words. |
| **Diagnosis** | CPL-22/23 reached `paymentAgent`, but it still had the older **collection flow** that the other nine agents no longer have: **18** *"I collect for complaint"* triggers, three `WHAT I COLLECT FOR FLOW 3/4/5 COMPLAINTS` lists (date, mode, amount), and `BLOCK 5` telling it to ask the next detail. Flow 5 ended *"COMPLETION: complaint registered."* — a finished-registration state reached by collecting, not by calling. Flow 4 had *"a complaint will ensure the team follows up"*. `TOOL 1`'s `When:` had been **empty** since before CPL-22, so nothing on the tool definition said when to call it. The 19:33:17 turn is the Flow 5 list, asked aloud; 19:34:22 is its `COMPLETION`. |
| **The rule now** | `When:` filled in: the call is the only way a complaint gets registered; when a flow says a complaint is due, for a grievance that already happened, for a data dispute, or an escalation; no announcement; never ask for a date/amount/mode just to fill `complaintSummary`. Every *"collect for complaint"* now reads *"I register a complaint per COMPLAINT TOOL on a turn of its own, with no text"*. Where a first sentence states a verdict (overcharge), the call is on the **next** turn. The three `WHAT I COLLECT` lists are deleted (Flow 3 keeps one line: the summary carries what the consumer already said, and never bank/UPI/card/OTP). Flow 5 `COMPLETION` and Flow 4's follow-up promise are replaced by the standard *"register per COMPLAINT TOOL and follow only its message"*. `BLOCK 5` now allows only a question that decides whether a complaint is due, never one that fills the summary. |
| **⚠️ Open** | The call itself was a delivery grievance (GREY ZONE says route). The routing decision is untouched. **CC not ported.** |

### NUM-07 · `connectionServicesAgent` spoke the complaint number as one whole number — its own voice rule said "no separators" at normal speed — CRC only (2026-09-22)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — NUM-01 compliance. `connectionServicesAgent`; the long-number exception is also added to both booking agents. |
| **Source** | Live call: LLM output *"आपका शिकायत नंबर 12644509 है"* → TTS *"एक करोड़ छब्बीस लाख चवालीस हज़ार पाँच सौ नौ"*. The TTS expanded the raw number the model wrote. |
| **Diagnosis** | The model wrote raw numerals. Three rules in the file contradicted each other: `VOICE CALL DISCIPLINE` (from the original bulk commit) said `" - "` is allowed **only in slow/noting mode** and *"In normal-speed delivery, no separators at all"*; `§9 RULE 2` said every digit is a **Hindi** word, always in three pieces; `LONG NUMBER DELIVERY` (NUM-01/NUM-06) said English digit words with `" - "`, complaint number in one turn. The complaint number's one-turn default reads as "normal speed", so *no separators* + *one go* produced `12644509`. No other agent has the slow/normal-speed line. |
| **The rule now** | `VOICE CALL DISCIPLINE`: `" - "` goes between the digit words of **every** long number, complaint number included; never raw numerals. `§9 RULE 2` is now a pointer to `LONG NUMBER DELIVERY` (English digit words, contact numbers in pieces, complaint number in one turn). `§9`'s "one piece at a time" and the TTS-safe "every digit as a Hindi word" line point there too. The `§22 CRITICAL REMINDERS` line *"Phone digits as Hindi words in natural flow first time (no dashes — dashes break TTS)"* — missed in the first pass, found during CC-PORT-02 — now points there as well (2026-09-22). `bookingEligibleAgent` / `bookingNonEligibilityAgent`: *"Convert every digit, number, and date into Hindi words"* now excludes long numbers. |
| **⚠️ Open** | Unmeasured. |

### BNE-03 · `bookingNonEligibilityAgent` invented a last-booking date and a day-count when the handoff held a real booking date and a "planned for Delivery" status — CRC only, CC pending (2026-09-22)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. `bookingNonEligibilityAgent`. Extends BNE-01/BNE-02. **CC not ported.** |
| **Source** | Handoff: *"LPG refill was booked on 2026-09-20 21:02:00.000. Refill Order has been planned for Delivery. Customer is not eligible for booking."* Spoken: *"आपकी पिछली booking पंद्रह जुलाई दो हजार पच्चीस को हुई थी और अगली booking आप सात दिन बाद कर पाएंगे।"* Neither value exists; the booking was 20-09-2026. |
| **Diagnosis** | (1) *"planned for Delivery"* was not among the open-order shapes (only *"received Successfully…"* and *"delivered on…"*), so OPEN ORDER did not fire. (2) THREE SEPARATE FACTS said the booking timestamp sits **inside the VERDICT**; here it was its own sentence, so it was never read as the last booking date. (3) The gap-not-over template was picked with no gap in the VERDICT, and its two blanks were filled from the prompt's own tables: *"दो हजार पच्चीस"* (plain ज) is the conversion table's year example, and the output also used पंद्रह and सात from the table. `activeDeliveryAgent` has *HOW I USE THAT TABLE — IT IS A CONVERSION TABLE, NOT DATA* and *NO EXAMPLE DATE IS EVER REAL DATA*; this agent had neither. |
| **The rule now** | Both guards ported from `activeDeliveryAgent`. Open-order shapes matched by meaning, including *planned for Delivery*, order+payment received, and a failed attempt with another coming. A *"booked on …"* sentence is the last booking date wherever it sits. **THE LAST BOOKING DATE** is spoken in Hindi words on the first turn of OPEN ORDER / gap not over, and whenever it answers the consumer; time only as सुबह/दोपहर/शाम/रात; never asked. Gap-not-over needs a gap or day-count **written in the VERDICT**; a missing value drops its clause. The gap+K Y C example's real-looking values (*बारह जून … आठ दिन*) are now placeholders. |
| **⚠️ Open** | **CC not ported.** |

### CPL-23 · The English instructions were still scripting the complaint turn, and a general new-connection question was answered from the zip walkthrough — CRC only, CC pending (2026-09-18)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. The ten complaint-capable CRC agents. Extends CPL-22. **CC not ported.** |
| **Source** | `activeDeliveryAgent`, 18-09-2026 11:30, after CPL-22 was live. (1) *"…चूँकि मेरे पास अभी delivery की कोई निश्चित तारीख उपलब्ध नहीं है, इसलिए मैं आपकी शिकायत दर्ज कर देती हूँ ताकि हमारी टीम इस पर तुरंत कार्रवाई कर सके और आपसे संपर्क करे।"*, then *"आपकी शिकायत दर्ज कर दी गई है और हमारी टीम जल्द ही आपसे संपर्क करेगी।"*, with no tool call and no number. (2) *"gas connection मिल रहा है?"*: Vaani confirmed the topic and spoke the new-connection switch line, then **answered it herself in the same turn** from the zip App walkthrough ("Bharatgas for Home → Explore Bharatgas Products → register/login"), then offered a distributor visit. |
| **Diagnosis** | (1) CPL-22 removed the Hindi scripts, but **the English instructions were the same script**. Line 386 read *"acknowledge it, say the exact delivery date is not available with me right now, and register the complaint … so our team can follow it up and make contact"*. The 11:30:09 turn is that line translated clause by clause. Across the ten agents there were **~110** such clauses: *"register a complaint and tell them our team will make contact"* (36), *"so our team can …"* (40), *"I offer to register"* (24), and about 20 *"say X / reassure them, then register"* pairings. Each put speech, and a promise, on the same turn as the complaint. (2) The zip walkthrough says *"I WALK THEM THROUGH IT MYSELF AND DO NOT ROUTE IT"*, and its step 7 says *"New Connection चुनिए"*. A general new-connection question matched it, and that overrode the domain router. The switchagent rule never said the switch line is the whole turn. |
| **The rule now** | (1) Every *"tell them our team will make contact"* and *"so our team can …"* clause is deleted. Every *"offer to register"* is now *register per COMPLAINT TOOL*, and it is conditional ("if they want it looked into") wherever a spoken answer came first. Every *"say / reassure … then register"* is reversed: **the call goes alone, and the reassurance or explanation comes on the next turn, with the tool's message**. The COMPLAINT TOOL block gains **THE REGISTERING TURN IS THE CALL AND NOTHING ELSE**: no acknowledgement, apology, reason, "इसलिए", team or contact on that turn. (2) The zip walkthrough opens with a gate: it is only for a consumer who named zip themselves. A general new-connection, Ujjwala or P M U Y question is another domain and goes to routingAgent. (3) The switchagent line is **the whole of the text on that turn**; answering after it means no switch happened. |
| **⚠️ Open** | **CC not ported.** |
