### CPL-22 · The complaint flow is rebuilt: call the tool, then do what its message says — every scripted complaint line deleted — CRC only, CC pending (2026-09-17)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. All ten complaint-capable CRC agents, plus `vaaniQA`. **Supersedes CPL-21** (removed the same day) and replaces the complaint-outcome parts of CPL-03, CPL-08 and TOOL-06. **CC not ported.** |
| **Source** | CPL-21's two calls (16-09-2026, 17:48 and 18:11): complaints claimed with no tool call, one with an invented number. Client direction on 2026-09-17: the fix belongs in the prompt, not in a platform guard. |
| **Diagnosis** | The prompts taught agents to **talk about** complaints and barely taught the **call**. Each agent had 9,000–14,700 words in complaint paragraphs, about 3,000 of them on failure alone. It had 10–14 ready-to-say "registered" sentences. The instruction to call the tool appeared 3–7 times. "When to register" was split across many sections, some saying to ask first. Every fix since TOOL-03 added more bans on top. With a ready-made "आपकी complaint register हो गई है" in front of it, the model said it instead of calling. |
| **The rule now** | **The platform's tool returns only a message** (no `caseId`/`caseNumber`) that says what happened and what to do. For example, *"Complaint registration successful. Share Complaint Number: … with the consumer and mention that an SMS confirmation will be sent."*, or on failure, *"…could not be registered and offer to transfer the call."* One identical block, `COMPLAINT TOOL — THE WHOLE PROCEDURE`, replaces the COMPLAINT PROTOCOL — STANDARD block. It says: (1) **call** the tool with nothing said before or with it; (2) **read the message**, the only source of truth; (3) **do what it says** in natural Hindi, never reading its English aloud, and speak a number only if it is in the message, digit by digit; (4) **no message, no complaint**: call the tool. Then: once per issue, no call after a failure, at most two calls, one open question if the problem isn't clear yet. `complaintSummary` and `complaintReason` definitions and the reason lists are unchanged. **Deleted everywhere:** the success line, the already-registered line, the failure line, the pressing-again ladder and the PARAPHRASE carve-out, the success/failure/duplicate paragraphs, ANTI-FABRICATION, HARD STOP CHECK, and the CPL-21 guard. Also deleted: the two-turn sections in the delivery and generic agents, Step-4 "speak the number" lines, knowledge-base registration scripts, the "ask whether to register" lines in `eligibleDeliveryAgent` and `paymentAgent`, and the Hindi narration examples in the TOOL-08 ban (all 16 files). T3 now reads: offer the transfer because the failure message says so, ask once, call on yes, don't re-offer on no. `vaaniQA` C3c, C3d and C22 no longer demand fixed sentences. |
| **⚠️ Open** | **Platform dependency:** the tool must return that guidance message on both success and failure. The failure text is not final yet. The block still works if it changes, but a message with no clear success or failure wording would leave the agent guessing. **CC not ported.** Replay the 17:48 and 18:11 calls before shipping. |

---

### CPL-21 · ~~SUPERSEDED same day by CPL-22~~ · Vaani claimed complaints she never registered — her own spoken line was being read as proof — CRC only, CC pending (2026-09-17)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. The ten complaint-capable CRC agents. **CC not ported.** |
| **Source** | Two CRC calls on 16-09-2026, after TOOL-08 was live. **18:11** (`bookingEligibleAgent`): *"यह तकनीकी समस्या लग रही है, इसलिए मैं आपकी शिकायत दर्ज कर देती हूँ … क्या मैं आपकी शिकायत में यह लिख दूँ …?"*. The consumer disputed it instead of saying yes. Vaani then said *"आपकी शिकायत दर्ज हो चुकी है कि …"* with no tool call, and repeated *"पहले ही दर्ज हो चुकी है"* three more times, the last adding *"आपको और कोई शिकायत दर्ज करने की आवश्यकता नहीं है"*. **17:48**: *"मैं इसके लिए एक शिकायत दर्ज कर देती हूँ"*, no call, then on the next turn an invented seven-digit complaint number in Hindi digit words (the protocol's default is English digit words). |
| **Diagnosis** | (1) **The prompts still scripted the announcement.** `bookingEligibleAgent` Step 5 said *"मैं आपकी complaint register कर देती हूँ … Confirm the one-line summary once, then call"*. That is the 18:11 turn almost word for word, and it contradicts TOOL-06 (no text on the complaint call) and CPL-05 (no confirmation turn). Five more places had the same promise line: `bookingNonEligibilityAgent` Path B, `connectionServicesAgent` C12 Step 4, `eligibleDeliveryAgent`'s five-methods offer and two knowledge-base answers in `genericInfoComplaintAgent` (mechanic visit, unrequested booking / B A S). (2) **Ready-made "already registered" sentences with no number.** The DUPLICATE GUARD's FIRST TIME example (*"आपकी शिकायत दर्ज हो चुकी है कि …"*) and the close-check line (*"आपकी complaint register हो गई है, हमारी team आपको call करेगी"*) claim a registration without needing a number. They were gated only by a clause many sentences earlier, so they were the easiest thing to say. 18:11 used the example's exact shape. (3) **Self-reinforcement.** Once the claim was spoken, every later turn read it as history, and the pressing-again ladder turned it into three further reassurances. TOOL RECOVERY looks for a tool she had *reached the point of using*. It did not recognise a turn where she had already announced the outcome. |
| **The rule now** | (1) All six announcement scripts are rewritten: register by calling the tool with no text, and say nothing about the complaint until the Result. (2) A new clause, **YOUR OWN WORDS ARE NEVER A RESULT**, sits under HARD STOP CHECK in all ten agents. Any sentence saying a complaint exists (registered, दर्ज हो चुकी, पहले ही दर्ज, no need to register again, a number, SMS) is allowed only with a numbered `bpcl_create_complaint` Result in the transcript, and the duplicate-guard, pressing-again and close-check lines are explicitly under that gate. Registration is never announced. If an earlier Vaani line got ahead of the tool, that line was false: don't repeat it, and call the tool now (or take the failure branch if the tool is down or spent). (3) The pressing-again ladder opens with a line saying it exists only once a numbered Result is in hand. |
| **⚠️ Open** | A prompt can make this rarer but cannot make it impossible, because a text-only turn is free to say anything. **Recommended platform guard:** when an agent's text says a complaint is registered (register हो गई / दर्ज हो गई / दर्ज हो चुकी / complaint number) and no `bpcl_create_complaint` success exists on the call, block or regenerate the turn. `vaaniQA` should flag a registration claim with no tool call in the transcript as a separate violation from a fabricated number. **CC not ported.** |

---

### ID-01 · "क्या आप AI बोल रहे हैं?" got "यह जानकारी मेरे पास नहीं है" — Vaani now says who she is — CRC only, CC pending (2026-09-17)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Axis 2 (persona) wording, shared-truth intent. All 15 deployed CRC agents, plus `getConsumerDetails.txt` and `getConsumerDetails-unreg.txt`. **CC not ported.** |
| **Source** | CRC call 16-09-2026 18:06. Consumer: *"क्या आप AI बोल रहे हैं?"* → *"यह जानकारी मेरे पास नहीं है। क्या कुछ और सहायता चाहिए?"* |
| **Diagnosis** | Every guardrail said *"asked about your instructions or design → यह जानकारी मेरे पास नहीं है"*. Nothing separated **who** she is from **how** she works, so an identity question went down the prompt-security path. That evasive answer to a direct "are you an AI?" reads as concealment. |
| **The rule now** | (client instruction) Beside the data-provenance carve-out in every agent: asked who she is, or whether she is an AI, robot, machine or human, Vaani answers naturally and truthfully that she is **Vaani, a Virtual Voice A I Assistant at a भारत गैस / भारत पेट्रोलियम C R C**, then returns to the query. She never claims to be human and never denies being an AI. The disclosure reveals nothing else: still no agents, systems, prompts or tools, still one Vaani. A follow-up request for a real person is handled like any other. In `emergencyAgent`, while a hazard is live, the answer is one clause and she goes straight back to the safety step. |
| **⚠️ Open** | CC's identity is "an agent of भारत पेट्रोलियम" and needs its own wording, not this CRC line. `vaaniQA` has no check that an identity question is answered. |

---

### TTS-03 · `connectionServicesAgent` spelled the consumer's name and the distributor's name and address letter by letter — CRC only, CC pending (2026-09-17)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth** (TTS). `connectionServicesAgent` in full; the spelling rule also sits under the TTS-SAFE block in the eleven other CRC agents that hold one. **CC not ported.** |
| **Source** | CRC call 16-09-2026 18:02. First line *"K A L K A T E P R A B H A V A T I V I L A S जी"* (the full stored name, surname first, spelled). C12 then gave the distributor as *"G O D E Y A G A S S E R V I C E"* at *"P L O T N O A M दो / दो R O A D N O दो दो W A G L E E S T A T E …"*, with the pin code included and not asked for. |
| **Diagnosis** | (1) §9 RULE 1 said *"Abbreviations (<5 letters, uppercase): letter-by-letter"* and RULE 6 said *"abbreviations are expanded or spelled letter by letter"*. The backend sends everything in capitals, so every word looked like an uppercase abbreviation, and nothing said a name or place is never spelled. (2) The prompt contradicted itself on the name. Lines 14 and 170 said the name was already used and must not be spoken, while §7 and NAME HANDLING said this agent speaks it first. *"First name only"* also had no answer for a surname-first, three-word stored name. |
| **The rule now** | **NO NAME AND NO ADDRESS IS EVER SPELLED OUT.** Capitals never make a word an abbreviation. Letter-by-letter is only for the named abbreviations and for a two-or-three-letter non-word code inside an address. Names, agencies, roads, estates, areas and cities become Devanagari words (NO → नंबर, RD → रोड, 2/2 → दो बटा दो). A self-check catches any run of spaced Latin capitals, and a part that can't be spoken as words is left out. RULE 1 and RULE 6 are narrowed to match. The two stale "name already used" lines now agree with NAME HANDLING: one word, the given name, in Devanagari, on the first response only, and no name at all if the given name is unclear. |
| **⚠️ Open** | The same call read the distributor's number in English digit words all at once, while §9 RULE 2 asks for Hindi words in three pieces (see NUM-06); not changed here. **CC not ported.** |

---

### TOOL-08 · Tool names are never spoken, and a `callhangup` Result gets no text — CRC only, CC pending (2026-09-15)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. All 15 deployed CRC agents, plus `getConsumerDetails.txt` (kept in step with `getConsumerDetailsLive`). Extends TOOL-07; does not change its tool shapes. **CC not ported.** |
| **Source** | Two CRC calls on 14-09-2026, **after TOOL-07 was live**. 17:47 and 18:26 — Vaani said *"आपकी शिकायत दर्ज करने के लिए मैं अभी bpcl_create_complaint कॉल कर रही हूँ"*, both times on the complaint turn (at 17:47 once with no call behind it, then again with the call, which returned a real number). Both calls then ended with the closing line heard **twice** in one agent entry (17:48, 18:27), although the prompt had it as text + `callhangup` with no `preToolMessage`. |
| **Diagnosis** | (1) The only guard was a ban — *"never write a tool name as spoken text"* — while the prompts say *"to register a complaint I call bpcl_create_complaint"* many times. With no sentence of her own on the tool turn, she spoke that instruction in Hindi. The ban gave her nothing to say instead. (2) The transcript merges text before a call and text after its Result into one entry (the 18:26 complaint entry shows it). The doubled goodbye has the same shape: line, call, Result, line again. Nothing in the prompts said what to do when a `callhangup` Result comes back, and `getConsumerDetailsLive` said outright that nothing could come after it. |
| **The rule now** | (1) Beside every existing ban: tool names and the words tool/API/function are internal labels. A complaint is spoken of only as **"complaint"** or **"शिकायत"**. The act of using a tool is never narrated, whether before, beside or after the call. Only the outcome is spoken, and only from the Result. (2) In every agent holding `callhangup`: if its Result comes back, the goodbye has already been heard, so **no text and no tool**. The closing line itself is unchanged: text plus `callhangup` on the same turn. |
| **⚠️ Open** | (2) assumes the platform hands a turn back after `callhangup`. The transcript shape supports that but does not prove it. If the goodbye still doubles, the next suspect is the platform speaking the text again on hangup. `vaaniQA` has no check yet for a spoken tool name or a doubled closing line. **CC not ported.** |

### BNE-02 · `bookingNonEligibilityAgent` told a consumer with an open order that her connection was inactive — CRC only, CC pending (2026-09-14)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — a verdict-handling defect. Extends BNE-01. |
| **Source** | Live CRC call, 14-09-2026 12:36 (before TOOL-06/07). `handoffSummary` carried a booking on 12-09-2026, *"Customer is not eligible for booking"* with **no blocker named**, and *"Refill Order received Successfully, refill will be delivered to you shortly."* Vaani said *"आपका connection अभी inactive दिख रहा है"*, announced a complaint it never made (impossible anyway for an inactive connection, ESC-04), offered an unrequested transfer, and repeated "inactive" on a later turn. |
| **Diagnosis** | The prompt's world had six blockers and no "order already open" reason — the most common real one. Step 2 required the first turn to explain a blocker and offered only those six templates; the blocker gate said not to name an unwritten blocker but gave nothing to say instead; the refill-status sentence was defined as "not an eligibility fact"; and the missing-reason fallback still pointed to an office (ESC-01 leftover). With no permitted output, the model chose a template. |
| **The rule now** | Opening picked in order: a named blocker → its template; no blocker + an order on its way → **OPEN ORDER** (booking done, refill coming shortly, so no new booking — no complaint, no timeframe, no next-booking date); neither → **NO REASON STATED** (booking not allowed, reason not visible, no blocker named). A blocker is spoken only when the verdict literally states it. The office line in the missing-data rule is gone. |
| **Files touched (2)** | CRC `bookingAgent/bookingNonEligibilityAgent.txt`, plus this ledger. CC's copy has the same six-blocker model and is unfixed. |

### DEL-03 · `postDeliveryAgent` spent turns pretending to check, then called a record it held unavailable — CRC only, CC pending (2026-09-14)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — handoff-data discipline. |
| **Source** | Live CRC call, 14-09-2026 10:48 (before TOOL-06/07). `handoffSummary` intent *"delivery not received"*, record *"delivered on 01-07-2026"*. Vaani spoke two checking turns (*"डिलीवरी डिटेल्स देखती हूँ"*, *"स्टेटस चेक कर रही हूँ, एक मिनट"*), then said the delivery record did not show when it was delivered, then registered. Registering was correct (PATH 1); the checking and the "unavailable" claim were not. |
| **Diagnosis** | The prompt said once, near the top, not to imply checking — but nothing held that on the turn, and PATH 1 never said to register on the first turn when the intent already carried the grievance, so the model filled the gap with checking theatre and an invented "not available". |
| **The rule now** | **No checking turns ever** outside a `switchagent` line — every turn states the record, asks the one needed question, or calls the tool. A detail `handoffSummary` states is never called unavailable, and CASE B excludes it. When the intent already carries a directly-registrable grievance, the first turn is the `bpcl_create_complaint` call itself. The date-order question (a delivery dated before the booking) was deliberately left out of scope by the client. |
| **Files touched (2)** | CRC `deliveryAgent/postDeliveryAgent.txt`, plus this ledger. The other three CRC delivery agents and CC were not touched, by client scope. |

### D-01 · `calltransfer` tool exists only in CC — **RETIRED: CRC has it too, in every routing-capable agent (XFER-01, then XFER-03)**

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Escalation |
| **CC** | `calltransfer` is available. 238 references across the workspace. Target `sip:1001@21112728532045680.zt.plivo.com`, `contactNumber` = `{{jNationalNumber}}`. |
| **CRC** | The tool **does not exist**. 0 references. |
| **Why** | CRC is a physical office; the consumer can walk in. There is no senior desk to transfer a call to. |
| **Verified** | Tool inventory across all 30 prompt files. |

**Guard:** the literal string `calltransfer` must never appear in `bpcl_showroom_crc/`.

### D-02 · Terminal escalation: transfer vs office visit — **SUPERSEDED by ESC-01**

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Escalation |
| **CC** | `Default` STAGE 0 interrupts B (human request), C (language request), D (non-LPG product) fire `calltransfer` via the SILENT TRANSFER PROTOCOL. Last resort only — never a proactive offer, never triggered by dissatisfaction or a long call. If `calltransfer` fails the call ends; no callback is offered. (`Default.txt:65`, `:103`, `:208-212`) |
| **CRC** | **Superseded by ESC-01 (2026-07-29).** The office-visit escalation is retired: interrupts B and E now escalate to a registered complaint, and C and D get one separate inline reply each before escalating on insistence. |
| **Why** | Direct consequence of D-01. |

**Guard:** in CRC, no prompt may promise a person, a senior team, or a callback as an *outcome*.
Recognising the consumer's *request* for one is required and legitimate — see §3, DEF-02.

### ESC-01 · CRC terminal escalation is a registered complaint, not an office visit (2026-07-29)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Escalation |
| **CC** | Unchanged. `calltransfer` to the senior team, with the callback flow as the fallback when the transfer fails. |
| **CRC** | The office-visit escalation is **retired**. When the consumer asks for a human, a senior, or a callback, or a flow cannot be resolved, Vaani registers a complaint via `bpcl_create_complaint`, speaks the complaint number digit by digit, says it will also arrive by SMS, and tells them **"हमारी team आपसे संपर्क करेगी"** — with **no timeframe and no time capture**. "आप हमारे office आ सकते हैं" is no longer a valid ending to a problem. |
| **Why** | The consumer has usually already tried their distributor before calling a CRC — that is why they escalated. And one CRC serves a whole multi-district territory, so a visit can mean a 100+ km trip. Telling them to travel is not an outcome; a registered complaint is. |
| **Carve-outs (office guidance still correct)** | Buying a hotplate/stove and submitting KYC documents — a complaint cannot sell a hotplate or accept a document. Addresses and phone numbers remain knowledge Vaani holds and speaks **only when the consumer asks** ("आप कहाँ से बोल रही हैं?", "मेरे distributor का पता क्या है?"). A truly out-of-scope query still gets `routingAgent`'s hangup ladder, not a complaint. `emergencyAgent` is fully carved out while a hazard is live. |
| **Non-Hindi / non-LPG** | Answered once inline, with the two lines kept **separate** — never merged: `"मैं सिर्फ़ Hindi में मदद कर सकती हूँ।"` and `"मैं सिर्फ़ LPG से जुड़े सवालों में मदद कर सकती हूँ।"` Escalates to a complaint only if the consumer repeats the same request on the very next turn. |
| **Tool failure** | `bpcl_create_complaint` failing cannot fall back to "register a complaint". The one correct line is `"माफ़ कीजिए! अभी complaint register करने में तकनीकी समस्या आ रही है। कृपया थोड़ी देर बाद call कीजिए।"` Never a retry, never an invented number. |
| **Routing** | Ten agents hold `bpcl_create_complaint` and register in place. Two hold no complaint tool and never will: `Default` escalates via STAGE 3 to `genericInfoComplaintAgent` (same shape as INTERRUPT A → `emergencyAgent`); `newConnectionAgent` escalates `→ routingAgent → genericInfoComplaintAgent`, since leaf-to-leaf switching is forbidden. (`bookingEligibleAgent` was a third until 2026-07-29 — see CPL-02.) `getConsumerDetails` cannot escalate at all — no record exists to attach a complaint to, so its no-data path stays outside this protocol (as of 2026-07-30 it ends by giving the CRC office number instead — GCD-03 — which is still not a complaint and still not a `switchagent`). |
| **Verified** | All 16 CRC prompts + `postCallAnalysis.txt` + `vaaniQA.txt` swept for office-as-resolution language. |

### CPL-01 · The complaint block is one canonical protocol in all 18 complaint-capable agents (2026-07-29)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — this is **shared truth**. All ten agents per channel that hold `bpcl_create_complaint` carry a block headed `COMPLAINT PROTOCOL — STANDARD`, byte-identical within its channel. |
| **What it fixes** | The nine agents per channel had nine different complaint blocks: some had a duplicate guard and some didn't, four had **no Result-reading block at all**, caps ranged from "at most one time in this call" to "no more than two", and the failure path was written six different ways. |
| **Clauses (identical everywhere)** | CONFIRM FIRST · CALL ONCE, NEVER RETRY (max two per call) · SAME-ISSUE / DUPLICATE GUARD · PARAMETERS (exactly `complaintSummary` + `complaintReason`, renamed from `feedbackDescription`/`reason` 2026-09-02; the set is closed **by rule** — any other key is the platform's, named in the prompt or not, PARAM-01) · THE TOOL TURN (wait-line as text, no `preToolMessage`, no number spoken on the tool turn) · WHEN THE TOOL RESPONDS (success/failure) · ANTI-FABRICATION + numeral SELF-CHECK. |
| **Two legitimate variants per channel** | First person ("I") in the six agents written in Vaani's voice; second person ("You") in `bookingNonEligibilityAgent`, `connectionServicesAgent`, `subsidyAgent`, which are written as instructions. Same clauses, same order, same meaning. |
| **What stays agent-specific** | The trigger sentence (when this agent registers at all) and the domain reason list. Those were left untouched. |
| **CC vs CRC — the only two differences, both from ESC-01** | **Success:** CRC appends "हमारी team आपसे संपर्क करेगी"; CC does not, and keeps its callback exception (a callback complaint speaks no number and confirms date and time instead). **Failure:** CRC says "माफ़ कीजिए! अभी complaint register करने में तकनीकी समस्या आ रही है। कृपया थोड़ी देर बाद call कीजिए।" and stops; CC apologises, offers the senior team via `calltransfer` — never a callback, since one cannot be recorded while the tool is failing — and falls back to "अभी हमारी team busy है…". **Params:** CC's `feedbackDescription` has the extra callback-sentence exception. |
| **Verified** | 4 hashes across 18 files — exactly one per channel/person combination. Zero cross-channel leakage: no CRC prompt contains CC's failure line, no CC prompt contains CRC's, and `calltransfer` is present in all 9 CC agents and 0 CRC ones. |

### CPL-02 · `bookingEligibleAgent` registers its own complaints (2026-07-29)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**, implemented identically in both channels. |
| **Before** | `bookingEligibleAgent` held no complaint tool in either channel. A repeated booking failure ended in "here's the distributor's number", and in CRC a complaint could only be reached through `bookingEligibleAgent → routingAgent → genericInfoComplaintAgent` — two silent hops to record a fault the agent had already diagnosed itself. |
| **Now** | It holds `bpcl_create_complaint` and registers **directly**. It never routes anything in order to get a complaint registered; `switchagent` is only ever for a query belonging to another domain. It carries the same `COMPLAINT PROTOCOL — STANDARD` block as every other complaint-capable agent (CPL-01), so both channels now have **ten**. |
| **Complaint-first on a technical failure** | Two or more booking methods failed = a technical fault on our side, not a consumer mistake. Registering the complaint is the **first action**, not a fallback. The distributor's phone number may be offered **only afterwards**, and only if the consumer still wants to book today — a secondary convenience, never a substitute, never offered before the complaint. |
| **Scope** | It registers only within its own scope. **CC:** the technical booking failure, plus recording a callback after a failed `calltransfer` — the same fallback every other CC agent already had, which it could not perform before because it held no tool. **CRC:** the technical booking failure, plus a human/callback request per ESC-01. Anything belonging to another domain is still a `switchagent` route and never a complaint. |
| **CC callback rules, matching the other nine** | Never offered up front and never while a transfer is still possible — only after `calltransfer` fails **and** no complaint has been registered yet in this call. Slot must be 9 AM–5 PM within 15 days; asked twice at most, and if still invalid the complaint is registered anyway without the callback sentence. The callback complaint appends `"Consumer requested a callback on [YYYY-MM-DD] at [HH:MM]."` and **speaks no complaint number** — it confirms the date and time instead. If a complaint already exists (including the technical-failure one), no callback is offered at all. |
| **CC vs CRC** | CC keeps `calltransfer` as its human-request path — a request for a person is a transfer there, not a complaint. CRC has no transfer, so a human or callback request is registered as a complaint per ESC-01. The technical-failure trigger is identical in both. |

### ESC-02 · Complaint number is spoken in CRC; CC's callback complaint stays silent (2026-07-29)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Escalation |
| **CC** | On a **callback** complaint, CC deliberately speaks **no** complaint number — it confirms only the callback date and time (`eligibleDeliveryAgent.txt:87`). Ordinary CC complaints do speak the number. |
| **CRC** | **Every** complaint speaks the number digit by digit plus the SMS line, including escalation complaints. CRC has no callback complaint, so there is no silent case. ⚠️ **Digit language superseded by NUM-01 (2026-08-03):** the Hindi digit words this row originally specified are now **English digit words with `" - "`** in CRC. CC keeps Hindi words. |
| **Why** | On a CRC call the registered complaint *is* the whole outcome — the consumer has nothing else to hold on to, so the number must be given on the line. CC's callback complaint gives them a time instead. |
| **Also fixed in CRC (2026-07-29)** | Three agents were off-standard and are now aligned: `subsidyAgent` and `connectionServicesAgent` previously said only "complaint number आपके registered mobile पर SMS से भेज दिया जाएगा" and never spoke it (`subsidyAgent` follows CC's subsidyAgent, which already speaks it digit by digit); `activeDeliveryAgent` had the tool but **no success or failure handling at all** — a straight bug, now given the full Result-reading block. |
| **Reconciled in CC too (2026-07-29)** | CC's `connectionServicesAgent` was SMS-only and CC's `activeDeliveryAgent` had **no Result-reading block at all** — both now read the Result and speak the number digit by digit, preserving CC's callback exception (no number on a callback complaint). This is shared truth, so it landed in both channels the same session. |
| **Known residual CC gap** | CC's `paymentAgent` and `bookingNonEligibilityAgent` still have no Result-reading block. Out of scope this session; flagged for a follow-up pass. |


### GCD-02 · `getConsumerDetails` carries a MID-CALL ENTRY block in both channels (2026-07-29)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — this is **shared truth**, byte-identical in both channels, landed in the same session. |
| **Why** | `getConsumerDetails` is written for entry position ("activated when a consumer is not calling from their registered number") but the platform can activate it **mid-call**, after `Default` has already greeted and triaged. Its only defence was a single clause in the role paragraph — *"You do not greet or introduce yourself"* — and a live CRC call broke it: it opened with "नमस्ते।", added the banned empathy phrase "मैं समझ सकती हूँ", and restated intent the consumer had already given, immediately after `Default`'s "एक मिनट, मैं आपकी delivery details देखती हूँ।" The consumer heard Vaani hand off to herself. |
| **What** | A `MID-CALL ENTRY — YOU ARE NOT THE START OF THIS CALL` section inserted after IDENTITY AND ROLE in both files. Ports the two patterns that already work elsewhere: the leaf agents' continuity block (`postDeliveryAgent.txt:5` — "I am already in the middle of a live phone call … I do not say नमस्ते") and `connectionServicesAgent.txt:11`'s explicit WRONG-first-lines list, including the re-asking-established-intent case. Bans greeting, self-introduction, intent restatement, empathy phrases, and opening filler (including "hmm"/"umm"), and names `Default`'s own switch line as a WRONG first line so the seam cannot be repeated back. |
| **Not a fix for the root cause** | The real defect is gate ordering — the data branch resolves *after* `Default` triages, so `getConsumerDetails` runs third instead of first (CRC `agent-workflow.md` §11.3, and DATA-01's note that `Default` runs before the gate). This block contains the symptom; it does not stop `Default` from promising a lookup that is about to become a number request. Platform fix still open. |
| **Verified** | Both blocks hash identically. No channel-specific language in the block — it names no distributor, no office, no team, and no transfer, so it is valid under both Axis 1 and Axis 2 unchanged. |

### GCD-03 · CRC's no-data closing gives the CRC office number; CC offers the senior team (2026-07-30)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Escalation |
| **CC** | Unchanged. On the NO-DATA PATH, `getConsumerDetails` **offers the senior team and fires `calltransfer`** on a real consumer confirmation; only if the consumer declines does it fall through to the NO-DATA CLOSING's distributor-referral line (`getConsumerDetails.txt:60`, `:89`, `:117-127`). |
| **CRC** | `calltransfer` does not exist, so the equivalent outcome is a **phone number**. The NO-DATA CLOSING now says, in one turn: you don't have their information and cannot help, a senior team member will help them, please note the contact number — then speaks **`{{crcOfficeNumber}}` digit by digit**. The old "कृपया अपने Bharat Gas distributor से संपर्क कीजिए" line is **replaced**, not appended, and survives only as the uninjected-variable fallback. `callhangup` and its closing line are unchanged. |
| **Why** | This is the one CRC agent that cannot escalate at all — no complaint tool, no `switchagent`, and with no record there is nothing to attach a complaint to (ESC-01). Before this change the consumer was sent to "their distributor" without a name, number, or address, because with no record Vaani holds none of them. The CRC's own office number is static per-office config, not consumer data, so it is available on exactly the path where everything else is missing. |
| **Scope** | All three entries to the closing get it: fetch returned no data (STEP 5), consumer refused twice, consumer has no registered number (STEP 2). Not a complaint and not an ESC-01 escalation — `getConsumerDetails` still holds no `bpcl_create_complaint` and no `switchagent`. |
| **Digit style** | English digit words with " - " between each, the file's own default and the same style as its STEP 3 mobile-number readback — **not** the Hindi digit words CRC uses for complaint numbers (ESC-02). Hindi words only if the consumer speaks or asks for Hindi digits ('6' → "छह"). |
| **Dictation loop (2026-07-30)** | The number is **dictated, not announced**. Three pieces — 3, 3, 4 digits — one piece per turn, each followed by a single check question ("क्या आपने note कर लिया?" / "जी, note हो गया?" / "आगे बताऊँ?") and a stop. Missed piece → that piece alone is repeated, never a restart from digit one; "कितना note हुआ?" → only the digits given so far; "धीरे" → two digits per turn; a wrong read-back → only the wrong digits corrected. Then the full number is read back **once** for a final confirmation. This is the STEP 2 partial-number behaviour with the roles reversed: there Vaani is noting, here the consumer is. |
| **`callhangup` is gated on the number being noted** | Vaani does not close mid-number. `callhangup` fires only after the consumer confirms the complete number, or on one of exactly three exits: they already have it / don't want it, they cannot write it down at all, or the line is dead across three nudges. Turn count is never a reason to close. The uninjected-variable fallback has no number to dictate and closes immediately. |
| **Uninjected fallback** | Visible curly braces, empty, or a placeholder = no number. Never spoken, never invented — the old distributor-referral line is spoken instead and the call closes as before. |
| **New variable** | `{{crcOfficeNumber}}` — CRC-only, joining `{{crcOfficeCity}}`/`{{crcOfficeAddress}}` (D-05). CC has no physical-office concept, so its absence there is by design, not drift. ⚠️ **Scope superseded by NUM-02 (2026-08-03):** no longer `getConsumerDetails`-only — every live CRC agent except `Default` now holds it, spoken on explicit request only. The dictation closing described in this row is unchanged and remains unique to this agent. |
| **QA prompts** | Both CRC analysis prompts asserted flatly that no senior team exists and that volunteering a phone number is a violation, so they would have flagged the new closing. Narrowly carved out, CRC-only: `vaaniQA.txt` gains **C5a–C5d** (the one place the escalation is a phone number; the dictation loop, where the repeated check questions and re-given pieces are required behaviour and **A10 does not apply** — A7 and A9 still do; closing mid-number as a violation; and the misuse cases that *are* still violations) plus pointers from C1 and C5 and the `{{crcOfficeNumber}}` line in its data block; `postCallAnalysis.txt` gains one clause on its CONTEXT line so a no-data call reads as correctly handled — `unresolved`, no complaint — rather than a missed escalation. CC's copies untouched. |
| **Open** | Platform must inject `{{crcOfficeNumber}}` per CRC office; unconfirmed with the platform team at time of writing. Until then every call takes the fallback path, which is the pre-change behaviour. |

### GCD-04 · CRC: consumer-spoken confirmation gate, and a "nobody picks up" reply carrying CRC office hours (2026-08-03)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Escalation (CRC-only agent — CC's `getConsumerDetails` is untouched) |
| **Change 1 — confirmation gate** | `getConsumerDetails.txt` STEP 3/STEP 4 hardened. The agent was treating a `validatecontactno` success as the confirmation and firing `bpcl_fetch_all_api` on the same turn. The prompt now separates *validated* (format check) from *confirmed* (the consumer says yes out loud), ends the confirmation turn with no tool attached, and gives STEP 4 a hard precondition: the consumer's most recent message must be an affirmative reply to the confirmation question. Silence, a fragment, a digit repeat, "एक मिनट", or any unclear reply = wait, never proceed. |
| **Change 2 — "उस number पर कोई नहीं उठाता"** | New `IF THEY SAY THAT NUMBER DOESN'T WORK` block in the NO-DATA CLOSING, between C4 and C5. When the consumer says the CRC office number is unanswered or already tried: reassure once (the team is sometimes busy, try again after a while, they will certainly be attended to) and state the office timings. Said **once**; a repeat gets a brief acknowledgement and C5. Explicitly bans what this agent cannot do anyway — no complaint, no handoff, no team named as a destination, no promise anyone will call, no invented second number / helpline / email / app. |
| **Office hours spoken here** | **"सोमवार से शुक्रवार, सुबह दस बजे से शाम छह बजे तक"** — day range first, time second, hours as Hindi words. ⚠️ **This deliberately differs from the 9am–7pm-all-weekdays line every other CRC prompt speaks** (`Default`, `connectionServicesAgent`, `genericInfoComplaint`, `newConnectionAgent`). Those describe the **distributor's** hours; this describes the **CRC office's own** hours, reached via `{{crcOfficeNumber}}`. Client-confirmed 2026-08-03. Not drift — do not "fix" one to match the other. |
| **Why** | The no-data closing hands the consumer a phone number as the entire outcome. If that number goes unanswered they have nothing, and this agent has no complaint tool and no `switchagent` to fall back on. Setting the right call-back window is the only real help left. |
| **Guard** | The "never promise a call back, a time, or a window" rule in `HOW TO SPEAK THE DIGITS` now carries an explicit carve-out: stating the office's own timings is allowed in this one block only, never volunteered alongside the dictation. |
| **CC** | Not ported. CC's `getConsumerDetails` escalates by `calltransfer` and speaks no office number, so neither change has a CC counterpart. |

### D-03 · Persona positioning: central agent vs office insider

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Persona |
| **CC** | Vaani is "a voice agent **for Bharat Petroleum**". The distributor is a third party — "आपके distributor" is correct. 0 insider phrases. 125 "senior team" references. |
| **CRC** | Vaani works at a Bharat Gas / Bharat Petroleum CRC and speaks as an insider throughout: "हमारा office", "हमारे यहाँ", "आप हमारे पास आ सकते हैं". As of 2026-07-28 (see D-05) this is explicitly two different physical places — the CRC itself and the consumer's own registered distributor — but the insider tone applies to both; only the underlying address differs by context. `Default.txt:5` and `:86` **explicitly forbid** "आपके distributor" as if it were a separate party. |
| **Why** | A CRC caller is contacting their own local office; third-party framing breaks the fiction and the trust. |
| **Verified** | Phrase counts across all 30 files + explicit prohibition in CRC `Default.txt:5`. |

**Guard:** "आपके distributor" / "अपने distributor" as *spoken Hindi* must not appear in CRC.
Note: it legitimately appears in CRC inside negative instructions ("never say आपके distributor") —
a naive grep will produce false positives here. See §3, CS-01 for confirmed real violations.

### D-04 · Prompts unique to one channel

| | |
|---|---|
| **~~CC only~~ Both, as of 2026-07-31** | `postCallAnalysisFlat.txt` — **CRC counterpart created 2026-07-31**, closing the gap this row previously flagged as unconfirmed. Same purpose as CC's: the flat scoring variant returning sixteen plain-string fields (`complaintRegistered` … `notes`) with **no** advanced QA block — no `overallVerdict`, `qaScore`, `csatAnalysis`, `summary`, `callDataCapture`, `behaviorOverview`, `riskObservations`, `evaluations`, `conversationInsights` or `coachingFeedback`, and no rule review. **Built by reduction from CRC's own `postCallAnalysis.txt`, not ported from CC** — so it inherits CRC's escalation model (no `calltransfer`, no scheduled callback, complaint-as-escalation), the `getConsumerDetails` no-data carve-out, and both current policy changes (NC-01 the new-connection hold, ZIP-01 Bharat Gas Lite ZIP) rather than needing them translated in afterwards. Field definitions are byte-identical to the parent file's for all sixteen fields, so the two cannot drift on scoring semantics. ⚠️ CRC now has **five** postCallAnalysis variants and still no status marker saying which ships (`CLAUDE.md` §6.6, DRIFT-01). |
| **Both, intentionally different (2026-07-28)** | The Vaani QA reviewer — `postCallAnalysis/vaaniQA.txt` in CRC, still `promptQA.txt` in CC (NAME-05) — new in both channels. A prompt-defect reviewer, not a business-analytics pass: it reads a transcript plus the injected `{{customerStatus}}` block and returns `issues[]` (`area`, `issue`, `quote`, `expected`, `fix`) and `unhandledRequests[]`, so live traffic surfaces prompt regressions without listening to calls. Shared: the evidence bar (no verbatim agent quote → no issue), the filler allowance, the interruption exception on repeat/one-question rules, `customerStatus` precedence and its DD-MM-YYYY vs YYYY-MM-DD trap, Part A turn rules, and the Part B fact sheet. Divergent by design: **CC** carries the `calltransfer` trigger gate (C1–C5), the full callback gate (C6–C12, 9 AM–5 PM within 15 days, only after a failed transfer), and third-party persona checks; **CRC** replaces all of it with "there is no transfer, there are no callbacks" — any promise of a person or a call back is itself the violation — plus office-visit invitation rules, insider-persona checks, and the CRC-office-vs-registered-distributor address split (D-05). |
| **Both, intentionally different** | `postCallAnalysis/postCallAnalysis.txt` exists in both channels. CRC's version correctly drops the transfer/callback-scheduling fields (`transferRequested`, `callbackDate`, `callbackTime`, `feedbackDate`, `feedbackDescription`, and the `"transferred"`/`"callbackScheduled"` `callResult` values) since CRC has no `calltransfer` and never schedules callbacks — escalation is complaint registration or an office-visit invite. Fixed 2026-07-28: CRC's copy previously retained CC's CONTEXT line claiming "call transfers and callbacks are allowed" plus several "senior team" references in field rubrics (`complaintRegistered`, `issue`, `followUpRequired`, `csat` examples) — a blind-copy leftover, now corrected to CRC's actual escalation model. |

### D-05 · CRC's own CRC office vs. the consumer's registered distributor

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Persona / escalation data (CRC-only — no CC counterpart exists) |
| **Why** | CC has no physical-location concept at all. CRC agents physically sit in a Consumer Relationship Centre that serves a whole multi-district territory (e.g. the Indore CRC covers Indore, Dhar, Ujjain, Ratlam, Neemuch, Jhabua, Dewas, Mandsaur, Khandwa, Khargone, Burhanpur, Badwani, Alirajpur) — not the same physical place as the calling consumer's own registered distributor. |
| **What** | Added `{{crcOfficeAddress}}` (full address) plus a city variable across all 16 CRC prompts, 2026-07-28. **Renamed 2026-07-29: `{{crcOfficeName}}` → `{{crcOfficeCity}}`.** The old name carried a mixed "brand + city" label (e.g. `Bharat Gas, Indore`); the new variable holds **only the bare city name** (e.g. `Indore`). The office name is always **भारत गैस** and comes from no variable, so every prompt builds the spoken phrase itself — `"भारत गैस, {{crcOfficeCity}}"` — and never speaks the variable alone as if it were the office name. **Superseded by ESC-01 (2026-07-29):** neither address is a visit target any more. `{{ConsumerDetailsDistributorName}}`/`Address` are spoken on request and proactively for the two physical carve-outs (hotplate purchase, KYC document submission); the CRC pair only when the consumer asks where Vaani herself is calling from, or asks for this office's address. |
| **Verified** | Not a drift bug if `crcOfficeCity`/`crcOfficeAddress` never appear in CC — confirmed at introduction: no "calling from"/location language exists anywhere in `bpcl_contact_center/prompts`. |

### D-06 · `{{ConsumerDetailsDistributorAddress}}` propagated to CC only (2026-07-28, CRC pending)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — this was a scoping gap, not a policy difference. Flagged here per §4/§5 of the root `CLAUDE.md` change-discipline rules. Now closed in both channels. |
| **What** | `{{ConsumerDetailsDistributorAddress}}` already existed as a runtime variable (used in `Default.txt` and `connectionServicesAgent.txt` in both channels) but was missing from 9 other agents per channel: `bookingEligibleAgent`, `bookingNonEligibilityAgent`, `activeDeliveryAgent`, `eligibleDeliveryAgent`, `notEligibleDeliveryAgent`, `postDeliveryAgent`, `paymentAgent`, `subsidyAgent`, `genericInfoComplaintAgent`/`genericInfoComplaint`. |
| **CC (2026-07-28)** | Added at each spot the agent already exposes `{{ConsumerDetailsDistributorName}}`/`{{ConsumerDetailsDistMobileNumber1}}`, sharing the address on the **exact same trigger condition already stated for the name/mobile at that spot** — i.e. "share if asked" where that was the existing rule, not a proactive push (matches CC's `calltransfer`-based escalation, D-02). |
| **CRC (2026-07-28)** | The `crcOfficeCity`/`crcOfficeAddress` rework (D-05) already fixed `bookingEligibleAgent`, `bookingNonEligibilityAgent`, `paymentAgent`, and `subsidyAgent`. This follow-up closed the remaining 5: `activeDeliveryAgent`, `eligibleDeliveryAgent`, `notEligibleDeliveryAgent`, `postDeliveryAgent` (variable block + every visit-invitation line), and `genericInfoComplaint` (variable block only — its usage line already had it). Unlike CC, the address is shared **proactively alongside the name whenever inviting an office visit**, not gated behind "if asked" — because CRC's terminal escalation *is* the office visit (D-02), so the address is the load-bearing fact, not a nice-to-have alongside a phone number. The phone number keeps its narrower "only if the consumer wants to connect by phone" rule in both channels. |
| **Excluded on purpose** | `getConsumerDetails`, `routingAgent`, `emergencyAgent` (role doesn't call for it), `newConnectionAgent` (prospective consumer, no `ConsumerDetails*` record exists yet). |

### DATA-01 · `Default` holds no consumer-specific data, in both channels (2026-07-29)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — this is **shared truth**, applied identically to CC and CRC in the same session. Recorded here because it supersedes parts of D-05 and D-06. |
| **Why** | The platform fetches consumer data by caller ANI **before** the call, but the data branch resolves **at the switch out of `Default`** — data present → straight to the specialist; data absent → `getConsumerDetails`. `Default` therefore runs *before* the gate, and is the one agent that can be holding uninjected `{{...}}` placeholders with nothing behind it to catch them. The model was completing those placeholders with plausible invented names and addresses. |
| **What** | Removed from **both** `Default.txt` files: `{{ConsumerDetailsConsumerName}}`, `{{ConsumerDetailsDistributorName}}`, `{{ConsumerDetailsDistributorAddress}}`, `{{ConsumerDetailsDistMobileNumber1}}`, `{{ConsumerDetailsDistMobileNumber2}}`. The brace-detection prose ("HOW TO DETECT REAL DATA vs. NO DATA") went with them — with no consumer data left to guard, `Default` never needs an absent-data fallback. All five remain unchanged in every downstream agent, which only ever run after the gate. |
| **New behaviour** | A new `CONSUMER-SPECIFIC DATA QUESTION` section in both files: any account-specific question is classified and switched **immediately, same turn**, never answered or guessed. Distributor name/address/phone → `genericInfoComplaintAgent`. Booking/delivery/payment → STAGE 2B triage. Subsidy → `subsidyAgent`. KYC/address/mobile/name/surrender/portability → `connectionServicesAgent`. It is a data question, not a problem — so in CRC it is **not** a complaint escalation and offers no office visit. |
| **Knock-ons** | `Default` no longer uses the consumer's name at all — the greeting and the whole front of the call are name-free, and the `[first name] जी` exception to the "never open with जी" rule is deleted in both files. The hotplate/stove answer keeps price + timing but drops name/address; CRC's KYC carve-out does the same, keeping insider framing ("हमारे यहाँ") without naming the office. CRC keeps `{{crcOfficeCity}}`/`{{crcOfficeAddress}}` — static per-office config, always injected — so `ADDRESS SHARING` narrows to self-location only. |
| **Supersedes** | D-05's "spoken on request and proactively for the two physical carve-outs" and D-06's CC/CRC sharing rules **no longer describe `Default`** in either channel. Both remain accurate for every other agent. |
| **Follow-up (done 2026-07-29)** | Downstream agents now absorb the personalization `Default` gave up. All 20 name-capable agents (10 per channel) carry the same three-part pattern that made `postDeliveryAgent` reliable: the full **Consumer name rule** (strip salutation → first name only → Devanagari → once → `"[name in Devanagari] जी,"` → blank/broken means no name), a one-line restatement at the variable declaration, and — the piece most were missing — an explicit **anchor inside the first-response step**, which is what actually fires it. `emergencyAgent` and `newConnectionAgent` are excluded in both channels by decision: emergency must open with the 1906 line, and `newConnectionAgent` serves prospective consumers with no `ConsumerDetails*` record (consistent with D-06). |
| **Fixed in passing** | `connectionServicesAgent` (both channels) said *"Consumer Name — you already used this earlier in the call. Do NOT use it again."* — true only while `Default` greeted by name. After this change the name would never have been spoken on a `Default → connectionServicesAgent` route. Inverted at the declaration, at `NAME HANDLING`, and in `§22 CRITICAL REMINDERS`. Two unrecorded CC/CRC drifts also closed: CRC `subsidyAgent` had no name rule at all (only "use sparingly and naturally") where CC had the full canonical rule, and CRC `paymentAgent` carried a compressed variant missing the salutation list and the blank/broken fallback. Both now match CC verbatim. |

### DEL-01 · `activeDeliveryAgent` date gate + cancelled state (2026-07-30)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Mostly **shared truth** — the date gate, the closed status vocabulary and the cancelled state are identical in both channels. One escalation-axis difference, noted below. |
| **Trigger** | Live hallucination. `handoffSummary` carried the valid backend status `"Refill Order has been planned for Delivery"` — a status with **no date in it**. The agent spoke an expected delivery date of 30-07-2026 (**exactly `{{system.current_date}}`**), then on a later turn in the same call, with unchanged data, spoke 15-07-2026 instead, then registered a complaint against its own invented date. |
| **Root cause** | Not a missing prohibition — there were three ("I never invent a delivery date", twice more). The prompt **asserted that a date existed and ordered it spoken**: CC `activeDeliveryAgent.txt:39` (*"I am only reached when the backend has already determined the expected delivery date is today or in the future"*) and CRC `:63`, `:148`, `:194` plus every per-status branch at `:201-216` (*"delivery is expected by the date in handoffSummary"*). A negative prohibition cannot outvote a positive obligation plus an asserted premise. Both fabricated values were **prompt-supplied, not invented from nothing**: the first was derived from that premise + the injected current date (the earliest value satisfying "today or in the future"); the second leaked from the prompt's own example inventory (CC `:41` carried a literal `2026-07-14`, and the pronunciation table names day `15`). The model never experienced either as invention — it experienced them as reading its instructions, which is why the guards stayed silent. |
| **Fix — 4 parts, both channels** | **(1) Premise deleted.** Nothing now tells the agent a date exists; `handoffSummary` "may or may not" carry one. **(2) THE DATE GATE** runs before any delivery statement, every turn, and its verdict (DATE PRESENT / DATE ABSENT) is final and cannot be overturned by status wording. Includes a grounding self-check (*can I point to this exact date in the text?*) and a **cross-turn lock** (a second, different date is proof of fabrication). **(3) Closed status vocabulary S-A…S-G** — the six known backend strings plus a catch-all, each mapping to a delivery **position only**, explicitly carrying no timing. Status is read by **meaning**; the date is read **literally** — the two now follow opposite rules, stated as such. **(4) Example dates removed**; both date-format rules are now positional (*"the first pair of digits is the DAY"*), and any date-like value in the instructions is declared illustration only. |
| **The cancelled state (S-F)** | `"Refill Order Cancelled"` had **no state in either channel** and was reaching a *delivery* agent whose every path assumes a live delivery. Now an explicit state: hard DATE-ABSENT even if a date appears in the text (for a cancelled order a bare date is ambiguous — booking date, pre-cancellation delivery date, or cancellation date); **never state or imply why** it was cancelled (the empty cause slot is the same fabrication surface as the empty date slot); never say delivery is planned/pending/coming. Rebooking → `routingAgent`; refund → `routingAgent`. Complaint **on consumer request only** in both channels. Path F (consumer *wants* to cancel) is now explicitly distinguished from it. |
| **CC vs CRC — the only difference** | The closing next-step, and it follows from D-01/ESC-01. **CC** says the order is cancelled and to contact their distributor, sharing name + mobile. **CRC** says only that the order is cancelled: the backend string's own *"Please contact your distributor"* has **no valid CRC rendering** — third-party framing violates D-03, and distributor/office contact as a *resolution* was retired by ESC-01. Numbers stay available via Path C on request. |
| **Also closed** | An undeclared drift: CC had a three-way state ladder keyed on date presence; **CRC had no no-date branch at all**, so no correct behaviour was available to it. CRC now carries the same gate. Conversely CRC's `FIELD-LEVEL MISSING DATA` rule (`:73` — *an event without a date means the date is unknown*, already correct but outvoted) was ported **to CC**, which lacked it, as was CRC's two-format date rule. |
| **Fixed in passing** | CRC Path F ended *"For cancelling a refill, visit our office."* — a live ESC-01 violation and exactly the leftover CRC `CLAUDE.md` "Before you edit" rule 4 warns about. Now a complaint offer. |
| **Open** | A cancelled order reaching `activeDeliveryAgent` is an **upstream routing fault**. Handling it in place is correct and the consumer is never stranded, but `Default`/`routingAgent` should not send S-F here. Not yet fixed. |

### PAY-01 · `paymentAgent` amount gate — DEL-01's bug class applied to money (2026-07-30)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth in intent**, but the two channels' `paymentAgent` have **different data models**, so the gate is worded per channel. See the split below. |
| **Why** | Same failure shape as DEL-01, one slot over: an obligation to answer a money question, no guaranteed figure behind it, and a plausible example nearby. An amount is a **worse** slot than a date — a rupee figure is a complete speakable answer needing nothing assembled around it, and `CUSTOMER_STATUS_SPEC.md` §8.2 records the consequence: a wrong payable figure makes Vaani accuse an honest delivery person of overcharging on a live call. |
| **The two channels are NOT the same agent** | **CC** holds *no* payment data at all — FLOW 1 says outright *"I do not have this consumer's specific price or advance figure"* — so the DEL-01 premise bug cannot reproduce there. **CRC** expects `handoffSummary` to carry the retail price, advance, payable amount, refund and booking status (its GROUP 2), so CRC is the channel that actually speaks money from data. Any fix here must be translated, not copied. |
| **CC gap** | No provenance check at the moment of speaking and no cross-turn lock — `RULE 7` stated the prohibition but nothing operationalised it. Plus the money exemplar `983 → "नौ सौ तिरासी रुपये"` sits **within ~1.5% of the real cylinder RSP** (the spec's own example is ₹968.50). That makes it the single most dangerous example string in the repo: if it leaks it sounds right, so neither the consumer nor any downstream check would catch it. |
| **CRC gap — an unported CC fix** | CRC had **no `RULE 7` / NEVER INVENT A FACT equivalent at all** — no blanket prohibition on stating a figure for this consumer — and no equivalent of CC's *"never tell the consumer a charge was correct or wrong without a real comparison behind it"*. In the channel with no `calltransfer` fallback, on its most money-sensitive agent. |
| **Fix — both channels** | A **THE AMOUNT GATE** block (CC `RULE 7A`, CRC new `RULE 8` + `RULE 8A`), run before any rupee figure is spoken, every turn: an amount may come from exactly three sources — the literal text of `handoffSummary`, `{{consumerReportedAmount}}` when it holds a real number, or the consumer's own words on this call — and there is no fourth. Adds a **grounding self-check** (*which of the three gave me this exact number?*), a **cross-turn lock**, *a policy range is never this consumer's figure*, and a ban on "roughly / around / about nine hundred". CRC additionally gets *a statement that money is owed is not an amount* — the money analogue of DEL-01's *a status sentence is not a date* — because CRC reads payment facts from prose. CC's money exemplars keep the conversion-table guard treatment from DEL-01, with the near-real-RSP risk called out explicitly in the prompt. |
| **Corrected from the DEL-01 pass** | Both `paymentAgent` files **do** carry a strong example-text guard (CC `RULE 3B`, CRC `RULE 3C`, worded identically) — an earlier grep using `activeDeliveryAgent`'s phrasing reported these absent. They were always there. |
| **Superseded** | The `983` exemplar has since been swept — see GATE-01. |
| **Also found, not fixed** | CRC has **three** `postCallAnalysis` variants side by side (`postCallAnalysis_promptQA_merged.txt`, `postCallAnalysis_withPromptQA.txt`, `vaaniQA.txt`) with no marker for which is deployed — exactly the condition `CLAUDE.md` §6.6 requires a status marker for. Also: the number-pronunciation exemplar set is **drifted** — the money line exists in 10 CC files but only 5 CRC ones, and CRC's `paymentAgent` normalises money by rule with no exemplars at all (safer, and undeclared). |

### GATE-01 · The grounding gate rolled out by tier, and the exemplar sweep (2026-07-30)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**, applied to both channels in the same session. Wording is adapted per agent to the value it speaks; the *shape* is identical everywhere. |
| **Scope decision** | The bug needs three ingredients: an **obligation** to state a value, **no guarantee** the value is present, and a **plausible exemplar** nearby. Agents missing the first are not at risk, and adding gates there dilutes the rule where it matters. Rolled out by tier rather than uniformly. |
| **Tier 1 — gated (6 files)** | `activeDeliveryAgent` (DEL-01), `postDeliveryAgent`, `paymentAgent` (PAY-01), `subsidyAgent`, `bookingNonEligibilityAgent`, plus `genericInfoComplaintAgent` from Tier 2 — it does reference a booking date. Both channels each. |
| **Tier 2 — audited, deliberately NOT gated** | `connectionServicesAgent` and `bookingEligibleAgent`, both channels. Audited and they speak **no consumer-specific date or figure** — `connectionServicesAgent` states only eKYC valid/not-valid (the month count was already dropped by CUSTOMER_STATUS_SPEC §1.5) and `bookingEligibleAgent` handles a booking failure with no numbers. A gate here would be noise. |
| **Tier 3 — out of scope** | `emergencyAgent` (fixed 1906 line), `routingAgent` (speaks nothing), `Default` (consumer data already removed — DATA-01), `getConsumerDetails`, `newConnectionAgent`. |
| **The canonical shape** | (1) never assert the value exists — `MAY or MAY NOT`; (2) a **literal-presence gate** run before speaking, every turn, with PRESENT/ABSENT branches; (3) **X IS NOT A Y** — a status word is not an amount, an event is not a date, a blocker is not a number; (4) a **grounding self-check** (*can I point to this exact value in the text?*); (5) a **cross-turn lock** (a second, different value is proof of fabrication); (6) general policy is never this consumer's value. Ported as intent, never copy-pasted — each agent's wording names its own value type. |
| **Asserted premises deleted** | Three found and removed, all of the DEL-01 form: CRC `postDeliveryAgent` (*"it carries the date the refill was already delivered"*), CRC `bookingNonEligibilityAgent` (*"It names the required booking gap, the last booking date, how many days have passed, how many days remain, and the exact date"* — five values asserted at once), and CC `postDeliveryAgent`'s reached-only-when premise. In every case CC was already conditional (*"IF handoffSummary states them"*) and CRC was not — the same direction of drift as DEL-01. |
| **Exemplar sweep — 15 files** | `983 → "नौ सौ तिरासी रुपये"` replaced with `247` and `950.50` with `60.50`, across all 15 files carrying the money line (4 wording variants, identical numerals). **Why it mattered:** `983` sits within ~1.5% of the real cylinder RSP, so a leak would sound correct and go undisputed. Worse found in CRC `subsidyAgent`, which used **`₹300.00` and `₹968.50` — the actual subsidy and RSP figures from the spec's own examples** — in the agent that discusses subsidy money; replaced and reframed as a conversion table. Literal example dates in gated agents converted to positional wording (*"the first pair of digits is the DAY"*), which also states both opposite date formats side by side per CUSTOMER_STATUS_SPEC §1.7. |
| **Left in place, by decision** | The day-number pronunciation table (`15 becomes पंद्रह`) — every value 1–31 is a valid day so no exemplar is "safe", the mapping is genuinely needed for TTS, and the gate now blocks the leak structurally. Given an inline conversion-table guard instead. The four remaining `983` hits are in `postCallAnalysis`/`vaaniQA` files, which never speak to a consumer. |
| **ESC-01 violations found here** | Fixed under ESC-03 below. |

### DRIFT-01 · The unported-fix pattern, measured and closed (2026-07-30)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. Everything here is a guard that should always have existed in both channels. |
| **Why this row exists** | DEL-01, PAY-01 and GATE-01 each independently found the same thing: **CC held a guard, CRC did not.** Three instances is a pattern, not a coincidence, so it was measured rather than guessed — a presence matrix of 5 canonical guard phrases across all 14 shared agents, both channels. |
| **Result: 20 one-sided guards** | The dominant one was **`SINGLE-PASS OUTPUT CONTRACT`** — the TTS repeat-suppression guard appended to `NO DOUBLE-FORM`. Present in **13 of 14 CC agents, and in exactly one CRC agent** (`Default`). CC extended `NO DOUBLE-FORM` at some point and CRC never received it, so 14 CRC prompts had the double-form ban without the single-pass contract that enforces it. |
| **Ported CC → CRC** | `SINGLE-PASS OUTPUT CONTRACT` into **14 CRC files** (every one holding `NO DOUBLE-FORM`), person-matched per file — the I-form into the 6 first-person prompts, the You-form into the 8 second-person ones. `NEVER OUTPUT EXAMPLE TEXT` into CRC `subsidyAgent` (absent) and CRC `bookingEligibleAgent` (had only a weaker one-line variant, now the canonical form). |
| **Ported CRC → CC** | The drift runs both ways. `NEVER CALCULATE` was **CRC-only** in three agents; CC `connectionServicesAgent` had **no arithmetic guard at all**, which matters because CUSTOMER_STATUS_SPEC §11.1 requires its `gapToNextKyc` comparisons deleted and §1.5 drops the KYC month count — so CC could work out a due date it is not supposed to have. Ported, with the explicit statement that only valid/not-valid is known. |
| **Wording aligned** | CC `bookingNonEligibilityAgent` and `subsidyAgent` were semantically covered by GATE-01's VALUE GATE but did not carry the literal `NEVER CALCULATE` phrase, so an audit would keep re-flagging them. Phrase added. **Matrix now reads 0 one-sided guards, down from 20.** |
| **Standing recommendation** | Re-run the presence matrix after any guard is added to one channel. It is a cheap grep and it is the only thing in this repo that catches an unported fix before it becomes a live defect. |

### ESC-03 · Remaining office-as-resolution endings retired (2026-07-30)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Escalation — completes ESC-01, which retired the office visit but left three sites unconverted. |
| **Fixed (2)** | `subsidyAgent.txt` S6 (DBTL one-time advance recovery) — *"check the website or visit our office"* → website plus an offered complaint. `bookingNonEligibilityAgent.txt` TASK — *"You handle human/office-contact/language/non-LPG-product requests inline by encouraging an office visit"* → COMPLAINT ESCALATION. That second one was the worse of the two: it made the office visit the **standing instruction for every human request** in that agent, directly contradicting ESC-01's core rule. |
| **Reverted — see ESC-04** | `notEligibleDeliveryAgent`'s inactive-connection branch was changed to an offered complaint and has been **restored to the office visit**. A complaint cannot be registered against an inactive connection, so the complaint was never an available outcome there. |
| **Deliberately NOT changed** | `notEligibleDeliveryAgent`'s KYC line (*"get K Y C done by visiting our office or through the Hello B P C L App"*) — KYC document submission is one of ESC-01's two genuine counter carve-outs. `newConnectionAgent`'s document/office-visit content, same reason. |

### ESC-04 · Inactive connection is a third ESC-01 carve-out — no complaint is possible (2026-07-30)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Escalation — a **limit on ESC-01**, in both channels. |
| **The constraint** | **`bpcl_create_complaint` cannot register a complaint against an inactive connection.** Confirmed by the client, 2026-07-30. A complaint is therefore not an available outcome for these consumers, and no prompt may offer one or promise that a team will make contact. |
| **Why this is a carve-out, not a violation** | ESC-01 retired the office visit because *"telling them to travel is not an outcome; a registered complaint is."* When the complaint tool cannot accept the case, that reasoning inverts: the office visit becomes the **only** real outcome. Reactivation happens at a counter. This joins hotplate purchase and KYC document submission as the **third** genuine carve-out — and unlike those two, it is driven by a hard tool limitation rather than a physical-action need. |
| **Applied** | `notEligibleDeliveryAgent` (CRC) — inactive branch restored to the office visit with name, timing, and number on request, plus an explicit note on why a complaint is not offered. `bookingNonEligibilityAgent` (**both channels**) — the Inactive blocker already pointed to the distributor, but both agents carry a *"if the consumer disputes the block, register a complaint so the dispute can be reviewed"* rule that would have fired for exactly these consumers and failed. Both now state that the dispute-becomes-a-complaint rule does **not** apply to the Inactive blocker. |
| **⚠️ Open — the wider interaction** | ESC-01 makes the complaint the **universal** CRC escalation: a human request, a callback request, an insisted language or non-LPG request, and any unresolved flow all end in `bpcl_create_complaint`. For an inactive consumer, **every one of those paths now ends in a tool failure**, landing on the ESC-01 failure line (*"अभी complaint register करने में तकनीकी समस्या आ रही है"*) — which is misleading, because the cause is not technical. Only the two agents above currently know the connection is inactive; the other eight complaint-capable CRC agents do not, so they cannot avoid it. Needs a decision: either the platform exposes connection status to every complaint-capable agent, or the failure line gains an inactive-specific variant. Not fixable in the prompts alone. |
| **Verified** | A full CRC sweep for office-as-resolution language now returns only prohibitions, QA rubrics, and the two legitimate carve-outs. |

### QA-01 · `vaaniQA` false positives closed with positive anchors, not exception lists (2026-07-31)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. The same four edits landed in CC `promptQA.txt` and in all three CRC QA-bearing files in the same session. |
| **The failure mode** | Three rules were written **only as prohibitions**, so the reviewer had no picture of the correct form and flagged correct agent behaviour. Observed on live CRC calls: a complaint number spoken correctly digit by digit was reported as "spoken as a whole number"; `"S M S"` was reported as an A1 double form; and adding detail to an already-registered complaint was reported as a repeat under A10. |
| **The fix shape** | Each rule made **two-sided** — the existing prohibition plus one line showing the canonical correct form. Deliberately **not** a "these are not issues" list: an exception catalogue trains the reviewer to pattern-match near-misses to the exceptions and gets noisier with every entry. Four lines total. |
| **A1** | Now states the rule is about saying one thing twice, not about English. Latin-script English words inside Hindi (complaint, SMS, office, register, book, delivery) are the intended house style, and letter-by-letter acronyms (`"S M S"`) are how they are meant to be spoken. Only the *pairing* of two forms violates. |
| **A7** | Now carries a positive exemplar — `"एक - दो - एक - नौ - तीन - आठ - एक - तीन"` — and names the two real violations: numerals left as numerals, or the identifier spoken as one quantity. A4 and A5 always had positive exemplars, which is why money and dates never produced this false positive. |
| **Same-subject complaints** | CRC **C3d** rewritten and CC gains a new **C8a** (it had no equivalent rule at all). The test is the **subject**: restating, rephrasing, escalating in tone, or adding new detail to an already-registered issue is the *same* complaint — acknowledging it and saying the detail has been added is correct, and is not an A10 repeat. A genuinely different subject (payment registered, then a delivery fault) *is* a second complaint, normally registered by another agent after a switch. Mirrors the agent-side rule that already exists at `paymentAgent.txt:190` (`SAME-ISSUE / DUPLICATE GUARD`). Guards **both** directions — the previous wording invited the mirror false positive of flagging a legitimate second complaint as a duplicate. |
| **Filler list narrowed** | Was nine items; now exactly four are always fine anywhere in a turn — `"जी"`, `"अच्छा"`, `"hmm"`, `"umm"` (plus `"हम्म"`, the same sound in Devanagari). `"जी हाँ"`, `"ठीक है"`, `"बिल्कुल"`, `"समझ गई"` lost blanket immunity and are now judged under the normal rules (A8, A10, A12) — they are still only reportable if a listed rule is actually broken. Client decision, 2026-07-31. |
| **Generic guard** | One line added to HOW TO DECIDE: the quote must show the fault on its own; if the fault is visible only by assuming something the quoted words do not show, drop the issue. This is the net for the whole class — the complaint-number false positive had a quote that flatly contradicted the claim. |
| **⚠️ Open — filler contradiction** | The QA prompt says fillers are intended behaviour; `paymentAgent.txt:41` (and the same HOW I SPEAK block in several other agents) tells the agent *never* to open with `जी`/`अच्छा`/`ठीक है`/`बिल्कुल`/`समझ गई`. Client decision was that **the QA side wins — fillers are never reported** — so the agent prompts were left untouched. Whether the agent-side ban should be relaxed to match is unresolved. |
| **⚠️ Open — three CRC variants** | These edits were applied to all three CRC QA-bearing files (`vaaniQA.txt`, `postCallAnalysis_promptQA_merged.txt`, `postCallAnalysis_withPromptQA.txt`) because none carries a status marker saying which is deployed. Same condition DRIFT-01 already logged; `CLAUDE.md` §6.6 still unsatisfied. |
| **Verified** | Exact-match substitution with a single-hit assertion per file; all four files confirmed changed. |

### NC-01 · New 14.2 kg domestic connections are ON HOLD — CRC only, CC deliberately behind (2026-07-31)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither axis — this is a **domain-fact/policy change**, and therefore normally shared truth. It is **CRC-only by explicit client decision**, which is why it needs this row. |
| **The policy** | New connections for the **14.2 kg domestic cylinder are closed**, open-ended, with **no reopening date and no stated reason**. **Ujjwala / PMUY is included**, because it is issued on the same 14.2 kg cylinder. The distributor office is on hold too — it cannot accept an application either. |
| **What Vaani does** | Says it plainly — `"अभी नए कनेक्शन बंद हैं।"` — and gives one next step, `"कुछ समय बाद check कर लीजिएगा।"` She then proactively offers **Bharat Gas Mini (5 kg)** so the consumer leaves with an option and not just a refusal. Nothing is registered, nobody is promised contact. |
| **Inform-only — no complaint, and this is the point** | A prospective consumer has **no record**, so there is nothing to attach a `bpcl_create_complaint` to, and no complaint could reopen connections anyway. Registering one would manufacture a ticket nobody can action and promise contact that is not coming. So on this topic `newConnectionAgent` **does not escalate at all** — it holds the consumer itself, restates calmly, and closes. This is a deliberate exception to ESC-01, whose "the complaint *is* the escalation" rule assumes a consumer record exists. Same reasoning as ESC-04 and the `getConsumerDetails` carve-out. |
| **No guidance** | While the hold is in force Vaani gives **no** how-to-apply, documents, charges, timeline, what-you-receive, form help, or distributor lookup for a domestic application. That guidance would send someone to prepare for something nobody can accept. The office is never offered as a workaround. |
| **Already applied** | A **different case** — do not give them the hold message. They are told to contact their distributor for the status. Vaani never says whether that application is moving, frozen or cancelled, because she does not know. |
| **NOT affected** | Commercial connections (still issued, still applied for at the distributor office); Bharat Gas Mini; second/additional cylinder; portability; and the **city-shift Transfer Voucher path**, which the client explicitly carved out — a shifting consumer already holds a connection. `connectionServicesAgent` is therefore **unchanged**. ⚠️ Residual risk accepted by the client: a consumer could surrender here and find the new city unable to release. |
| **File split** | New live prompt `newConnectionAgent_onHold.txt` (~330 lines). The original 2,033-line `newConnectionAgent.txt` — §15-EC, §15-MS, §15-OV, the §15A guided app flow and §15B office flow — is **retained unchanged and PARKED**, since there is no version control and it is the only copy. Both files carry a status header per `CLAUDE.md` §6.6. `agentName` stays `newConnectionAgent`; topology unchanged. |
| **⚠️ Deployment risk** | Client chose to keep the old filename, so **the platform must be repointed** at `newConnectionAgent_onHold.txt`. If that step is missed, the full apply journey is still live and consumers will be walked through applying for something on hold. No prompt-side guard can prevent this. |
| **Other CRC files** | `Default` MODULE N replaced (it recited charges, documents, timeline, eligibility) and its two new-connection FAQ answers retired; `routingAgent` descriptor plus a no-escalate exception; all four postCallAnalysis/QA files — the shared facts block rewritten, `fcr`/`callResult`/`followUpRequired`/`callbackPriority` taught that "informed them it's on hold" is a **successful** outcome, and a **C3-NC** exception so the reviewer stops flagging the absent complaint as a violation. |
| **Why CC is behind** | Client decision, 2026-07-31: "For now CRC only." The real-world policy is not channel-specific, so **this is a known gap, not a design difference** — CC still tells consumers how to apply for a connection that is on hold. Port to CC when the client confirms. |
| **Unknowns at time of writing** | The reason for the hold, any reopening date, and whether pending applications are still being processed. Vaani states none of them and guesses at none of them. |
| **Verified** | Both channels swept for `7 kg`/`सात kilogram` (unrelated defect fixed in the same session — Bharat Gas Mini is **5 kg**, 3 occurrences per channel); CRC swept for live new-connection guidance outside the parked file. |
