### ZIP-01 · Bharat Gas Lite ZIP rolled out across CRC — CC deliberately behind (2026-07-31)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — a **product/domain-fact change**, normally shared truth. **CRC-only by client decision**, same as NC-01. |
| **The product** | **भारत गैस लाइट ज़िप**, launched **July 2026**. Premium **Free Trade LPG (FTL)**, non-subsidised: the Bharat Gas Lite composite cylinder + instant new connection + **express delivery within four hours**. Cylinder is lightweight, corrosion-free, transparent (visible gas level), ergonomic, safer than steel. **Lite is the cylinder; Lite ZIP is the service package** — the distinction consumers ask about most. |
| **Spoken name** | **भारत गैस लाइट ज़िप**, everywhere. Client chose the spaced form over the press note's "भारतगैस", because this repo already fixes the brand as **भारत गैस**. Never the Roman form, never both together (double form). |
| **ZIP is EXEMPT from the NC-01 hold** | This is the load-bearing fact. ZIP is the **one new-connection route still open**. `newConnectionAgent_onHold` now offers **Mini and ZIP together in one short turn** — client wording, both options presented simultaneously, consumer chooses — and explains only the chosen one on the **next** turn. Benefits never crammed into the offer: the consumer has just been told no. |
| **The availability hedge** | ZIP is not in every city (30 at launch, 100 across 24 states by 15 Aug 2026). **Nothing on this side can see whether it is in the caller's city**, so Vaani never says ZIP is available to them — the hedge rides with every offer. Raising hopes and dropping them is worse than the original no. |
| **The soft unknown line — ZIP ONLY** | ZIP's commercial spec does not exist yet (capacity, charges, deposit, refill price, refund/cancellation, documents/KYC, booking channels, express-delivery charges/coverage, regulator & stove compatibility, steel exchange, cylinder limits, payment methods, advance payment, warranty/damage, existing-consumer switch). For these the flat `"यह जानकारी मेरे पास नहीं है"` is replaced by: *"जी, यह service अभी नई है, तो फ़िलहाल मेरे पास इतनी ही जानकारी है… नज़दीकी भारत गैस distributor से बात कर लीजिए — Hello B P C L App और भारत पेट्रोलियम की website पर भी जानकारी मिल जाएगी।"* Full form **once per call**, short form after. **"संपर्क कीजिए", never "जाकर पूछिए"** — a phone enquiry, not a journey, so ESC-01 is untouched. **Explicitly fenced**: every agent carries a clause saying this does not apply outside ZIP, or it would quietly reopen the distributor-redirect habit ESC-01 retired. |
| **⚠️ Time-bound wording** | *"यह service अभी नई है"* is accurate in July 2026 and **expires silently** — no date logic, no build step, nothing that notices. Client accepted the debt and will reword later. Revisit when the KB fills out, or ~6 months, whichever first. |
| **The data problem, and the fix** | Delivery agents could not tell a ZIP booking from a standard one — **`customerStatus` carries no product-type field**. Originally resolved two ways: (1) a new **`{{ConsumerDetailsProductType}}`** variable, consumed by all four delivery agents and the six other specialists; (2) **consumer-volunteered ZIP is usable, agent-asked is banned** — Vaani acts on ZIP when the consumer names it, and never probes. Fits the existing slot-memory rule (a filled slot is never asked). **Fix (1) was removed on 04-08-2026 — see the row below. Only (2) is live.** |
| **⚠️ `{{ConsumerDetailsProductType}}` REMOVED — 04-08-2026** | The variable **never existed**: the platform does not inject it and no equivalent field was ever confirmed. It was removed from all ten prompts that referenced it (four delivery agents, both booking agents, `paymentAgent`, `subsidyAgent`, `connectionServicesAgent`, `genericInfoComplaint`). **No behaviour changed** — an uninjected variable meant only the volunteered-ZIP path ever fired — and it retired a live risk: a brace-wrapped placeholder reaching the model is exactly what the never-speak-a-placeholder rule guards against. **Do not re-add it under this name.** Standing gap: delivery agents cannot distinguish a ZIP booking from a standard one unless the consumer says so. If the platform team exposes a real product field, rename the references back in rather than reinventing the block. |
| **~~OFFERED in one agent only — KB everywhere else (04-08-2026)~~ SUPERSEDED by ZIP-02 (05-08-2026)** | ZIP is **offered** solely in `newConnectionAgent_onHold` (§8-ALT, Mini + ZIP together, consumer chooses). In all ten other ZIP-bearing agents it is a **knowledge base only**: Vaani answers a ZIP question the consumer asks and never raises it herself, never presents it as an option, and never offers it against a blocked booking, a delivery problem or the extra-refill block. **Bharat Gas Mini is the only alternative offered outside the new-connection flow.** Enforced by the `ZIP IS KNOWLEDGE ONLY IN MY FLOW — I NEVER OFFER IT` clause in the ten specialists/delivery agents, the imperative equivalent in `Default` MODULE Z, and a matching entry at the head of `vaaniQA`'s STILL VIOLATIONS ON ZIP list. Both booking agents and `connectionServicesAgent` already carried narrower no-offer rules; those stay and are now backed by the general clause. |
| **Four hours is a standard, never a promise** | Vaani may state ZIP's four-hour express standard as a product fact. She must **never** commit to a specific delivery arriving in four hours — the existing no-delivery-promise rule binds even when ZIP is confirmed. If ZIP is confirmed and the window has clearly passed, it is a genuine service failure and goes through the normal complaint path. |
| **~~Deliberately NOT asserted~~ RESOLVED by ZIP-02 (05-08-2026)** | Client confirmed: an existing consumer **may take** a ZIP alongside their connection but **may not switch or exchange into one**. Was previously: whether an **existing registered consumer** can take, switch to, or exchange for ZIP is **unconfirmed**. Vaani never says they can and never says they cannot. Consequence: ZIP is **not** offered as a way around a blocked booking (`bookingNonEligibilityAgent` keeps Mini as Path A), and `connectionServicesAgent` answers with the soft line rather than a process. |
| **Files touched (13)** | `Default` (MODULE Z + three-way ZIP triage), `newConnectionAgent_onHold` (§13-Z, §13-Z-GAP, rewritten §8-ALT), `routingAgent` (route-by-intent descriptor), the four delivery agents, both booking agents, `subsidyAgent` (ZIP is non-subsidised — the one ZIP fact squarely in its domain), `paymentAgent`, `genericInfoComplaint`, `connectionServicesAgent`. |
| **QA side** | The three QA-bearing files gained ZIP to the allowed-facts block (or every correct ZIP statement would be reported as invented), an explicit note that the softer line and the distributor/app/website pointer are **required** behaviour and not an office-visit escalation, and a list of what **is** still a ZIP violation. `departmentArea` maps ZIP by intent — wanting one is `newConnection`, a problem with one maps to that problem's domain. |
| **Routing model** | Three-way, by intent not keyword: **awareness** → `Default` answers inline (most ZIP calls end here); **wants one** → `newConnectionAgent`; **problem with one they hold** → the agent that owns that problem today. A late ZIP delivery is a delivery problem that happens to be ZIP. |
| **Why CC is behind** | Client decision, 2026-07-31: CRC only, same as NC-01. The product is not channel-specific, so this is a **known gap, not a design difference**. |
| **Verified** | Single-hit assertions per file on every substitution; all 13 prompts plus 4 analysis files confirmed changed. |

### TTS-01 · The CRC office pair gets the injected-address speaking pattern (2026-07-31)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — a **voice/TTS rule**, normally shared truth, but it governs `{{crcOfficeCity}}`/`{{crcOfficeAddress}}`, which are **CRC-only variables** (D-05). CC has no physical-office concept, so there is nothing to port. |
| **The defect** | Observed live: Vaani spoke both variables **exactly as the backend injects them** — the city as a raw Latin token inside an otherwise Hindi sentence, and the full address as raw ALL-CAPS English with abbreviations, punctuation and a trailing pin code read straight out. The conversion rule that already existed for the *consumer's distributor* address (`connectionServicesAgent` §9 Rule 6 / `TTS-SAFE DELIVERY`) had **never been extended to this office's own pair**. Every prompt said *what* to speak and *when*; none said *how*. |
| **Why it was invisible** | The two variable-definition blocks were written entirely around policy — bare city name only, build "भारत गैस, [city]" yourself, never a visit target — so they read as complete. `genericInfoComplaint` and `connectionServicesAgent` each carried a one-clause *pointer* to TTS-safe delivery ("same as any distributor address"), which is why the gap looked closed from those two files. The other 11 prompts had no delivery rule of any kind. |
| **The fix** | One self-contained `TTS-SAFE DELIVERY — THE CRC OFFICE PAIR` block placed directly under the variable definition in **13 CRC prompts** — `Default`, both booking agents, all four delivery agents, `paymentAgent`, `subsidyAgent`, `genericInfoComplaint`, `newConnectionAgent_onHold`, `getConsumerDetails`, and the parked `newConnectionAgent` — in each file's own voice (`I` vs `you`). `connectionServicesAgent` instead had its existing `TTS-SAFE DELIVERY` header widened from *"ALL distributor data"* to *"ALL injected location data"* with two office-pair bullets, since it already owns the canonical §9 Rule 6. **Coverage is now every file in the workspace that holds any `crcOffice*` variable** — asserted by sweep, not by inspection. |
| **`getConsumerDetails` was a real gap, not an exemption** | The first pass skipped it on the reasoning that it holds only `{{crcOfficeNumber}}`. Wrong: its `§1` line explicitly lets it answer the self-location question with `"भारत गैस, {{crcOfficeCity}}"` and the full `{{crcOfficeAddress}}` on request, so it speaks the pair like everyone else. It now carries the block plus one clause extending the same principle to `{{crcOfficeNumber}}` — Hindi-word digits, dictated in the three pieces of the NO-DATA CLOSING, never a number that did not arrive in the variable. |
| **The parked file too** | `newConnectionAgent.txt` (PARKED per NC-01) received the block as well. It is a **safety rule, not a flow change** — the parked apply journey is untouched — and NC-01's ⚠️ deployment risk means it could still be the live prompt if the platform is not repointed. Recorded here because it breaks the "retained unchanged" wording in NC-01. |
| **What the block says** | City → transliterate to Devanagari, spoken only inside the self-built phrase, never the raw Latin value and never the variable alone. Address → natural spoken parts with a pause between each (building/shop number, building or colony name, landmark, area, city); numbers as Hindi words; abbreviations expanded or spelled; ALL CAPS to natural case; `&` and stray punctuation dropped; Indian proper nouns to Devanagari, everyday English loanwords left alone. Pin code → dropped unless asked, then digit by digit in Hindi words. Long address or a consumer noting it down → slow mode, two or three parts at a time. |
| **No worked example, deliberately** | The block gives **no sample address**. Client instruction, 2026-07-31: a specimen address sitting in the prompt is a hallucination source, and a fabricated office address spoken to a real consumer is worse than a robotic one. The rule is written as a transformation, not a lookup. Each copy also carries a `CONVERT, NEVER SUBSTITUTE` clause saying the absence of a sample is deliberate — otherwise a later editor "helpfully" adds one back. |
| **Every specimen removed — both channels** | The same instruction was applied to the examples that already existed. **(1)** `(e.g. "Indore")` illustrating the bare-city-name shape — deleted from **11 CRC prompts and all 3 QA files**, replaced with *"the city name and nothing around it"*. It was the likeliest thing to be spoken verbatim: a real city, in the right script, sitting next to the variable it illustrates. **(2)** §9 Rule 6's `KHARGHAR BHARATGAS DISTRIBUTORS` / `SHOP NO.22, PLOT NO.17 & 18 …410210` before-and-after pair — removed in **both channels** and rewritten as a description of the transformation (order of parts, numbers to Hindi words, abbreviations expanded, pin code dropped unless asked). **(3)** The `TTS-SAFE DELIVERY` worked example, which carried a full fake name **and** address **and** a plausible 10-digit mobile — replaced in both channels by a shape-only template where every field is a bracketed instruction, with only the office-timing sentence as fixed text. **(4)** `getConsumerDetails` in both channels: *"NEVER write raw digits (like 9876543210)"* → the same rule stated without a dummy number. |
| **Why removing a WRONG/RIGHT pair is safe here** | The pair taught format, but the format is fully expressible as an ordered transformation, and every element it demonstrated survives in prose. What it also did was park a complete, plausible, speakable Indian address inside a prompt whose whole job is speaking addresses. On a voice channel, a specimen that gets spoken once is a wrong address given to a real consumer. |
| **Also fixed in passing** | Both new-connection prompts told the agent to answer the self-location question *"casually with `{{crcOfficeCity}}`"* — speaking the variable alone, which every other prompt explicitly forbids (D-05). `newConnectionAgent_onHold` §16 and the parked `newConnectionAgent` §19 now both build `"भारत गैस, {{crcOfficeCity}}"` like the rest. The two files were the **only** place this defect survived; swept and confirmed. |
| **QA side** | All three QA-bearing files gained a two-sided line under the office-pair fact block (QA-01 shape): a converted city or address **no longer matching the raw variable letter for letter is correct behaviour and never an issue**; reading either value aloud as it arrives is the violation. Without it the reviewer would flag every correct conversion as a mismatch against the injected value. |
| **⚠️ Open — the wider gap this exposes** | §9 Rule 6 and `TTS-SAFE DELIVERY` still live in **`connectionServicesAgent` only, in both channels**. The other 12 agents per channel speak `{{ConsumerDetailsDistributorName}}`/`{{ConsumerDetailsDistributorAddress}}` with no conversion rule at all — the identical defect, on the distributor pair, across ~24 prompts. Deliberately **not** fixed here: it is shared truth and would have to land in both channels in one session (`CLAUDE.md` §6.4). Needs its own pass. |
| **⚠️ Open — one specimen left standing** | §9 Rule 2 in both `connectionServicesAgent` files still demonstrates digit flow with a fabricated 10-digit number (`"उनका number है नौ चार दो …"`), as does its slow-mode example. Left in place because the WRONG/RIGHT contrast it teaches — natural flow beats dash-separated digits, which break TTS — **cannot be shown without digits**, and because QA-01 deliberately *added* a positive digit exemplar on the reviewer side by client decision. Same hallucination class as the address specimen, lower blast radius (a wrong number is verifiable and recoverable; a wrong address sends someone travelling). Flagged for a client call, not fixed unilaterally. |
| **Verified** | Single-hit assertion per file on every substitution. 21 files changed: 13 CRC prompts + `connectionServicesAgent` (CRC) + 3 CRC QA files + `connectionServicesAgent` (CC) + `getConsumerDetails` (CC). Two sweeps returned zero: no `crcOffice*`-holding file without the rule, and no `Indore`/`KHARGHAR`/`REKHI`/`DAFFODIL`/dummy-number specimen anywhere in either channel. |

### TTS-02 · Web addresses spoken in typed form — RULE 3 was an example, not a rule (2026-07-31)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. Landed in **both channels** in the same session. |
| **The defect** | Observed live on `newConnectionAgent_onHold`: Vaani said *"आप www.my.ebharatgas.com पर जाइए"* — the address typed out, `www.` and all, straight into TTS. The prompt's own `§13-L` carried the correct spoken form (`m - y - dot - e - bharat - gas - dot - com`), so the model did not read it wrong; it **reconstructed the URL from world knowledge** and spoke the written one. |
| **Root cause** | `RULE 3 — Portals / URLs` was phrased as *"first time naturally — [one example]"*. An example with no prohibition. Every sibling rule is two-sided — Rule 2 says **NEVER** write raw digits, Rule 5 says **never** the ₹ symbol — but Rule 3 never said a web address must not be spoken in typed form, and never said which addresses may be named at all. Nothing blocked the model from supplying a URL it knew. |
| **The fix** | Rule 3 rewritten as a prohibition plus a closed list, in **all 7 files that carry it** (`connectionServicesAgent`, `subsidyAgent`, `newConnectionAgent` in both channels, plus CRC's `newConnectionAgent_onHold`): never `www.`, never a full stop inside the address, never `.com`/`.in` as written text, never a slash; `"dot"` is a spoken word and every part is separated by `" - "`. **The only addresses that may be named are the ones written in that prompt, in exactly the spoken form given there** — never assembled, completed, corrected or guessed, never a `w - w - w` added to one that lacks it, and never a fallback to an address known from elsewhere. If it is not in the prompt, Vaani says she does not have it. |
| **`Default` had no Rule 3 at all** | Both channels' `Default` carry the LPG knowledge base and the FAQ answers but no URL rule. One compact line added to each `LANGUAGE` block, same closed-list shape. |
| **Raw URLs in the knowledge base — the second source** | The rule alone was not enough: the KB itself held typed addresses that Vaani read out verbatim. CRC `Default` had `mylpg.in` twice — once in the subsidy KB line and once **inside a ready-made spoken Hindi FAQ answer** — and both channels had `Portability: Choose distributor on e-bharatgas.com`. All converted to the dashed spoken form. |
| **An unported CC fix, found on the way** | CC `Default` had already been fixed to `my l p g dot in` in both places; CRC still had raw `mylpg.in`. Textbook DRIFT-01. Both channels are now on the canonical dashed form (`"my - L P G - dot - in"`), which also retires CC's inconsistent space-separated variant. |
| **Canonical spoken forms** | `"w - w - w - dot - bharat - petroleum - dot - in"` · `"w - w - w - dot - e - bharat - gas - dot - com"` · `"m - y - dot - e - bharat - gas - dot - com"` (a different address from the previous one — do **not** merge them) · `"my - L P G - dot - in"`. `connectionServicesAgent`'s ellipsis style (`"w… w… w… dot..."`) is retired in both channels. |
| **QA side** | All 4 QA-bearing files (3 CRC + CC `promptQA`) gained a two-sided line: a dashed address is correct and never an issue; the typed form, **or any web address outside the canonical three**, is a violation. Without the second half the reviewer would have passed the exact failure that started this. |
| **Verified** | Single-hit assertion per substitution across 15 files. Final sweep for `www.` / `.com` / `mylpg.in` / `bharatgas.com` in both channels returns only the new prohibition text itself and the `sip:` transfer parameter (never spoken). |

---

### CPL-03 · The registering line is not the registration — failed-turn and recovery clauses (2026-08-03)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. Landed in **CRC only** this session; **CC deliberately behind**, same as NC-01 and ZIP-01. Client asked for CRC first so it can be validated on live calls. |
| **The defect** | Observed live on CRC `postDeliveryAgent`. Vaani spoke *"एक मिनट रुकिए, मैं आपकी complaint register कर रही हूँ"* and **never invoked `bpcl_create_complaint`**. The platform's silence prompt fired ten seconds later; on the next turn Vaani read the success template and spoke a **fabricated seven-digit complaint number**. Reported as happening across many agents, not just this one. |
| **Root cause 1 — the sentence looked like the action** | `THE TOOL TURN` described the turn almost entirely as a *speech* instruction, and `PARALLEL EXECUTION` said *"the **platform** runs the backend call"* — attributing the call to the platform. Having produced the line, the model had satisfied everything the paragraph described. Nothing anywhere said a text-only turn was a failure, and there was no recovery path once it happened. |
| **Root cause 2 — a spoken claim became state** | These prompts use the transcript as their state machine. The transcript holds speech, not tool results, so *"I said I was registering"* was indistinguishable from *"the tool returned success"*. The intervening silence prompt broke tool-turn adjacency and made the two collapse. |
| **Root cause 3 — an ungated template at the point of use** | `Section 7`'s success sentence carried a fillable `[complaint number digit by digit in Hindi]` slot with **no gate attached**. The `ANTI-FABRICATION` clause that would have stopped it sat ~240 lines away inside the Tool 1 block and was never read at the moment it mattered. |
| **The fix** | In all **10** CRC complaint-capable agents: `PARALLEL EXECUTION` rewritten to attribute the call to Vaani (*"the platform does not call the tool for me"*); `THE TOOL TURN` retitled *"THE TOOL CALL IS THE POINT OF THIS TURN, NOT THE SENTENCE"* with an explicit **FAILED TURN** clause and a **RECOVERY** clause run on every turn after a registering line — if no Result came back, the call never fired, invoke it now, speak no number. The `GATE` sentence is now appended inline to **every** success template in every file (13 instances). |
| **Why not `preToolMessage`** | Considered and rejected: it would make the line structurally impossible to speak without the call, but on this platform it fires the tool *before* speaking, which loses the fill-the-silence purpose. |
| **Open platform question** | Whether the platform grants a consecutive agent turn without consumer input. If it does, a deliberate two-turn design (speak, then call) becomes viable and the recovery clause becomes the primary path. Until confirmed, same-turn is primary and recovery is the safety net. |
| **Model-capability caveat** | Whether the deployed model reliably emits text **and** a tool call in one turn is unverified. If it cannot, no prompt wording fixes this — an empirical co-emit test was recommended before relying on these clauses alone. |

### CPL-04 · Resolution ladder — a complaint is the last option, not the first (2026-08-03)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. **CRC only** this session; CC deliberately behind. |
| **The defect** | Client feedback from live calls: agents treat a complaint as the resolution to everything, including questions that have answers. Structural, not stylistic — **no prompt in either channel contained any instruction to attempt resolution before registering.** Every "before registering" clause in the repo was the duplicate guard. |
| **Compounding cause** | The reason list was the only classification vocabulary these prompts carried. An agent whose sole taxonomy is 18 complaint phrases reads every input as complaint-shaped. Fixed together with CPL-05. |
| **The fix** | A `RESOLUTION LADDER` block added above the protocol in all 10 agents: (1) is the query clear — if not, ask ONE clarifying question, never register against a query not understood; (2) can I resolve it from my own data or knowledge — if yes, do that and check whether it resolved; (3) does it belong to another domain — route, since being out of scope is never a reason to register; (4) only then, a complaint. |
| **The carve-out** | **A grievance about something that already happened** — cylinder not delivered, test not performed, staff behaviour, money already taken — skips steps 1–3 entirely. Nothing Vaani says undoes it, so the complaint *is* the correct resolution and must not be slowed down. The ladder governs **questions and requests for something to happen**, not grievances about what already did. |
| **`genericInfoComplaintAgent` had nothing to resolve with** | Its entire knowledge content was delivery timeline/methods/limits, while `Default` routes underweight, mechanic, stove/hotplate, Bharat Gas Mini and BAS queries to it — for which it had **no answer content at all**, making a complaint its only legal move. Section 4 reframed as *"MY RESOLUTION INVENTORY"* and given content for all five topics, sourced from `Default`'s knowledge base. |

### CPL-05 · Confirmation collapsed into the registering line; reason lists scoped per agent (2026-08-03)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. **CRC only** this session; CC deliberately behind. |
| **Three competing confirmation rules existed** | (A) the protocol's *"confirm the one-line summary exactly once"*, in all 10; (B) *"CONFIRM ONCE, ONLY WHAT IS NEW AND CONSEQUENTIAL… do not confirm things the consumer said plainly"* in `connectionServicesAgent`/`subsidyAgent`/`newConnectionAgent`; (C) *"I do not ask further questions. I move directly to confirmation"* in the `postDeliveryAgent`/`genericInfoComplaint` path catalogues. B directly contradicts A. That split is why some calls confirmed a summary and others registered straight away. |
| **The unified rule (client chose B + C)** | Never re-confirm what the consumer said plainly; confirm only what is genuinely new and consequential. When the issue is clear and a complaint is the right resolution, **no confirmation turn at all** — the one-line summary rides inside the registering line itself (*"आपकी शिकायत दर्ज कर रही हूँ कि सिलेंडर अभी तक नहीं आया — एक मिनट रुकिए।"*). No extra turn is spent, and the consumer still hears what is being filed and can correct it — which matters because STT mangles these calls heavily. |
| **Reason lists — three broken classes found** | **Correct:** `paymentAgent` (3 scoped phrases). **Dead list:** `subsidyAgent` carried all 18 delivery/staff phrases followed by a paragraph saying none of them apply — 18 unusable phrases biasing an agent toward delivery framing. **Dangling reference:** `bookingEligibleAgent`, `connectionServicesAgent`, `activeDeliveryAgent`, `notEligibleDeliveryAgent` pointed at *"my own domain reason list"* **which did not exist in the file**, while forbidding the agent to invent a phrase — no legal move. |
| **The fix** | Every agent now carries an explicit `REASON LIST — MINE ONLY`. Delivery-family and generic agents keep the 18; `subsidy`, `booking` (both), `connectionServices` and `notEligibleDelivery` get `others` only, with an explicit note that delivery/staff phrases are deliberately absent because they do not own those issues and route them instead. Client confirmed `others` is fully acceptable to the backend. |
| **Two invented reason phrases removed** | `connectionServicesAgent` instructed *"reason is a short English phrase that best matches the issue, e.g. 'connection not reactivated', 'surrender refund pending', 'transfer pending'"* — instructing invention, directly against the protocol. `activeDeliveryAgent` used `"unauthorized booking"`, which is not a backend phrase. Both now `others`. |
| **Empathy unified** | `subsidyAgent` and `connectionServicesAgent` **mandated** *"Empathy first for any problem (समझ सकती हूँ, माफी चाहूँगी)"* while 6 other agents **banned those exact phrases** — Vaani's warmth depended on which agent took the call. Client decision: **no empathy phrases in any specialist agent**; acknowledgement belongs to `Default` at the front of the call, and the consumer wants a resolution. Mandates removed from `subsidyAgent`, `connectionServicesAgent`, `newConnectionAgent_onHold`. |

### DEL-02 · The stale delivery record — a June record answering a July booking (2026-08-03)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. **CRC only** this session; CC deliberately behind. |
| **The defect** | Root cause of the reported call. The record showed a refill delivered `03-06-2026`; the consumer was asking about a booking made 26 July. `postDeliveryAgent` asserted the June delivery as the answer, and the consumer had to argue with it for four turns. **No agent in either channel had any rule for a record that predates what the consumer is describing.** |
| **The fix** | A `STALE RECORD` block in all 4 CRC delivery agents: if the consumer names a booking or delivery date **later** than the date in the record, the record is from an earlier cycle and does not describe the refill in question. Vaani does not assert delivery is complete, does not repeat the older date, and does not ask the consumer to accept it — she treats it as an open issue with no record on her side and registers a complaint for the refill actually described. |
| **Bounded deliberately** | The comparison is only ever between a date the consumer speaks and a date written in the record. No calculation, no day-counting, no invented comparison date — consistent with the existing `NEVER CALCULATE` and date-gate rules. If the consumer gives no date, the rule does not fire. |

### TRI-01 · `Default` skipped the booking/delivery confirmation question (2026-08-03)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. **CRC only** this session; CC deliberately behind. |
| **The defect** | On the reported call the consumer said *"मेरा surrender book है… आज तक deliver नहीं हुआ"* and `Default` routed straight to the delivery family without asking whether a booking confirmation was ever received. |
| **Root cause — two STAGE 2B rules contradicted each other** | `:321` gave an unconditional escape hatch (*"if their first utterance already makes the classification clear, skip the triage questions"*); `:358` required a confirmation question for exactly the booking/delivery overlap case. The permissive rule won. The question genuinely changes the destination — no confirmation received routes to `bookingEligibleAgent`, not delivery — so skipping it misrouted the call. |
| **The fix** | `:321`'s shortcut now carries an explicit, non-optional exception for the booking+delivery overlap, and `:358` says **ALWAYS** ask, *"never skip it on the grounds that the classification sounds clear, because it is precisely here that it is not."* |
| **Cylinder precedence (client decision)** | When an utterance carries a surrender/return word **and** a refill/booking/delivery signal together, it is a refill matter — go to STAGE 2B, do not spend the clarification, do not route to `connectionServicesAgent`. The `SURRENDER (CLARIFY FIRST)` rule now fires only when surrender is the **sole** subject. Rationale recorded in the prompt: consumers use "surrender" loosely and STT mangles it further, so a cylinder they are waiting for always outranks a word they may not have said. |

### QA-02 · `vaaniQA` taught the CPL-03/04/05 and DEL-02 rules (2026-08-03)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. **CRC only**; CC deliberately behind, in step with CPL-03/04/05 and DEL-02. |
| **Why** | Same lesson as QA-01 and TTS-02: a policy change that does not reach the reviewer means the reviewer passes the exact failure that prompted it. Before this, `vaaniQA` would have flagged the *fabricated complaint number* on the reported call and **missed the missing tool call that caused it** — and would have had nothing to say about a complaint filed in place of an answer, or a stale record asserted at a consumer. |
| **New rules** | **C3g** — the registering line must be followed on the very next agent turn by either the complaint number or the technical-failure line; a silence prompt, re-greeting, fresh question or topic change in between is the signature of a spoken line with no tool call, and is reported even if a number appears later. **C3h** — a complaint used in place of an answer Vaani was holding, or filed against a query she never clarified, is a violation; a *grievance about something that already happened* is explicitly never reportable. **C3i** — re-confirming what the consumer said plainly costs a turn and is a violation. **C3j** — standalone empathy sentences once she is working the issue, with the front-of-call acknowledgement and the A12 merged-sentence case carved out. |
| **Data rules** | **D9** added for the stale record (DEL-02), bounded to fire only when the consumer themselves spoke a date later than the record's. **D8 rewritten** — it listed *"a complaint"* among the things a consumer goes somewhere for, a leftover from the office-visit policy ESC-01 retired; it now states plainly that a complaint is never a journey. |
| **Every new rule is two-sided** | Each carries its own never-reportable half, per the QA-01 finding that one-sided rules generate false positives faster than they catch real ones. |
| **Not changed** | `postCallAnalysisFlat` (describes the call, does not judge the agent) and `postCallAnalysisHuman` — see the open question below. |

### NUM-01 · One long-number speaking + repeat protocol across CRC; English digit words become the CRC default (2026-08-03)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth deliberately split**. CRC only; CC untouched and now behind. Client decision, 2026-08-03. |
| **Problem** | CRC carried **three** incompatible number styles. `getConsumerDetails` mandated English digit words with `" - "` between each and a full repeat protocol; the booking family gave Hindi digit-word examples with no separators (`bookingEligibleAgent.txt:46`); the delivery/payment/generic family said only *"digit by digit slowly"* with no language and no separator. Worse, **no agent outside `getConsumerDetails` had any repeat-request handling at all** — a consumer who missed a complaint number got it re-read at the same speed, or not at all. |
| **Change** | A single `LONG NUMBER DELIVERY` block, identical in wording (first/second person adjusted), now sits in **12 live CRC prompts**. It governs every long number: complaint number, `{{crcOfficeNumber}}`, `{{ConsumerDetailsDistMobileNumber1}}`/`2`, the S M S / missed-call and WhatsApp booking numbers. |
| **First delivery** | The **whole number in one go**, one turn. Deliberately *not* the `getConsumerDetails` dictation — no pen-and-paper preamble, no piece-splitting, no "did you note it". Volunteering the dictation unasked is now itself a violation (`vaaniQA` C5f). |
| **On a repeat request** | *Then* the `getConsumerDetails` C3 behaviours apply, unchanged: three pieces (3-3-4), one piece and one check question per turn, missed piece repeated alone and never a restart, "कितना note हुआ?" → only the digits already given, "धीरे" → two per turn, wrong read-back → only the wrong digit corrected, no cap on repeats. |
| **Digit language — the split** | **English digit words with `" - "` are now the CRC default for every long number, including the complaint number.** This overrides the shared-truth line in `CLAUDE.md:155`, `bpcl_showroom_crc/CLAUDE.md:44` and **ESC-02** below, which pinned complaint numbers to Hindi words. Hindi digit words remain correct on consumer preference ('6' → "छह"), and Hindi words remain the rule for counts, money, dates and durations — this is a long-number rule only. **CC keeps Hindi words** in its 10 complaint agents. The two channels now differ on this; that is intended, not drift. |
| **Not ported** | `emergencyAgent` — fully carved out; 1906 is four digits, already English, and a hazard call is never slowed. `routingAgent` — a silent re-router that speaks no numbers. `getConsumerDetails` — the source; its stricter dictation closing is unchanged. `newConnectionAgent.txt` — PARKED (NC-01). |
| **QA prompts** | `vaaniQA` gains **C5f** (first delivery whole, repeat protocol required once asked, A10 suspended for that repetition). Its A7/line-75 anchor already accepted Hindi *or* English digit words, so the language change needed no QA edit. |

### NUM-02 · `{{crcOfficeNumber}}` extended channel-wide, on explicit request only (2026-08-03)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Escalation (CRC-only variable; CC has no physical-office concept — D-05, GCD-03) |
| **Change** | `{{crcOfficeNumber}}` is no longer `getConsumerDetails`-only. **Every live CRC agent except `Default` now holds it.** This supersedes the "used in `getConsumerDetails` only" line in GCD-03 and in `bpcl_showroom_crc/CLAUDE.md`. |
| **The rule — deliberately narrow** | It is spoken **only when the consumer explicitly asks for a number to call** — "कोई number दीजिए", "कहाँ call करूँ?", "आपके office का number क्या है?". It is **never volunteered** and is **never the answer to a problem**. **One exception, added 04-08-2026 (CPL-07): after a FAILED `bpcl_create_complaint` it is volunteered and dictated.** |
| **ESC-01 is unchanged** | A consumer asking for a human, a person, a senior, a specialist or a callback still gets a **registered complaint**. The number does not replace it, shorten it, or precede it. Offering the number *instead of* registering is a violation (`vaaniQA` C5e). The consumer may be given the number as well, or after the complaint is registered, if they ask for one. |
| **Which number** | Their own distributor's number remains `{{ConsumerDetailsDistMobileNumber1}}`; a request to reach *us* / this office / a person here is `{{crcOfficeNumber}}`. |
| **`Default` excluded** | DATA-01 — `Default` runs before the data gate and holds no consumer-specific data. It gets the `LONG NUMBER DELIVERY` block (language + repeat rules) but **no number and no variable**, and continues to route escalations to `genericInfoComplaintAgent` via STAGE 3. |
| **`newConnectionAgent_onHold` included, with a fence** | It has no complaint tool and no escalation (NC-01), so it is the one agent where a consumer asking for a number otherwise gets nothing. It may give the number on request, but **giving it is not an escalation**: no person, callback, complaint or office visit may be promised with it, and it is never offered as a route around the connection hold. |
| **Uninjected** | Visible curly braces, empty, or a placeholder = no number. Never spoken, never invented; Vaani says she has no number to give and carries on. Same guard as GCD-03. |
| **QA prompts** | `vaaniQA` line 38 rewritten (the variable is no longer scoped to one agent) and **C5e** added. C5a–C5d still describe the `getConsumerDetails` *dictation* closing, which is unaffected; C5d's "anywhere outside this closing" clause is now explicitly fenced to that agent. |
| **Open** | Platform must inject `{{crcOfficeNumber}}` for **every** agent, not just `getConsumerDetails`. Still unconfirmed with the platform team — until it lands, every agent takes the missing-value path, which is silence about the number. |

### PCA-01 · Post-call prompts move off `{{system.current_*}}` to explicit call timestamps — CRC only (2026-08-03)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. **CRC only**; CC deliberately behind, in step with NC-01, ZIP-01 and QA-02. |
| **Change** | All three CRC post-call prompts — `postCallAnalysisFlat.txt`, `postCallAnalysisHuman.txt`, `vaaniQA.txt` — replace `{{system.current_date}}` / `{{system.current_time}}` with four explicit variables: **`{{callStartDate}}`, `{{callStartTime}}`, `{{callEndDate}}`, `{{callEndTime}}`**. All four are IST, `YYYY-MM-DD` and `HH:MM:SS` respectively, used exactly as given with no UTC conversion and no reformatting. |
| **Why** | `{{system.current_date}}` was never the call's date — it was the date the *analyser* ran, and the prompts papered over the gap with the assertion *"this analysis runs immediately after the call ends, so this IS the date the call happened."* That assertion silently fails on any delayed, retried or backfilled analysis run, and every relative date the consumer spoke ("कल", "do din pehle") would resolve against the wrong day. The new variables state the fact instead of inferring it, so that sentence is deleted. |
| **`{{callStartDate}}` is the anchor** | Every relative date either party spoke resolves against `{{callStartDate}}`, never `{{callEndDate}}` and never the analyser's own date. This is stated once in each file. `{{callEndDate}}` differs from `{{callStartDate}}` only on a call that ran across midnight — the one case the old single-date model could not express at all. |
| **Absent values** | Empty, the word `null`, or visible curly braces = absent. Never guessed, never substituted with the current date. Same guard as everywhere else in the repo. |
| **Nothing else changed** | No field, rubric, checkpoint or JSON shape was touched — `postCallAnalysisFlat`'s flat output and `vaaniQA`'s / `postCallAnalysisHuman`'s nested objects are byte-identical in structure, so the monitoring that reads them is unaffected. `vaaniQA` D3 still contrasts the DD-MM-YYYY dates inside `customerStatus` against the YYYY-MM-DD call date, and remains correct. |
| **CC gap — same shape, not yet ported** | CC holds the identical defect in **three** files: `postCallAnalysisFlat.txt` (`:5`, `:6`, `:104`, `:107`), `postCallAnalysis.txt` (`:5`, `:6`, `:224`, `:227`, `:234`) and `promptQA.txt` (`:37`, `:38`). Note CC's `postCallAnalysis.txt` carries an **extra** usage at `:234` with no CRC counterpart. Porting is mechanical once the platform confirms it injects the four variables on the CC side too. |
| **Open** | Platform must inject `{{callStartDate}}` / `{{callStartTime}}` / `{{callEndDate}}` / `{{callEndTime}}` into the post-call context. Unconfirmed with the platform team at time of writing — until it lands, all four read as absent and every relative-date resolution degrades to "do not guess" rather than to a wrong date. |

### WGT-01 · Underweight cylinder becomes verify-first; the complaint is never offered — CRC only (2026-08-04)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. **CRC only**; CC deliberately behind by client decision, in step with NC-01, ZIP-01, QA-02 and PCA-01. |
| **Was** | Every agent owning the `Less weight` reason registered a complaint immediately, with an explicit *"I do not ask further questions"*. The one partial exception was `genericInfoComplaint`'s KB entry, which gave the 14.2 kg formula and then still ended in a complaint. No prompt in either channel held a remedy. |
| **Now** | One canonical **UNDERWEIGHT CYLINDER — I VERIFY, I DO NOT REGISTER** block. Turn 1 gives the standard as a *usual* figure and asks the single question: *"क्या आपने distributor के पास जाकर cylinder तुलवाया था और वजन कम निकला?"* **Yes** → replacing a short refill with a correct-weight one is the distributor's responsibility. **No** → take the refill to the distributor office to be weighed; if short it is replaced there. Vaani stops after either line. |
| **The complaint is never offered** | Registration happens only when the consumer takes it there: they already went and the distributor refused or did not replace it, they say they cannot go and ask her to register, or they ask for a complaint outright. `Reason: Less weight` unchanged; `feedbackDescription` now carries what was verified and what the distributor did. |
| **Carve-out inverted, deliberately** | Underweight leaves the CPL-04 grievance carve-out. A short refill still has a remedy, so it is not a "nothing said undoes it" grievance. **A weight test not performed at delivery is unaffected** and still registers directly — that one has no remedy. Both channels' analysers still map "cylinder ka weight kam hai" → `Less weight`; no reason string changed. |
| **A fourth CRC physical-action carve-out** | Naming the distributor counter is normally an ESC-01 violation. Weighing joins hotplate purchase and KYC submission: a complaint cannot put a cylinder on a scale. `vaaniQA` C3e now lists four carve-outs, and `genericInfoComplaint`'s PHYSICAL-ACTION CARVE-OUT lists three. |
| **Files** | `postDeliveryAgent`, `activeDeliveryAgent`, `eligibleDeliveryAgent`, `genericInfoComplaint` (KB entry + Section 7 path, with recurring-quality narrowed to leakage/bad quality), `Default` (MODULE E + the weight FAQ, answer-only — it holds no complaint tool), `vaaniQA` (C3e, C3h, PART B). |
| **CC gap** | All four CC files still register directly: `postDeliveryAgent:224`, `eligibleDeliveryAgent:97`, `genericInfoComplaintAgent:246`, plus the `Default` FAQ. Porting is mechanical — the block needs no CC translation, since it names the distributor, not a CRC office. |

### WGT-02 · Underweight verify-first was silently losable to the complaint-follow-up shortcut — CRC only (2026-08-07)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**, direct follow-up to WGT-01. **CRC only**; CC gap from WGT-01 still open, this compounds it. |
| **Source** | Live CRC transcript (client-reported): consumer opens with *"मैंने अभी दो दिन पहले complaint कराई थी... ढाई किलो gas कम थी"* — in one breath naming both "already complained" and "underweight." Routed `Default → activeDeliveryAgent →` (platform, on backend refill-delivered state) `→ postDeliveryAgent`, which registered a complaint immediately with no weigh-first question at all. |
| **Not a routing bug** | `Default`'s route was correct — underweight is explicitly a delivery-family topic (STAGE 2B example 7b), and the two-hop `activeDeliveryAgent → postDeliveryAgent` hand-off is the documented platform behavior for a family agent resolved by backend state (agent-workflow.md §11.4), not a leaf-to-leaf violation. `postDeliveryAgent` also had the WGT-01 verify-first KB block in full. |
| **The actual gap** | Two special-case paths in the same file both claimed this utterance and neither named the other. WGT-01's underweight block already overrides the CPL-04 grievance carve-out (a short refill has a remedy) but said nothing about **PATH 4's "complaint follow-up"** case, added later, which fast-paths straight to registering a fresh complaint for *any* topic once the consumer frames it as "I already complained, nothing happened" — including underweight, where "already complained" says nothing about whether the consumer ever tried the distributor-reweigh remedy the whole WGT-01 block exists to offer. With both paths matching and neither yielding, the model picked PATH 4 and the verify-first question never ran. |
| **The fix** | Underweight named as an explicit exception on both sides of the conflict, in `postDeliveryAgent` and `genericInfoComplaintAgent` (the only two files with both blocks): the underweight block now states it also overrides PATH 4's shortcut, and PATH 4 now points back to the underweight block for that topic. The verify-first question still runs even when the consumer says a complaint was already filed — UNLESS their own words already answer it in this call (they say outright they already weighed it and the distributor refused/did not replace it), in which case registering directly is correct and unchanged. |
| **Files (2)** | `postDeliveryAgent`, `genericInfoComplaintAgent`. `activeDeliveryAgent` and `eligibleDeliveryAgent` hold the underweight block but no "complaint follow-up" path, so the conflict does not exist there. |
| **⚠️ Open** | CC has neither block (WGT-01 gap), so this specific conflict cannot exist there yet — but will need the same two-sided fix the moment WGT-01 is ported. |

---

### PER-01 · CRC persona reversed — Vaani is at a regional head office and the distributor IS a third party — CRC only (2026-08-05)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | **Axis 2 — persona positioning.** A straight reversal of the CRC half of it. CC unaffected: it already treated the distributor as a third party, so this narrows the gap between the channels rather than widening it. |
| **What was wrong** | Every CRC identity block asserted Vaani was an insider **of the consumer's own distributor office** and explicitly banned `"आपके distributor"`. Client states this was never true: she calls from a **head CRC office for an area/region covering multiple districts and multiple distributors**. |
| **What is true now** | She is an insider of **भारत पेट्रोलियम**, not of the consumer's distributor. **`"अपने distributor से पूछ लीजिए"` is correct and expected.** `"हमारे यहाँ"` / `"हमारा office"` now refer to **this CRC only**. |
| **Escalation untouched** | ESC-01 stands in full. Pointing at a distributor means a **phone enquiry**; telling a consumer to **travel** is still forbidden, and an unresolved problem is still a registered complaint. The multi-district territory is exactly why. |
| **Behavioural consequence** | The **hotplate/stove purchase** and **KYC document submission** carve-outs now point at the consumer's **own distributor**, not this CRC — a regional head office does not sell hotplates or take documents over a counter. |
| **QA flipped** | `vaaniQA` **C8 RETIRED** — it scored `"आपके distributor"` as a violation and would now have failed Vaani for correct behaviour. **C7** rewritten to say third-party framing is correct. The C8 cross-references in **C3-NA** and **C3f** were cleared in the same edit. |
| **CS-01 moot** | The earlier "third-party phrasing leak" fix in `connectionServicesAgent` was correcting something that was never wrong. Its factual-naming rewrite is still fine; nothing is reverted. |
| **Files touched (8)** | `Default`, `subsidyAgent`, `genericInfoComplaintAgent`, `newConnectionAgent_onHold`, `vaaniQA`, plus `CLAUDE.md`, `bpcl_showroom_crc/CLAUDE.md` and this ledger. `newConnectionAgent.txt` is **PARKED and was deliberately not edited** — it still carries the old persona text and must be reconciled if it is ever unparked. |
| **⚠️ Sequencing** | Landed as a **separate pass after ZIP-02 in the same session**, deliberately, so a live-call regression can be attributed to one change or the other. |

### ZIP-02 · New Mini/ZIP knowledge base; ZIP becomes an offered product, not a connection — CRC only (2026-08-05)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. **CRC only**; CC deliberately behind, in step with NC-01, ZIP-01, QA-02, PCA-01 and RFL-01. |
| **Supersedes** | The whole of **ZIP-01's** "offered in one agent only" rule, its "deliberately not asserted" row, and most of its soft-unknown list. Also amends **RFL-01**, whose Mini-only alternative is retired. |
| **ZIP is a PRODUCT, not a connection** | The load-bearing reframe. भारत गैस लाइट ज़िप is a **10 kg composite cylinder** anyone may take when they need gas urgently — including alongside a domestic connection they already hold. It is no longer described as "instant new connection + express delivery", and wanting one is **no longer a new-connection intent**. |
| **Routing changed** | `Default` answers "I want a ZIP" **inline** with the Hello BPCL App steps instead of routing to `newConnectionAgent`. `routingAgent`'s three-way ZIP split collapses to **two**: wants-one-or-asking-what-it-is → `genericInfoComplaintAgent` (which now owns the ZIP KB); problem-with-a-ZIP-they-hold → the agent owning that problem. `newConnectionAgent_onHold` keeps full ZIP handling and no longer routes a ZIP enquiry out. |
| **ZIP is now OFFERED, with Mini, at three moments** | Mini **and** ZIP together in one turn, consumer chooses, only the chosen one explained on the next turn: (1) the NC-01 new-connection hold, (2) the RFL-01 extra-refill/second-cylinder block, (3) a booking the consumer is not eligible to make. Benefits lead; **price is never volunteered**. Offered **once** per call. For an **Ujjwala/PMUY** caller Mini is named **first** — client-agreed, these are the most price-sensitive callers. |
| **Never offered against a complaint** | Client-agreed carve-out. A late delivery, faulty cylinder, money taken, or a missed four-hour ZIP window gets the **complaint registered first**, exactly as today; the offer may follow **only** if the consumer is still without gas and still not satisfied. Offering a paid product to someone angry their cylinder never arrived reads as selling instead of fixing. |
| **The facts now held** | 10 kg only (no 5 kg ZIP). Empty cylinder is a **one-time purchase, not a deposit** — spoken *"दो हजार सात सौ रुपये प्लस अठारह परसेंट G S T"*. Gas on top; regulator (₹250) and hose bought separately, same as a normal connection. GST on all products and payment transactions. **No refund** on returning the cylinder or on cancellation — but a **failed or double payment is still refunded in 3–7 working days** (client-confirmed carve-out; without it `paymentAgent`'s blanket refund rule would have contradicted the KB). ID proof only: Aadhaar, Passport, PAN, Voter ID, DL, or any Government-issued ID. Domestic **2 ZIP cylinders/month**, commercial **10** — **entirely separate from the 14.2 kg quota**, so a regular refill and a ZIP may be booked the same day, and there is **no booking gap on ZIP**. Regular domestic valve, regulator and stove work with ZIP **and** Mini. Express is free; **four hours stays a standard, never a promise** (ZIP-01 rule preserved). |
| **Add vs switch** | An existing consumer **may take** a ZIP alongside their connection; they **may not switch, convert or exchange** their existing connection into one. ZIP-01's "never asserted" row is retired — Vaani now answers both halves. No steel-cylinder exchange. |
| **Bharat Gas Lite** | The Lite-is-the-cylinder / ZIP-is-the-package framing is **deleted everywhere**. भारत गैस लाइट was a **domestic connection and is now closed**. Vaani treats "लाइट" as ZIP unless the consumer clearly means Lite, then states plainly that they are two different things. |
| **Bharat Gas Mini restated** | A **5 kg STEEL** cylinder and a **Free Trade LPG product**, not a domestic connection — anyone may take it even holding one. Previously described only as a retail 5 kg option. |
| **Availability** | Consumer checks it themselves on the **Hello BPCL App by PIN code**; if they would rather not use the app, they phone their distributor. The never-promise-availability hedge is unchanged and still rides with every mention. |
| **A fourth website** | Refill price is **never quoted**. Vaani walks the consumer to **`w - w - w - dot - commercial - l - p - g - dot - in`** → "More" dropdown → "LPG Prices" → State/District/Area → **"LPG 10 KG FTL COMPOSITE REFILL FILLED"**. Delivered 2–3 parts at a time with confirm-and-continue, per the existing URL rule. ⚠️ **`vaaniQA` previously scored naming any address outside the known three as a violation** — the new address was added to its allowed list in the same edit, or QA would have failed Vaani for correct behaviour. |
| **Payment splits by channel** | App → paid **online**; IVRS → **cash on delivery**; distributor → **paid to the distributor when the refill is taken**. Relevant to `paymentAgent`: money asked for at the door on a ZIP order can be legitimate. |
| **Complaint cap unchanged** | The new KB's "multiple cases can be created for each issue" was **confirmed by the client to mean backend capability, not a licence to file more** — the max **2 per call** rule stands untouched in all ten complaint-capable agents. |
| **Files touched (15)** | `Default`, `routingAgent`, `newConnectionAgent_onHold`, the four delivery agents, both booking agents, `paymentAgent`, `subsidyAgent`, `connectionServicesAgent`, `genericInfoComplaintAgent`, `vaaniQA`, plus this ledger. |
| **Still unknown** | The exact refill price, warranty and damage cover, and which cities ZIP has reached. Only these three keep the soft-unknown line; the rest of ZIP-01's deflection list is retired, and deflecting a ZIP question Vaani now holds is itself a miss. |

### ZIP-03 · Lite ZIP KB refreshed against the business team's reviewed FAQ — CRC only (2026-08-17)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. **CRC only**; CC deliberately behind, in step with NC-01, ZIP-01, ZIP-02, QA-02, PCA-01 and RFL-01. |
| **Source** | *HelloBPCL — LPG FAQ, Bharatgas Lite ZIP v2, post business team review* (Z.1–Z.16). Amends **ZIP-02's** "facts now held", "availability" and "payment splits" rows; everything ZIP-02 said about routing, when ZIP is offered, the never-against-a-complaint carve-out, add-vs-switch, and the four-hour standard is **unchanged**. |
| **ZIP is domestic-only — commercial references deleted** | Client-confirmed in this round, closing the FAQ's own open item. The **commercial 10-cylinder limit is gone** from every prompt and from both analysers, and no ZIP answer references a commercial customer. `w - w - w - dot - commercial - l - p - g - dot - in` **stays** — it is the site that carries the price table, not a statement about who may buy. |
| **The app comes first, the website is the fallback** | Every ZIP lookup — availability, cylinder price, refill price — now leads with the **Hello BPCL App** and encourages the consumer to download it: **"Bharatgas for Home" → "Explore Bharatgas Products" → PIN code**. The website walk is offered **only** where the consumer is not comfortable with the app, and **one route or the other, never both in the same breath**. **Only that one URL** is ever given for a ZIP refill price — no mixing in `mylpg.in` or the भारत पेट्रोलियम site. |
| **IVRS is removed as a ZIP booking channel** | Booking is the **app or the distributor**, nothing else. The IVRS cash-on-delivery split goes with it. This is a ZIP-only removal — IVRS remains a valid booking channel for the regular 14.2 kg refill everywhere it already appears. |
| **Advance payment** | Payment is **completed before the cylinder is handed over**: app → paid online, in advance; distributor → paid when the refill is taken. **There is no cash on delivery on a ZIP.** Supersedes ZIP-02's three-way payment split. |
| **Cylinder limit** | Domestic **2 a month** (unchanged) **and 2 held at a time** — the at-a-time figure is spoken **only if the consumer asks**, so the common answer stays one number long. |
| **ID proof** | Now **Aadhaar, PAN, Driving Licence, Ration Card, or Passport**. Ration Card added per the FAQ; **Voter ID dropped** per client instruction, and the *"or any Government-issued ID"* catch-all dropped with it — keeping it would have re-admitted the Voter ID that was just removed. Aadhaar is kept, against the FAQ's own list, on client instruction. |
| **Two facts added** | The cylinder carries the **ISI certification mark**, so no extra equipment or setup is needed; and **anyone may apply — there is no eligibility condition.** |
| **Cylinder price hedged** | ₹2,700 + 18% GST is unchanged, but the price **can differ a little by area** and the consumer can check theirs on the app by PIN code. |
| **"Instant new connection" NOT adopted** | The reviewed FAQ's Z.1/Z.2 reintroduce *"instant new connection"* wording. **Deliberately not ported** — ZIP-02's product-not-a-connection reframe is what drives the routing, and re-admitting that phrase would quietly undo it. Flagged back to BA. |
| **`paymentAgent` self-contradiction fixed** | Its `ZIP AND MONEY` line still claimed Vaani holds no ZIP payment methods, advance-payment requirement, or refund terms — a ZIP-01 leftover that this change would have made a live contradiction, since the same file now states all three. Rewritten to say what she **does** hold; the exact refill price stays the one money gap. |
| **Files touched (16)** | `Default`, `newConnectionAgent_onHold`, `unregisteredComplaintAgent`, the four delivery agents, both booking agents, `paymentAgent`, `subsidyAgent`, `connectionServicesAgent`, `genericInfoComplaintAgent`, `vaaniQA`, `postCallAnalysisHuman`, plus this ledger. `routingAgent` and `postCallAnalysisFlat` hold no ZIP facts and were not touched. |
| **QA side** | `vaaniQA`'s allowed-facts block carries every changed fact, or it would report the new correct answers as invented — and it now knows the app navigation is **required** behaviour and not an office-visit escalation. `postCallAnalysisHuman` §3.5's held-facts list is updated in step. |
| **Still unknown, unchanged** | The exact refill price, warranty and damage cover, and which cities ZIP has reached. The soft-unknown line stays fenced to ZIP. |
| **Open with BA** | Whether the FAQ's ID list is exhaustive or illustrative (it drops Aadhaar, which we kept); and the Z.1/Z.2 "instant new connection" wording above. Neither blocks this change. |

### ZIP-04 · Lite ZIP merged with the standalone `litezipAgent` KB; the App walkthrough lands in every ZIP-bearing agent — CRC only (2026-09-09)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. **CRC only**; CC deliberately behind, in step with NC-01, ZIP-01, ZIP-02, ZIP-03, QA-02, PCA-01 and RFL-01. |
| **Source** | [`bpcl_lite_zip/litezipAgent.txt`](bpcl_lite_zip/litezipAgent.txt) — a 1,496-line standalone, no-tools, multilingual agent for whom Lite ZIP is the whole call. Its BLOCK 5 is **Z1–Z16**, the *same* reviewed FAQ ZIP-03 was built from: two independent readings of one document. This row records which reading won on each conflict. **Client ruled the standalone wins on facts**; CRC wins on channel layer. |
| **What was NOT ported** | The standalone's channel layer, all of it: no-tools operation, multilingual output, helpline **1800224344** as an exit, its inline safety beats, BLOCKS 0–4/7/8/10-fallback/11/12 and its twenty few-shots. CRC keeps Hindi-only, **1906 → `emergencyAgent`**, the complaint protocol, `switchagent`, and ESC-01's no-office-visit rule. A live gas hazard now explicitly outranks the ZIP walkthrough in every block. |
| **Sizes — ZIP is TWO sizes** | **दस किलो and पाँच किलो.** Reverses ZIP-02's "10 kg only (no 5 kg ZIP)". Bharat Gas Mini stays a **separate product on the same App screen**, in दो किलो and पाँच किलो, and its sizes are never called ZIP sizes. Carried with Z4's two guards: never promise a size is in stock (per-distributor, unknowable from here), and **never say a size does not exist** — "out of stock" and "does not exist" are different things. |
| **No ZIP amount is ever spoken** | The hardest reversal. **₹2,700 + 18% GST and the ₹250 regulator are deleted from every prompt and both analysers**, along with ZIP-02's "money facts she may state". No ZIP figure is approved: not cylinder, not regulator, not refill. Vaani says the price depends on city, distributor and size, and that the **full GST-inclusive, item-wise bill appears on screen in the App when a cylinder is selected, before payment**; pushed a second time she holds the line warmly rather than guessing, and she never repeats back an amount the consumer reads out. The non-numeric money facts survive — one-time purchase not a deposit, no refund on return or cancellation, failed/double payment still refunded in 3–7 working days. |
| **The website is gone** | `w - w - w - dot - commercial - l - p - g - dot - in` is **deleted everywhere** — the ZIP-03 refill-price walk, `newConnectionAgent_onHold` §14's allowed-address list, the pronunciation-rule address lists in `connectionServicesAgent` and `subsidyAgent`, and `vaaniQA`'s correct-address list (whose "these three" count is now accurate). **The App is the only place a ZIP price is ever looked up.** |
| **Four hours KEPT, night rule ADDED** | ZIP-01's rule survives intact: चार घंटे is a **product standard, never a promise** about one delivery, and a missed window on a consumer-confirmed ZIP is still a genuine service failure through the normal complaint path. The standalone's blanket ban was **not** adopted. **New from Z5:** an order placed शाम छह बजे के बाद सुबह छह बजे तक is delivered the **next day, सुबह आठ से रात आठ**, and the App shows a checkbox at that time which must be ticked or the payment button will not activate. |
| **Who can take it — commercial and industrial are back** | Reverses ZIP-03's domestic-only deletion. Z16: the App's Consumer Type field carries **Domestic, Commercial and Industrial** as three working options, and Vaani never tells a commercial or industrial caller ZIP is not for them. Limits restored per ZIP-02 and extended: **domestic 2/month and 2 held at a time, commercial 10, industrial the same as commercial** — spoken only if asked. Still entirely outside the 14.2 kg quota; still no booking gap. |
| **Anyone may take ZIP or Mini — but the spoken half follows the audience** | Client clarification, 2026-09-09, in two passes. **The rule** is universal and sits in every ZIP block: an existing consumer and someone with **no LPG connection at all** may both take a ZIP or a Mini, the one-household-one-connection rule does not apply, Vaani never asks whether they have a connection, and never treats not having one as a blocker. **The spoken line is not universal**, because the audiences differ. `getConsumerDetails` gates the call — a fetch returning no data goes to the NO-DATA CLOSING rather than routing on — so **every caller reaching a specialist has been identified and holds a connection**, and all eleven specialists (the four delivery agents, both booking agents, `paymentAgent`, `subsidyAgent`, `connectionServicesAgent`, `genericInfoComplaintAgent`) speak only the existing-consumer half: *"इसे आप अपने existing connection के साथ भी ले सकते हैं।"* They do not volunteer the no-connection half — it is not that caller's situation, and offered unprompted it reads as Vaani not having looked at the record. `newConnectionAgent_onHold` **leads with the opposite half** — *"इसके लिए connection की ज़रूरत नहीं है, कोई भी ले सकता है।"* — because its caller has just been told new connections are closed. `Default` carries **both halves and picks by what the consumer has said**, since it holds no consumer-specific data and genuinely cannot tell who it is talking to; it never asks and never assumes. The add-vs-switch rule is untouched: an existing consumer may **take** a ZIP, never **switch** into one. |
| **Subsidy — CRC wins** | The standalone's G6 ("you do not know whether subsidy applies") was **rejected**. ZIP is Free Trade LPG: **no subsidy, no DBTL, no PAHAL**, said plainly. This is `subsidyAgent`'s one ZIP fact and it does not become an unknown. |
| **Documents** | Adopted from Z8. Documents are **not uploaded in advance** — only the ID proof **category and number** are typed into the App, and the **physical document is shown to the delivery person at delivery**, matching what was entered; there is an acknowledgement to tick for it. The **App shows the list** (Passport, Aadhaar, Driving Licence, Voter ID and the like) and Vaani **never recites one document as though it were the only accepted one** — this closes ZIP-03's "Open with BA" row on whether the ID list is exhaustive. ZIP-02's fixed five-ID list is retired. |
| **Connection wording admitted, routing untouched** | Vaani **may** now say the connection comes immediately — no seven-day wait, no KYC de-duplication wait — and that the cylinder reaches home the same day, and may name the App's **"New Connection"** option. This reverses ZIP-03's "instant new connection NOT adopted" row **at the wording level only**. Everything ZIP-02 built on top of the product framing **stands**: ZIP is exempt from the NC-01 hold, never gets the hold message, and a ZIP enquiry is **never routed out as a new-connection intent**. `Default`, `routingAgent` and `newConnectionAgent_onHold` triage are unchanged. |
| **The App walkthrough — an 11-step ladder, in every ZIP-bearing agent** | Client chose the full ladder over a two-agent split. Compressed from the standalone's ~550-line state machine to **one paragraph, one step per turn**: App → "Bharatgas for Home" → "Explore Bharatgas Products" → भारत गैस लाइट ज़िप; register/login (an **already-registered, logged-in consumer finds details and address pre-filled** — Z13); consumer details; delivery address (**the App checks serviceability itself**); distributor; cylinder and size; **Refill vs New Connection**; regulator; order summary; payment; confirmation. Entered only after the consumer says they want to go ahead. |
| **Refill vs New Connection — the default is New Connection** | Client-specified. Z6 calls this the most misunderstood step. Vaani **assumes New Connection and proceeds on that basis until the consumer says otherwise**, asking exactly one question: *"क्या आपके पास इसी तरह का खाली सिलेंडर है exchange करने के लिए?"* — नहीं → New Connection (cylinder **and** gas included); हाँ → Refill Cylinder (the delivery person takes the empty one). |
| **Regulator, and what arrives after payment** | Z7: the regulator appears under **"Add Ons" on the New Connection path only** — Refill never shows it — it is ISI-marked, and a consumer without one needs one. Z11: after payment the screen carries the **order number and the distributor's name, address and phone**, and an **SMS brings the order number plus an OTP the consumer must give the delivery person**. Distinct from the existing 14.2 kg delivery OTP; both are real. |
| **The three dead ends** | New to CRC — nothing covered these before. (1) **Not available in their area**: new cities keep being added, check the App again in a few days. Never suggest another PIN code, a relative's address, a launch date, or an office. (2) **Everything out of stock**: stock is per-distributor, so **one** retry with a different distributor, then stop. Never promise when stock returns. (3) **No smartphone**: the family-member route — whoever will *use* the connection supplies the details, because that ID is shown at delivery, and the SMS and OTP reach whichever number is logged into the App. If no smartphone exists at all, say so plainly. **Never** promise an SMS, IVRS, missed-call or counter route for ZIP; never offer to book it for her. |
| **Unknowns shrink to two** | **Warranty and damage cover, and which cities ZIP has reached.** The exact refill price leaves the list — it is no longer an unknown but a *never-spoken*, answered by pointing at the App. The ZIP-only softer line is otherwise unchanged, including "संपर्क कीजिए"/"पूछ लीजिए" and never "जाकर पूछिए". |
| **Stale ZIP-01/RFL-01 leftovers cleared in the same pass** | Four contradictions that predate this change and would have become live ones: *"I do NOT offer भारत गैस लाइट ज़िप here"* sitting four lines below *"I OFFER MINI AND ZIP ONCE, in the same turn"* in `eligibleDeliveryAgent`, `notEligibleDeliveryAgent` and `bookingNonEligibilityAgent`; the three *"whether an existing consumer can take ZIP is NOT yet confirmed"* trailers in `connectionServicesAgent`, `bookingEligibleAgent` and `bookingNonEligibilityAgent`, which ZIP-02 had already resolved; `newConnectionAgent_onHold`'s *"ZIP … is the one new-connection route still open"*; and `vaaniQA` still reporting an offer of ZIP against the extra-refill block as a violation when ZIP-02 made it required. |
| **Two canonical blocks, byte-identical** | The 11 live agents share exactly two blocks — a **22-line** specialist block (`paymentAgent`, `subsidyAgent`, `genericInfoComplaintAgent`, `connectionServicesAgent`, both booking agents) and a **23-line** delivery variant (the four delivery agents) that adds only the not-confirmed-ZIP clause. `Default` MODULE Z and `newConnectionAgent_onHold` §13-Z carry the same content in imperative voice. Verified identical after the edit — a future drift check is one `md5sum`. |
| **`ZIP` → `zip` in every prompt — a live TTS bug, not cosmetics** | **RULE 1** in the pronunciation block reads *"Abbreviations (<5 letters, uppercase): letter-by-letter, spaces, no dots — L P G, K Y C, S V…"*. **"ZIP" is three letters and uppercase, so the rule told the model to say "Z I P".** All **719** Roman occurrences across the sixteen CRC prompts are now lower-case `zip` (including `Mini-and-zip` and `non-zip`); ledger and channel-contract prose keep `ZIP-01…ZIP-04` as row identifiers, which are never read aloud. Two defences were added so this cannot regress: RULE 1 in all four files that carry it now names the exception outright, and the spoken-name line in **all twelve** zip-bearing agents carries *"zip" IS A WORD, NOT AN ABBREVIATION — never spelled out letter by letter as "Z I P"*. The Roman form is banned from being spoken at all in any casing; the spoken name stays the Devanagari **"भारत गैस लाइट ज़िप"**. The pre-existing bans on **टंकी** (14 files) and bare **BPCL** (~19 files) are untouched and were re-verified after the pass. |
| **Extra-refill prose caught in the final audit** | Two agents still described the RFL-01 extra-refill answer as *"the block plus Bharat Gas Mini"* / *"and offer Bharat Gas Mini"* while the **quoted script directly beneath offered both products** — `connectionServicesAgent` C8 and `bookingNonEligibilityAgent`'s declaration-form answer. Prose and script now both say Mini **and** भारत गैस लाइट ज़िप together. `postCallAnalysisHuman` also still counted *"three details nobody holds"* including the refill price; corrected to two, with an explicit note that the price is never-spoken rather than unknown. |
| **Files touched (17)** | `Default`, `routingAgent`, `newConnectionAgent_onHold`, the four delivery agents, both booking agents, `paymentAgent`, `subsidyAgent`, `connectionServicesAgent`, `genericInfoComplaintAgent`, `vaaniQA`, `postCallAnalysisHuman` — fifteen prompts — plus `bpcl_showroom_crc/CLAUDE.md` (a ZIP-04 banner on top of the existing superseded stack) and this ledger. `postCallAnalysisFlat` holds only ZIP *intent* examples, no facts, and was not touched. |
| **Standing gap** | `bpcl_lite_zip/litezipAgent.txt` is **not** a CRC prompt and is not registered in the agent registry. It remains a separate standalone deliverable; nothing routes to it, and CRC no longer needs it to answer a ZIP call. If it stays, it must be kept in step with this row or it becomes a second source of truth — the exact failure mode this ledger exists to prevent. |

### RFL-01 · Extra refill on an existing connection is temporarily blocked — ~~Mini is the only alternative~~ **Mini AND ZIP, superseded by ZIP-02 (2026-08-05)**

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. **CRC only**; CC deliberately behind by client decision. |
| **Policy** | Taking a **new refill on an existing connection beyond what that connection currently allows** — an extra cylinder, one more cylinder, a "double cylinder", an additional or second cylinder — is blocked. Applies to **every** such request, whether or not a complaint was registered earlier in the call. |
| **The line** | The block and the one option in a single short turn, then stop: *"अभी existing connection पर एक और refill लेना बंद है। पर एक option है — Bharat Gas Mini, जो पाँच kilogram का है और तुरंत मिल जाता है।"* Mini facts follow one per turn. Offered **once**. |
| **Hold discipline, borrowed from NC-01** | No reopening date, no reason, no hint that either exists. |
| **~~Mini only — no ZIP~~ SUPERSEDED by ZIP-02** | Was: ZIP is not offered against a blocked booking. **As of 2026-08-05 both Mini and ZIP are offered together** at this block and at a non-eligible booking, consumer chooses. The spoken line changed accordingly. |
| **Limits go quiet** | The 2/month · 15/year limits and the declaration form stay in the KB but are **not** spoken as the reason while the block is live. |
| **C8 demoted to reference-only** | `connectionServicesAgent` C8 now answers with the block + Mini. The application route (app / e-bharat-gas.com / distributor office with SV or Blue Book + ID) survives as **C8-KB**, spoken only when the consumer explicitly asks how to apply, never volunteered. The proactive C12 bridge is removed. |
| **Three contradictions cleared** | `newConnectionAgent_onHold` §8-OPEN, `Default` MODULE N and the refill-limit FAQ, and `vaaniQA` PART B all said a second/additional cylinder was unaffected. They now distinguish the **new-connection hold** (which still does not apply to it) from this **separate extra-refill block** (which does). `vaaniQA` scores quoting the limits, offering ZIP, giving a date or reason, or volunteering the application process unasked. |
| **Not a complaint** | This is a policy answer. No agent registers a complaint for it unless the consumer separately asks. |
| **Files** | `bookingNonEligibilityAgent`, `bookingEligibleAgent`, `eligibleDeliveryAgent`, `notEligibleDeliveryAgent`, `genericInfoComplaint`, `connectionServicesAgent`, `Default`, `newConnectionAgent_onHold`, `vaaniQA`. |
| **Open** | Whether the block has an end date, and whether it is meant to survive the new-connection hold being lifted. Neither is stated anywhere; the prompts correctly say nothing. |

### RTE-01 · `genericInfoComplaint` narrowed to what no specialist owns — CRC only (2026-08-04)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**. **CRC only**; CC deliberately behind by client decision. |
| **Was** | `Default` routed underweight, tests not performed, delivery-staff behaviour, cancellations, duplicate/unauthorized bookings and unwanted BAS bookings straight to `genericInfoComplaint`, even though the delivery and booking families own those and hold the backend state. Same reason strings, two owners, different depth. |
| **Now** | The agent is explicitly **the catch-all for what no specialist owns, not a shortcut past them**. It keeps state-free how-to, equipment and mechanic/stove/Mini, distributor behaviour, recurring and general delivery complaints, no home delivery, the WGT-01 verification, the RFL-01 block, and its escalation-sink job. It hands back a complaint about **one specific delivery**, and **booking-record** matters. |
| **Routing unchanged in name** | Hand-backs go to `routingAgent` by topic; agent names in the routing tables are untouched and the platform resolves the right family variant from backend state. |
| **The tool and reason list are NOT trimmed** | Only what routes *in* was narrowed. CRC's three complaint-toolless agents still escalate here, so this agent must be able to register anything that arrives that way. |
| **NO PING-PONG guard** | A topic is handed back **at most once per call**. If it already arrived from `routingAgent`, or the handoffSummary shows it was routed here deliberately, or it arrived as an escalation, it is handled here and registered — never sent back. Stated in the Section 3 scope block, the PATH 3 gate, and the unauthorized-booking path. |
| **Files** | `genericInfoComplaint` (Section 3 scope block, PATH 3 gate, unauthorized-booking path, recurring-quality path), `Default` (STAGE 2B classification, signal lists, agent inventory, Examples 7 and 7b), `routingAgent` (agent description + signal list). |
| **Depends on** | The platform resolving the delivery/booking family names to the right variant — the same dependency already logged for the four CRC prompts that are never a `switchagent` target by name. If it does not, a handed-back delivery complaint dead-ends. `genericInfoComplaint` remains the explicit fallback for anything with no specific booking or delivery behind it. |
