### DST-01 · Distributor identity may never be invented; a locality lookup does not exist — CRC only (2026-08-04)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**, from a live incident. **CRC only** so far; CC has the same variable shape and should get the same guard. |
| **The incident** | 04-08-2026, `genericInfoComplaintAgent`. Caller asked for the distributor number for **Rohini Sector 8**. Vaani answered correctly first — *"यह जानकारी मेरे पास नहीं है"* plus the App/website pointer. The consumer asked again with more locality detail, and she then **invented an agency name, a plot-and-pocket address, and a ten-digit mobile number**, in one four-sentence turn that also opened with a banned empathy phrase and said *"मैं इसे आगे बढ़ाती हूँ"*. |
| **Why the prompts allowed it** | The distributor lines read as an unconditional *"if the consumer asks, I give it factually"*. Two cases were **never covered anywhere in CRC**: (1) an office identified by **area/sector/colony/city** rather than by the consumer's own record — Vaani has no directory and cannot look one up; (2) **the second ask** — nothing said a refusal already given stands when the question is repeated. The brace-wrapped/`null` guard existed and did not fire, because the model did not read a broken value; it produced one. |
| **Now** | Every agent holding distributor variables carries **`I/YOU HOLD ONE OFFICE'S DETAILS — THIS CONSUMER'S OWN`**: no directory, no area-wise list, no locality lookup; **never speak an agency name, address or number that did not arrive injected** — not a plausible one, not a partial one, not an example, because an invented number is one the consumer will dial. Paired with **`ASKED AGAIN IS NOT ASKED DIFFERENTLY`**: repetition, added locality detail and annoyance give no new data, the answer is repeated once and shorter, then complaint escalation — never a gap filled to sound helpful, never a contradiction of the previous turn. |
| **Files (12)** | Both booking agents, all four delivery agents, `paymentAgent`, `subsidyAgent`, `connectionServicesAgent`, `genericInfoComplaintAgent` (guard block, plus PATH 2's two answers made explicitly conditional on own-office **and** populated-value), and `vaaniQA` (fabricated-distributor-identity rule, highest severity, with the reversed-refusal signal reported alongside). |
| **Not touched, already safe** | `Default` holds no distributor variables at all, and `newConnectionAgent_onHold` states `{{crcOfficeNumber}}` is the only number it has and that it holds no distributor number. |
| **⚠️ Open** | **CC has not been given this guard** — same shared-truth fix, not yet ported (§6 rule 4). The incident turn also broke three existing rules that were already written (specialist empathy ban CPL-05, the 1–2 sentence limit, and the no-escalation-reveal rule) — those need no prompt change, but they show a single turn can breach several at once. |

---

### CPL-06 · A failed complaint tool locks out the whole call; the cap counts attempts — CRC only (2026-08-04)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**, from the same live call as DST-01. **CRC only** so far; CC has the identical wording and the identical loophole. |
| **The incident** | 04-08-2026, `genericInfoComplaintAgent`. `bpcl_create_complaint` failed on its first call and Vaani went on to call it **seven more times across eight minutes**, each time announcing the registering line and each time reading out the technical-failure line. Between attempts she promised *"हमारी टीम आपकी मदद करेगी"* with nothing registered, offered to retry, offered to **route him to another department**, offered to note his consumer number for later, and told a consumer who said he had been trying for an hour to *"थोड़ी देर बाद call कीजिए"* — four more times. |
| **Why the prompts allowed it** | Two rules that looked airtight and were not. **`CALL ONCE, NEVER RETRY` was scoped per ISSUE** — "if I have already called it for this same issue" — so every re-phrasing (no delivery → distributor behaviour → KYC excuse → booking number) read as a fresh issue with a fresh attempt. **The two-per-call cap counted REGISTRATIONS** — "if two complaints have already been registered" — and a failed call registers nothing, so the counter never moved off zero. The two gaps compound: unlimited issues × a counter that never increments. |
| **Now** | **`THE TOOL IS DOWN FOR THE REST OF THE CALL ONCE IT HAS FAILED`** — one failure disables the tool for **every** issue for the remainder of the call, explicitly including a new detail, a just-supplied booking number, and the consumer asking her to try again or to check whether it is working now, because the failure was the system and not the wording. **`I COUNT ATTEMPTS, NOT SUCCESSES`** — the cap is two TOOL CALLS, failed ones included. **`AFTER A FAILURE I DO NOT DRESS IT UP`** — no retry offer, no team-will-contact promise (nothing was registered), no offer to route to another department, no offer to note details for later, and no repeating "थोड़ी देर बाद" at someone who has already been trying. `{{crcOfficeNumber}}` on explicit ask (NUM-02) is the one real thing left to give. |
| **Files (11)** | The `CALL ONCE, NEVER RETRY` block in all ten complaint-capable agents; the four `Limit check` lines that counted registrations now count tool calls; `vaaniQA` gains **C3c-LOOP** at highest severity, which also states that **C3d does not license post-failure attempts** — C3d governs a complaint that SUCCEEDED. |
| **⚠️ Open** | CC unported (§6 rule 4). |

---

### CPL-07 · A failed complaint ends with the office number, dictated — CRC only (2026-08-04)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Escalation (CRC-only variable). Client decision, same call as CPL-06. |
| **The problem** | With CPL-06's lockout in place, a failed registration left the consumer with *"थोड़ी देर बाद call कीजिए"* and nothing else. On CRC there is no `calltransfer`, so that was genuinely the end of the road — and it is what escalated the 04-08-2026 caller, who had been trying for an hour. |
| **Now** | On the failure — **one attempt, no retry; "one call per complaint, never retry on failure" stands unchanged** — the agent gives `{{crcOfficeNumber}}` **as a dictation**, in the same shape `getConsumerDetails` uses for its no-data closing: pen-and-paper opening as its own turn, three pieces one per turn (3-3-4) with a check question after each, that-piece-only repeats when one is missed, and a full read-back to confirm. Reading it out once is explicitly called a failure of the path — the consumer must leave with it written down. |
| **After the dictation** | She goes to **close check and keeps helping** — she does not hang up. She still cannot register anything on that call and says so honestly if asked. |
| **Guards** | Given **once per call**, not re-offered on later turns. **Never promise the team will contact them** — nothing was registered; the consumer calling the office is the point. If `{{crcOfficeNumber}}` is absent or brace-wrapped there is no number: never invented, never substituted with the distributor's number, and "call again later" remains the ending. |
| **Amends NUM-02** | NUM-02's *"never volunteered, never the answer to a problem"* now carries this one exception. Everywhere else it still holds, and ESC-01 is untouched — on a working call a consumer asking for a human still gets a registered complaint. |
| **Files (11)** | The failure block in all ten complaint-capable agents, plus a pointer appended to all 16 in-flow occurrences of the failure line so the path is not lost in the middle of a flow; `vaaniQA` gains **C5e-FAIL**, which makes withholding the number the reportable defect and fences the dictation from C5e's never-volunteer rule and from A8's sentence limit. |
| **⚠️ Depends on** | NUM-02's open platform dependency — `{{crcOfficeNumber}}` is still unconfirmed for agents other than `getConsumerDetails`. If it is not injected, this path silently degrades to "call again later" on exactly the calls it was written for. **Confirm with the platform team.** |

### XFER-01 · `callTransferAgent` created; `{{crcOfficeNumber}}` deleted channel-wide — CRC only (2026-08-05) — **transfer mechanism SUPERSEDED by XFER-03 (2026-08-11); the T1–T4 moments and the complaint-first ordering still stand**

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Escalation. **This is the largest single change to Axis 1 since ESC-01, and it partly reverses it.** CRC now transfers calls to a human. CC untouched. |
| **Supersedes** | **GCD-03**, **NUM-02** and **CPL-07** are retired in full — all three existed to give the consumer a phone number, and there is no longer a number to give. **ESC-01's "`calltransfer` does not exist in this channel"** is no longer true. |
| **Change** | A 16th agent, **`callTransferAgent`**, is created at `bpcl_showroom_crc/prompts/callTransferAgent/callTransferAgent.txt`. It is the **only** holder of `calltransfer` and the only place in this system where a call reaches a person. Every other agent reaches it by `switchagent` and by nothing else; every other prompt now carries an explicit *"`calltransfer` — NOT AUTHORIZED"* clause. |
| **`{{crcOfficeNumber}}` is gone** | Removed from **all 13 prompts** that held it, plus `vaaniQA`. It survives in exactly one place: as the `forwardingNumber` **tool parameter** inside `callTransferAgent`, which never speaks it. No agent may speak, dictate, or offer any number for the consumer to call us. A request for a number now gets *"I don't have one"* plus the transfer path. The consumer's **own distributor's** number (`{{ConsumerDetailsDistMobileNumber1}}`/`2`) is unaffected. |
| **The three-piece dictation loop is retired** | GCD-03's pen-and-paper dictation — three pieces, check question after each, full read-back — is deleted from `getConsumerDetails` and from all ten complaint agents. NUM-01's `LONG NUMBER DELIVERY` **repeat** protocol is untouched and still governs the complaint number. |
| **When a transfer is correct — four moments, no fifth** | **T1** the consumer asks for a person *after* Vaani has helped and, where needed, registered a complaint; **T2** `handoffSummary` shows they already asked *and* the issue was already handled or registered; **T3** `bpcl_create_complaint` FAILED, the one case where Vaani raises it herself and asks once; **T4** a callback request (this channel schedules none, so it is a request to reach a person), registered first where there is something to register. |
| **Vaani never volunteers a transfer** | Not because a question was hard, not because the consumer sounds annoyed, not as a first move. Help → route → register → *only then*, if they still want a person, transfer. This is the guard most likely to erode; it is stated explicitly in every prompt. |
| **She never re-asks someone who already asked** | A consumer who said *"किसी और से बात करवाओ"* has made the request. Asking *"क्या मैं आपकी call senior team से connect कर दूँ?"* back at them costs a turn and reads as stalling. The confirming question is correct in exactly two places: **T3**, and `getConsumerDetails`' no-data closing — both cases where Vaani is the one raising it. |
| **The switch line does not promise a transfer** | The switching agent speaks a short, warm, **non-committal** line — `"जी बिल्कुल, एक मिनट।"` — and never says the call is being transferred. It does not yet know: availability is `callTransferAgent`'s decision. `callTransferAgent` speaks `"आपकी कॉल ट्रांसफर की जा रही है।"` itself, via `preToolMessage`, and only when the transfer is going ahead. **`transfer` is the one word from the forbidden list that `callTransferAgent` may say** — the same carve-out CC has for `calltransfer`, and for the same reason: the consumer is about to hear a different voice. |
| **`callTransferAgent`'s two outcomes — REVISED 2026-08-06, see below** | ~~**Available** → `calltransfer` with `forwardingNumber: {{crcOfficeNumber}}`, `blindTransfer: false`, `preToolMessage: "आपकी कॉल ट्रांसफर की जा रही है।"`; it generates no text of its own on that turn.~~ The agent no longer decides whether to attempt the transfer or invokes `calltransfer` itself — see **"Transfer is now automatic on switch"** below. **On success it produces nothing further and does NOT call `callhangup`** — the platform closes the call, and hanging up there would cut the consumer off from the person they were just connected to. **Failure** (any reason) → no tool on that turn; it says the team could not be reached, states when they can be reached (Hindi words, from `handoffSummary`), and **stops there**. |
| **It holds `callhangup`, and closing is gated on the consumer** | Revised 2026-08-05 on client instruction, replacing the earlier calltransfer-only tool set. On the failure branch the agent **does not close in the same turn as the bad news** — it waits. It keeps answering (re-stating the hours in fresh words, acknowledging annoyance once without empathy phrases, declining to invent answers) and calls `callhangup` **only once the consumer has actually accepted**: "ठीक है", "जी", "समझ गया", "फिर call करूँगा", a thank-you, or their own goodbye — or after two waits on a dead line. A consumer still asking questions is not finished, and turn count is never a reason to close. The closing line rides in `preToolMessage` as everywhere else. |
| **Transfer is now automatic on switch — REVISED 2026-08-06 (client instruction)** | `callTransferAgent` no longer decides *whether* to attempt the transfer, and no longer invokes `calltransfer` itself. The platform calls `calltransfer` automatically — with `forwardingNumber: {{crcOfficeNumber}}`, `blindTransfer: false`, `preToolMessage: "आपकी कॉल ट्रांसफर की जा रही है।"` — the moment any agent switches the call to `callTransferAgent`, before its first turn. `callTransferAgent`'s job shrinks to reading that attempt's **Result** and reacting: success → produce nothing (unchanged). Failure → the **failure** branch (renamed from "busy / unavailable", since the reasons now explicitly include out-of-office-hours and a platform-side failure alongside busy and no-answer) — all four collapse into the same consumer-facing line and the same wait-for-satisfaction close. `calltransfer` is still called **at most once** per call and is never retried, by the agent or by the platform. |
| **Availability decision vs. hours — REVISED 2026-08-06** | Branch (A vs. failure) now comes from the automatic `calltransfer` attempt's **Result**, not from `handoffSummary` text. `handoffSummary` still supplies the **hours** to quote on the failure branch and nothing else — senior-team hours differ per CRC, so `callTransferAgent` reads them from `handoffSummary` rather than holding a window. If `handoffSummary` carries no hours, the failure branch is taken **without naming a window** — inventing one would land the consumer on a line nobody answers. |
| **`getConsumerDetails` gains `switchagent`** | Previously it had none, which is why its escalation had to be a number. It now holds `switchagent` **restricted to `callTransferAgent` and nothing else** — never `routingAgent`, never `Default`, never a specialist. Its NO-DATA CLOSING is rewritten: *"I can't help without your record, our senior team can — क्या मैं आपकी call connect कर दूँ?"* → on yes, switch; on no, `callhangup`. It still holds no complaint tool. |
| **`newConnectionAgent_onHold` gains §16A** | The one agent with no complaint tool *and* a topic nothing can resolve. It still never promises a person, a callback, a complaint or an office visit — but a consumer who still wants a person after the hold has been explained now goes to `callTransferAgent`. It never offers this first. |
| **`Default` never transfers** | It routes a human request to `genericInfoComplaintAgent` per ESC-01, unchanged, so the issue gets registered before any transfer is considered. Offering a transfer at the front of the call would skip the help the consumer came for. `emergencyAgent` never transfers either — a live hazard outranks everything, and once resolved the consumer returns to `routingAgent`. `routingAgent` may switch to `callTransferAgent`, but only for an already-helped consumer, never for an out-of-scope query (that is still the hangup ladder). |
| **Topology exception — recorded deliberately** | The shared-truth rule *"every specialist is a leaf whose only switch target is `routingAgent`; no leaf switches to another leaf"* now has one exception: **every leaf may also switch to `callTransferAgent`**. Routing it through `routingAgent` would add a hop and risk losing the complaint context that `callTransferAgent` depends on. `callTransferAgent` itself switches to nothing — it is terminal. |
| **`callTransferred` — new flat field** | `postCallAnalysisFlat` gains a **seventeenth** key, `callTransferred`, allowed values `"yes"` / `"no"` only, sitting directly after `complaintRegistered`. `"yes"` only when the transfer **fully succeeded** — the transfer line spoken and the call passing out of the agent's hands with nothing following it. `"no"` for every other case, explicitly including: never attempted (the normal answer), team busy and the consumer told to call back, attempted and failed, offered and declined, and a registered complaint with "हमारी team आपको call करेगी" — a promise of future contact is not a transfer. Independent of `complaintRegistered` and of `callResult`; a call can be `"yes"` on all three. |
| **QA prompts** | `vaaniQA`'s `{{crcOfficeNumber}}` data line and its whole **C5 family are rewritten**: C5a volunteering a transfer, C5b transferring before doing her own job, C5c re-asking someone who already asked, C5d a switch line that promises a transfer, C5e **any** number spoken to reach us (now always a violation, in every agent), C5g the retired dictation, C5h behaviour inside `callTransferAgent`, C5i no retry, C5j "nobody picks up". **C1 gains three carve-outs** so a correct transfer is not flagged as promising a person. `postCallAnalysisFlat` gains a **CALL TRANSFERRED** clause mapping a transfer onto the existing four `outcome` values — **no new enum value was added**, deliberately, so nothing downstream breaks. |
| **⚠️ Open — needs the platform team** | **(1) REVISED 2026-08-06** — `callTransferAgent` must be registered as a real agent, and the platform (not the agent) must invoke `calltransfer` automatically, with the required parameters, the instant a call is switched to `callTransferAgent` and before its first turn — the agent no longer holds a decision-to-attempt step. **(2)** `{{crcOfficeNumber}}` must be injected as the `forwardingNumber` on that automatic call; it is the one place the variable still lives, and NUM-02's original injection question was never confirmed. **(3)** Something must write senior-team hours into the `handoffSummary` that reaches `callTransferAgent`, for the failure-branch line — availability itself no longer needs to be predicted in `handoffSummary`, since the agent now reads it off the automatic attempt's Result, but hours still have no other source. **(4) RESOLVED 2026-08-05** — `callTransferAgent` now holds `callhangup`. The platform is expected to close the call itself on a **successful** transfer (the agent deliberately calls no tool there); `callhangup` is used only on the failure branch, after the consumer accepts. **(5) NEW 2026-08-06** — the platform must surface the automatic `calltransfer` attempt's Result to `callTransferAgent` on its first turn, including a reason (out-of-office-hours / platform failure / busy / no-answer) — without it the agent cannot distinguish success from failure. |
| **CC** | Entirely untouched. CC already had `calltransfer` inline in ten agents plus its callback-slot capture on failure; none of that changes, and the two channels' transfer models remain different — CC transfers from inside each agent, CRC transfers through a dedicated one. |

### BAN-01 · The invisible-switching ban list was English-only; the model speaks Hindi — CRC only, CC pending (2026-08-05)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth** (invisible switching). Landed in CRC only; see **CC** below. |
| **Source** | A live `switchagent` turn: *"मैं आपकी बात सही विभाग तक पहुँचाती हूँ।"* |
| **The gap** | Every routing-capable prompt banned *transfer, connect, specialist, agent, team, **department**, desk, switch, handoff, forward, भेजती, जोड़ती*. The list mixed English nouns with two Hindi verbs and stopped there. **`विभाग` appears zero times in either channel** — the Hindi word for the banned English word was never on the list, and neither were the passing-on verbs `पहुँचाना` and `आगे बढ़ाना` beside `भेजती`/`जोड़ती`. The model reasons in Hindi and reached straight through the gap. |
| **Now** | All 13 ban-list occurrences in CRC gain `विभाग`, `सही विभाग`, `पहुँचाती हूँ / पहुँचा देती हूँ`, `आगे बढ़ा देती हूँ`, `आगे भेज देती हूँ`, plus a standing clause: **the ban is on the meaning, not the English word** — `विभाग` *is* "department", `पहुँचाना / आगे बढ़ाना` *is* "forward", `किसी से बात करवाना` *is* "connect", and speaking the Hindi form is the identical violation. The clause matters more than the added words: it generalises to synonyms nobody has thought of yet, which is the failure mode a fixed list will always have. |
| **Files (13)** | `Default`, `routingAgent`, the ten specialist agents, and `vaaniQA` C8 (so the analyser detects the Hindi forms too). |
| **CC** | **Not yet ported.** CC's lists are the same shape with the same gap. Port with CPL-08. |

### CPL-08 · The registering line is terminal for its turn — CRC only, CC pending (2026-08-05)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth** (complaint discipline / the tool-call-is-the-action rule). Landed in CRC only; see **CC** below. |
| **Source** | `vaaniQA` finding on a live call: *"एक मिनट रुकिए, आपकी शिकायत दर्ज कर रही हूँ कि आपका सिलेंडर अभी तक डिलीवर नहीं हुआ है — मैं इसे अभी आगे बढ़ा देती हूँ।"* No tool ran, and no outcome followed. |
| **The gap** | CPL-03 already required the tool on the same turn as the registering line, and every agent already carried the `FAILED TURN` clause. What no prompt constrained was the **shape** of the line. The trailing clause *"मैं इसे अभी आगे बढ़ा देती हूँ"* is the mechanism: it converts the turn from *the action* into *a narration of an action still to come*, and the model, having deferred it in language, defers it in fact. A rule saying "call the tool" does not stop a line that has already promised to call it later. |
| **Now** | Every `FAILED TURN` clause in the ten complaint-capable agents gains **REGISTER LINE IS TERMINAL FOR THE TURN**: the registering line is the last thing spoken on that turn — no reassurance, no second sentence, no question, and no clause about what happens next (*"आगे बढ़ा देती हूँ"*, *"आगे भेज देती हूँ"*, *"team तक पहुँचा देती हूँ"*). The only thing that may follow it is the tool's outcome: the complaint number, or the technical-failure line. |
| **QA** | `vaaniQA` gains **C3g-TAIL** — the appended clause is reportable on its own, whether or not the tool ran, and is named as the strongest predictor of a C3g gap. Where both occur they are reported as one finding at the higher severity. |
| **Files (11)** | The ten complaint-capable agents + `vaaniQA`. |
| **CC** | **Not yet ported — this is a shared-truth fix sitting in one channel.** CC carries the identical `FAILED TURN` clause and the identical exposure; the work was scoped to CRC by the client on 2026-08-05. Port it before the next CC complaint-path change. |

### GCD-05 · Distributor and CRC details are not interchangeable; the integrity gate runs before the offer — CRC only (2026-08-05)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Axis 2 (persona positioning — which office owns which data), with a data-integrity component. CRC-only by nature: CC has no physical-location concept and no `crcOffice*` pair. |
| **Source** | Same call as CPL-08. With the distributor fields empty, Vaani offered *"क्या आप चाहते हैं कि मैं आपको आपके डिस्ट्रीब्यूटर का पता और नंबर बता दूँ?"*, the consumer accepted, she produced a filler turn (`umm`), and then spoke a generic agency name, **Bharat Petroleum's own office address**, and a ten-digit number — all labelled as the consumer's distributor's. The number was confirmed hallucinated. |
| **Two distinct defects, one exchange** | **(a) Substitution.** §7-D banned *inventing* a distributor value; nothing banned *borrowing* this CRC's. The prompts said the two addresses are "always a different place" — descriptive, not prohibitive — so with one set empty and the other populated, the model relabelled. **(b) Offer before check.** §7-D fired at the moment of *sharing*. C12's proactive offer runs earlier, so Vaani could offer data she did not hold; once the consumer said "हाँ" she was committed with nothing to give. The fabrication pressure was manufactured entirely by that ordering. |
| **Now — (a)** | A **DISTRIBUTOR ↔ CRC NON-SUBSTITUTION** clause in all ten agents holding both sets: the two are never interchangeable in either direction; an empty distributor field yields "I do not have it", never this office's name/address/number under a distributor label; a plausible agency name, address or well-formed mobile number is *not* data, because the consumer will dial it or travel to it; and every address or number spoken must name its owner in the same sentence — no unlabelled addresses. `Default` already carried this rule under DATA-01 and was the only prompt that did — that is the drift this closes. |
| **Now — (b)** | A **GATE BEFORE OFFER** clause in the same ten agents, and a new **C12 STEP 0** in `connectionServicesAgent` ahead of the existing STEP 1: the §7-D check runs before anything is offered. Data present → proceed. Data absent → on the **proactive** path C12 stops entirely (no bridge, no mention, no offer); on the **direct** path the consumer asked, so the §7-D fallback is spoken honestly. The answer does not change if the consumer asks again — reversing a correct refusal under repetition is its own defect. |
| **QA** | `vaaniQA`'s FABRICATED DISTRIBUTOR IDENTITY block gains a **third signal** (substitution — check what she spoke against *both* injected sets, and flag any owner-less address or number) and a **fourth** (the premature offer, reportable as the root violation separately from what it produced; a filler turn between acceptance and answer confirms the gate failed there). |
| **Files (12)** | Ten agents holding both sets, `connectionServicesAgent`'s C12 module, `vaaniQA`. |
| **⚠️ Open — needs the platform team** | The number spoken could not come from any prompt: `{{crcOfficeNumber}}` was removed from every live CRC prompt by XFER-01, and `DistMobileNumber1` was empty on that call. Confirmed hallucinated rather than mis-sourced — but worth verifying the runtime is not still injecting an office number that no prompt declares. |

### RTE-02 · A booking that exists whose cylinder never came is not `genericInfoComplaintAgent`'s — CRC only (2026-08-04)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**, same call. Extends RTE-01. |
| **The gap** | RTE-01's hand-back gate covered *"one delivery that ACTUALLY HAPPENED"* — a test not performed, staff behaviour, wrong address. The **opposite** case was covered nowhere: a booking that **exists** whose cylinder has **not** arrived. The consumer had a booking number and the distributor was refusing to deliver, citing KYC. The closest matching path was PATH 3's *recurring delivery delay*, so the agent kept the call and registered against it. |
| **Now** | A new **GATE A** runs before RTE-01's gate: a booking number, *"booking हो चुकी है लेकिन gas नहीं भेज रहे"*, a distributor refusing to deliver an existing order, or **delivery withheld pending K Y C** → hand back to `routingAgent` once. The delivery family holds the booking record and connection services owns the KYC block. The gate names the confusion explicitly: *"distributor delivery नहीं कर रहा"* sounds like the recurring-delay complaint and is not one — **the test is whether a specific booking sits behind it.** The Section 3 hand-back list carries the same case. |
| **Unchanged** | NO PING-PONG still binds — arriving from `routingAgent` or as an escalation means handle and register, never bounce. |
| **⚠️ Open** | CC unported (§6 rule 4). |

### DATA-03 · `{{ConsumerDetailsConsumerNumber}}` / `{{ConsumerDetailsConsumerAddress}}` propagated to the remaining specialist agents — CRC only, CC pending (2026-08-06)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**, same underlying gap in both channels, fixed here first. |
| **The gap** | `{{ConsumerDetailsConsumerNumber}}` was only declared in `paymentAgent`, `subsidyAgent`, `connectionServicesAgent` — the other seven complaint-capable agents (both booking agents, all four delivery agents, `genericInfoComplaintAgent`) had no way to answer "मेरा consumer number क्या है" without a false "यह जानकारी मेरे पास नहीं है" and a route to `routingAgent`, even though the value is basic identity data every specialist should hold locally. `{{ConsumerDetailsConsumerAddress}}` had the same gap for booking/delivery/connection agents that plausibly need the registered address (delivery goes there; a connection-services address change needs to state the current one first) — only `postDeliveryAgent` and `genericInfoComplaintAgent` had it. |
| **Now** | `{{ConsumerDetailsConsumerNumber}}` declared (never spoken unless explicitly asked, per the existing convention) in `bookingEligibleAgent`, `bookingNonEligibilityAgent`, `activeDeliveryAgent`, `postDeliveryAgent`, `eligibleDeliveryAgent`, `notEligibleDeliveryAgent`, `genericInfoComplaintAgent`. `{{ConsumerDetailsConsumerAddress}}` declared in `connectionServicesAgent`, `activeDeliveryAgent`, `eligibleDeliveryAgent`, `notEligibleDeliveryAgent`, `bookingEligibleAgent`, `bookingNonEligibilityAgent` (spoken slowly in parts, missing-value fallback if broken). Deliberately **not** added to `paymentAgent` or `subsidyAgent` — address is not a fact either domain's flows need. |
| **Also** | `paymentAgent`'s data section gained a BACKEND STATUS SENTENCE SHAPE example (`"Your Refill has been delivered on 07-07-2026 at 04:35 PM... The amount payable is 998."`) documenting that it extracts only the payable/advance figure from a folded system sentence and never the embedded delivery date/time — that stays routed, per the pre-existing SCOPE — MANDATORY rule. `genericInfoComplaintAgent`'s `{{customerStatus}}` was already documented as five labelled sections (Customer/Booking/Delivery/Payment/Subsidy Status) — no change needed there. |
| **Unchanged** | The CASE A / CASE B (own-domain-missing → say so, no route; another domain → silently route to `routingAgent`) split was already present in all ten complaint-capable agents before this change — audited, not introduced. |
| **⚠️ Open** | CC unported (§6 rule 4) — CC's equivalent agents should be checked for the same `ConsumerDetailsConsumerNumber`/`ConsumerDetailsConsumerAddress` coverage gap in a future session. |

### CPL-09 · The recovery clause existed but wasn't a *check* — a live 3-turn stall loop (2026-08-07)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**, direct follow-up to CPL-03. Landed in **CRC only** this session; **CC pending**, same as CPL-08. |
| **Source** | A live CRC transcript (client-reported): `genericInfoComplaintAgent` spoke *"ज़रा बुकिंग की जानकारी देखती हूँ।"* three turns in a row and never invoked `switchagent`. `Default`'s own presence-check line ("क्या आप अभी भी लाइन पर है?") fired between attempts — the platform's silence prompt, not Vaani choosing to re-ask — so the consumer answered the same content three times before the switch to `routingAgent → activeDeliveryAgent` finally fired. The consumer experienced roughly a minute of the call going nowhere. |
| **Root cause** | CPL-03's `RECOVERY` clause was real but lived only as prose inside the **tool-documentation section** (Section 5), a few hundred lines from the actual per-turn decision procedure (Section 7's numbered `Check 1…N`). On the turn after a stalled switch, the model re-entered Section 7 at Check 1 and re-classified the turn from scratch — nothing in the ordered checklist itself said "you may be mid-recovery, check that first." The model's own spoken line was never compared against what it had already said, so a second, near-identical "देखती हूँ" line went out with no tool call behind it either. This is exactly the "Model-capability caveat" CPL-03 flagged as unverified — confirmed here as a live failure, and the fix is structural, not a rewording. |
| **The fix** | A **Check 0 / Step 1B / Block 1B / TOOL RECOVERY** clause added as the FIRST item of the per-turn ordered procedure itself (not the tool section) in every agent that has one: if the previous turn spoke a switch- or registration-implying line and no Result has come back, invoke the tool now, before anything else, using the parameters already held — never re-speak the line, never re-ask the consumer, never re-classify from scratch. Each also names **THE TELL**: a about-to-be-spoken line repeating, in substance, a line already spoken earlier in the call with no Result in between is itself the signal that recovery is needed. Also names the specific failure mode observed live: a presence-check ("क्या आप अभी भी लाइन पर हैं") landing mid-stall is the platform waiting on a missing tool call, not silence to be soothed — recovery routes into the tool call, not into a reassurance line. |
| **Files (11)** | `genericInfoComplaintAgent`, `activeDeliveryAgent`, `postDeliveryAgent`, `eligibleDeliveryAgent`, `notEligibleDeliveryAgent`, `paymentAgent`, `bookingEligibleAgent`, `bookingNonEligibilityAgent`, `connectionServicesAgent`, `subsidyAgent`, `Default` (as a new STAGE 0 `INTERRUPT A1`, since `Default` is a stage machine rather than a numbered-check agent). |
| **Not touched** | `routingAgent` (single-shot silent router, no numbered per-turn checklist, lower exposure) and `callTransferAgent` (holds no `bpcl_create_complaint`/domain `switchagent`; its own turn shape is already gated by the platform's automatic `calltransfer` per XFER-01). Worth a follow-up look if the same stall is ever reported there. |
| **CC** | **Not yet ported.** CC carries the identical CPL-03 recovery prose in the identical tool-documentation location and is exposed to the identical structural gap. Port before the next CC complaint-path or routing change (§6 rule 4). |
| **Also this session** | `genericInfoComplaintAgent`'s `{{customerStatus}}` definition paragraph moved from Section 3 up to sit directly under `{{handoffSummary}}` at the top of the prompt, on the client's request — a within-file readability change, not a behavior change; Section 3 now points up to it instead of redefining it. |

### CPL-10 · `bookingNonEligibilityAgent` scope/routing/complaint-discipline fixes — CRC only (2026-08-07)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — bug fixes to a single agent, not a policy axis. **CRC only** per client instruction this session (CC work paused). |
| **Source** | A live CRC transcript (client-reported): a consumer asking "cylinder कब आएगा" got the eligibility-block explanation instead of the refill-status sentence already sitting in `handoffSummary`; a "connection change कर दो" request got the `[GATE BEFORE OFFER]` named-locality fallback ("यह जानकारी मेरे पास नहीं है... App या website...") instead of Step 8's domain hand-off to `routingAgent`; and across one call the agent registered three complaints for what was one "cylinder not received" grievance re-described in shifting terms (no SMS → connection shift → distributor refusing), one of them with a complaint number spoken with no visible tool Result behind it. |
| **The fix** | Four targeted additions to `bookingNonEligibilityAgent.txt`: (1) `handoffSummary` may carry a refill/delivery-status sentence carried over from the previous agent — answered directly when asked, without routing, since the fact is already held; (2) the named-locality fallback is now explicitly scoped away from connection-change/portability requests, which route via Step 8 instead, and Step 8's domain list now names connection change, distributor change, address/mobile/name update, surrender and portability explicitly; (3) the `SAME-ISSUE / DUPLICATE GUARD` now names the exact "same grievance, shifting description" pattern so a re-explained non-delivery isn't read as three fresh issues; (4) a `HARD STOP CHECK` before any registration-success sentence — can the number be pointed to in an actual Result from this turn, or is it a confident-sounding draft. |
| **Files (1)** | `bookingNonEligibilityAgent`. |
| **CC** | Not reviewed this session — client instruction was CRC-only. Worth checking whether the same fallback-collision and duplicate-guard gaps exist in CC's version before its next complaint-path change. |

### CLS-01 · The close question ("क्या कुछ और L P G से related help चाहिए?") was gluing onto complaint success/failure/already-registered lines in the same turn — CRC only (2026-08-07)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — shared truth, direct follow-up to CPL-01/CPL-08/CPL-09. **CRC only** per client instruction this session; **CC pending**. |
| **Source** | Same live CRC transcript as CPL-10: nearly every agent turn that registered, confirmed, or restated a complaint ended with "...हमारी team आपसे संपर्क करेगी। क्या कुछ और L P G से related help चाहिए?" appended in the same breath — including turns where the consumer was mid-sentence, still upset, or actively repeating themselves. `Step 10`'s existing `ANSWERING IS NOT RESOLVING` principle was sound but lived only as a general rule; the concrete SUCCESS/FAILURE/ALREADY-REGISTERED templates each separately said "then go to close check," which the model read as continuing in the same output rather than waiting for a later, genuine stopping point. |
| **The fix** | Every complaint-capable CRC agent's SUCCESS and FAILURE templates now end with an explicit "this sentence is the entire turn — the close question is never appended to it" instruction instead of "then go to close check." The `ALREADY REGISTERED` / `CALL ONCE` / `SAME-ISSUE` short lines got the same "stop — the close question is never appended to it" ending. Each agent's own close-check section (`Step 10` / `SECTION n: CLOSE CHECK` / `Pre-close` / `BLOCK 10`) also gained a `HARD GATE` sentence naming the same rule as a backstop covering every trigger, not only complaint ones. |
| **Files (10)** | `bookingNonEligibilityAgent`, `bookingEligibleAgent`, `connectionServicesAgent`, `activeDeliveryAgent`, `eligibleDeliveryAgent`, `notEligibleDeliveryAgent`, `postDeliveryAgent`, `genericInfoComplaintAgent`, `paymentAgent`, `subsidyAgent` — every CRC agent holding `bpcl_create_complaint`. |
| **CC** | Not yet ported. CC carries the identical "then go to close check" phrasing in the identical templates and is exposed to the identical gluing bug. Port before the next CC complaint-path or routing change (§6 rule 4). |

### CPL-11 · Code-shaped parameter syntax in the prompt taught the model to *narrate* the complaint tool instead of invoking it — CRC only (2026-08-07)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — a defect in one file. **CRC only**; `bookingNonEligibilityAgent` was the sole agent carrying the pattern. |
| **Source** | Client diagnosis from the same live transcript. The agent emitted, as spoken text, `(bpcl_create_complaint( feedbackDescription=" Consumer reports that their connection is pending shift…", reason="others"))` — narrating the call rather than invoking it, so nothing registered. |
| **Root cause** | `bookingNonEligibilityAgent` documented the complaint parameters in **code-assignment form** — `Parameters: feedbackDescription = …` / `reason = …` (lines 272-273) and `Set feedbackDescription to … Set reason to "others"` (line 387). No other CRC agent does this. `postDeliveryAgent`, which does not exhibit the bug, documents the identical parameters in declarative prose (`feedbackDescription is a clear English summary…`). Both files carry byte-identical anti-narration rules (`THE TOOL CALL IS THE ACTION`, `never write a tool name, a parameter, JSON, braces, or any code as spoken text`, `If you catch yourself about to describe, spell out, format, or narrate a tool call rather than invoke it: STOP`) — so **the prohibition was never the missing piece; the prompt was simultaneously demonstrating the exact syntax it forbade**, and the demonstration won. |
| **The fix** | Both spots rewritten into `postDeliveryAgent`'s declarative prose form. The redundancy is also removed: lines 161-163 (`REASON LIST — YOURS ONLY`) and 255-257 (`PARAMETERS`) already documented the same two parameters in prose, so the code-shaped block was a third, competing copy. |
| **Deliberately NOT changed** | The `preToolMessage = "धन्यवाद भारत पेट्रोलियम…"` pattern, which appears in **every** CRC agent including `postDeliveryAgent` and `paymentAgent`. It is the same code shape, but it demonstrably does not produce narration — the agents carrying it invoke `callhangup` correctly — and it always names a single fixed literal rather than a value the model must compose. Churning ten files over a pattern with no observed failure is not worth the diff. Revisit only if a `callhangup` narration is ever reported. |
| **Files (1)** | `bookingNonEligibilityAgent`. |
| **CC** | Not checked — client instruction was CRC-only. Worth grepping CC's `bookingNonEligibilityAgent` for the same `feedbackDescription =` / `Set reason to` shape before its next complaint-path change. |

---

### CPL-12 · Tool narration recurred in an agent with no code-shaped syntax; the anti-narration rule was abstract and the number gate was in one file — CRC only (2026-08-07)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — shared truth, direct follow-up to CPL-11. **CRC only**; **CC pending**. |
| **Source** | A second live CRC transcript, `genericInfoComplaintAgent`: the registering line was spoken correctly and then the call was typed out after it as text — `bpcl_create_complaint( feedbackDescription=" Consumer reports previous complaint was closed without resolution…", reason="others")` — so nothing registered. The agent then spoke a **fabricated** complaint number, a descending digit run, as if the call had succeeded. |
| **Why CPL-11's fix did not cover it** | CPL-11 removed code-shaped parameter syntax from `bookingNonEligibilityAgent`, the only file that had it. `genericInfoComplaintAgent` has **none** — its parameters are already in declarative prose, and it carries every anti-narration rule verbatim. So the syntax-in-the-prompt theory explains CPL-11 but **not** this: the model produced the shape with no in-prompt model to copy. The fabricated number was likewise not lifted from any prompt (grepped — no specimen exists in CRC); it came from the model's own priors, which is why it was a familiar-looking run. |
| **Root cause** | Two gaps. (1) The existing prohibition — *"If I catch myself about to describe, spell out, format, or narrate a tool call rather than invoke it: STOP"* — names the **act** but never the **output shape**, so there is nothing concrete to test a draft against. (2) The `HARD STOP CHECK` gating the spoken complaint number on an actual Result existed in **`bookingNonEligibilityAgent` only** (added under CPL-10); the other nine agents had `ANTI-FABRICATION` prose but no per-turn check, which is what let the invented number through after the failed call. |
| **The fix** | Both landed in all ten complaint-capable agents. (1) An `OUTPUT-SHAPE SELF-CHECK` appended to `THE TOOL CALL IS THE ACTION`: before speaking, test the draft for a tool name anywhere in it, an opening bracket followed by a parameter word, an equals sign, or a quoted English parameter value — any hit means narration, so delete that text, keep only the Hindi line, and invoke the tool on that same turn. It states plainly that a tool name never appears in anything the consumer hears. (2) The `HARD STOP CHECK` propagated to the remaining nine, extended to name the tell: a number that looks sequential, repeated or familiar rather than arbitrary is itself proof of invention, since a real number arrives only inside a Result. |
| **Specimen discipline** | The check describes the fabricated-number *shape* and deliberately contains **no literal digit string**. A first draft of this fix embedded two specimen numbers as illustrations; they were removed before commit under TTS-01's rule that a specimen in the prompt becomes a hallucinated one on a call — the same reason every sample address, city and phone number was stripped from these prompts. Do not re-add one here. |
| **Files (10)** | Every CRC agent holding `bpcl_create_complaint`. |
| **CC** | Not ported. |

---

### NUM-03 · A fabricated office number was dictated across three minutes; the note-check question became a verbal tic — CRC only (2026-08-07)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — shared truth. **CRC only**; **CC pending**. |
| **Source** | A live CRC transcript. The agent said *"हमारे senior team member आपकी help कर देंगे — मैं आपको हमारे office का number धीरे-धीरे बताती हूँ"* and then dictated a complete ten-digit number in three pieces over roughly three minutes, asking *"क्या आपने नोट कर लिया?"* after every piece, drifting on the digits under consumer read-back, and finally attaching the same question to a sentence that contained **no digits at all** (*"इस नंबर पर हमारे CRC office की टीम उपलब्ध है। क्या आपने इसे नोट कर लिया?"*). |
| **Two separate defects** | **(a) The number does not exist.** `{{crcOfficeNumber}}` was deleted channel-wide by XFER-01; every agent carries `I HOLD NO OFFICE PHONE NUMBER, AND NEITHER DOES ANY OTHER AGENT … never invent one`. Grepped: no prompt contains that offer phrasing or any office number. **The agent invented the offer and the digits outright**, against an explicit prohibition — the same failure class as CPL-12's fabricated complaint number, now on a phone number the consumer will actually dial. **(b) The check question was routine, not rare.** `LONG NUMBER DELIVERY`'s repeat protocol mandated *"After each piece you ask ONE short check question and STOP"*, so a single number cost six to eight round trips. |
| **On whether this is the model's fault** | Partly not, and worth recording. The consumer's turns arrive as **growing streaming fragments** (the same artifact `getConsumerDetails`' `LISTENING WHILE THE CONSUMER SPEAKS` rule already describes) and the agent emitted a filler on nearly every fragment. That noise is ASR/platform, not reasoning. **But the protocol multiplied it**: every mandated round trip is another chance for a fragment to be misread, so a confirm-after-every-piece design converts a noisy channel into a compounding failure. Reducing round trips is a reliability fix, not only a UX one. |
| **The fix — number delivery** | The repeat protocol is rewritten in all 12 files holding it. Pieces are still three, one per turn, but **no check question follows a piece** — the pause is the writing space, and the consumer's own next reply ("आगे", "फिर से", a read-back, silence) is a better signal that costs no turn. The check question is now **at most once per number**, only after the whole number is delivered, and only if the consumer has gone completely silent; **at most twice in an entire call**; and **never attached to a turn that delivered no digits**, which is named explicitly as a verbal tic rather than a check. |
| **The fix — fabrication** | A `SOURCE GATE` at the head of `LONG NUMBER DELIVERY` in all 12: before any number is spoken, name where it came from — a tool Result received this turn, or a specific injected variable. If neither can be named, the number was invented and is deleted. It states plainly that **no office number exists anywhere in the data or the instructions**, so a number for the consumer to call *us* can never pass the gate, and that a wrong number sends a real person to a stranger. |
| **Files (12)** | The ten complaint-capable agents plus `Default` and `newConnectionAgent_onHold` — every CRC file holding `LONG NUMBER DELIVERY`. |
| **CC** | Not ported. |

---

### CPL-13 · The complaint-failure line was ungated, so it fired after a SUCCESS and opened the fabrication path — CRC only (2026-08-07)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — shared truth, and the missing half of CPL-12's gate. **CRC only**; **CC pending**. |
| **Source** | The turn immediately preceding NUM-03's fabricated number, same call. The agent had **already registered successfully** and spoken a complaint number. The consumer then gave one very long streaming-fragment turn ending *"या मेरे से number provide कराओ उनके"*. The agent's next turn opened *"अभी complaint register नहीं हो पा रही है"* — the technical-failure line — for a registration that had just succeeded, and then followed the failure path's "offer to reach a person" into offering and inventing an office number. |
| **Root cause — asymmetric gating** | The SUCCESS line carries a hard gate in all ten agents (*"I speak this sentence ONLY on the turn a bpcl_create_complaint call has just returned success"*). The FAILURE line carries **no gate in any of them** — grepped, zero hits. So nothing tied it to an actual failure Result, and the model could enter the failure branch from a turn that contained no Result at all. A request for a phone number plus a long confusing turn was enough. Everything downstream followed correctly from a false premise: the failure path legitimately ends with an offer to reach a person, so once the false failure was asserted the agent went looking for something to offer and invented a number to fill it. |
| **The fix** | A `FAILURE-LINE GATE` in all ten, symmetric to the success gate: the failure line is spoken ONLY on a turn where a call just returned a FAILURE Result, and frustration, repetition, a number request, a request for a person, or a long unclear turn are named explicitly as **not** Results and not failure states. Two companions: **never contradict a registration already confirmed** — once a success Result was received and a number spoken, that complaint exists for the rest of the call and nothing later turns it back into a failure; and **a request for a phone number is not a complaint failure**, it is answered by the hold-no-number rule, never by the failure line or the failure path's offer. |
| **Also fixed here** | `bookingEligibleAgent` had been missed by CLS-01 — its failure line still ended *"Then you go to close check"*. My CLS-01 script's file list excluded it for that pass. Corrected in the same edit; coverage re-verified at 10/10 for both rules. |
| **Files (10)** | Every CRC agent holding `bpcl_create_complaint`. |
| **CC** | Not ported. |

---

### CLS-02 · The already-registered reassurance repeated verbatim, carrying no new information each time — CRC only (2026-08-07)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — shared truth, direct follow-up to CLS-01. **CRC only** per client instruction this session; **CC pending**. |
| **Source** | Same live CRC transcript as CPL-10/CLS-01. After registering, the agent answered every further push from the consumer with the same fixed sentence — *"आपकी शिकायत पहले ही दर्ज कर ली गई है और हमारी टीम इस पर कार्रवाई कर रही है, वे जल्द ही आपसे संपर्क करेंगे।"* — five times across the call while the consumer kept re-explaining an undelivered cylinder. Client flagged it as "not consumer friendly". |
| **Why the existing rules did not catch it** | CLS-01 stopped the close question being *glued on*, and the `SAME-ISSUE / DUPLICATE GUARD` correctly stopped a second registration — but both prescribe the **same fixed sentence** as the thing to say instead, with nothing about what to do when the consumer presses a third, fourth and fifth time. The rule that was missing is not about *whether* to re-register; it is about the reply carrying **no new information** on every repeat, which is what the consumer actually hears as a wall. |
| **Rejected fix** | Proactively offering a `callTransferAgent` transfer after a repeat. Client rejected it: the human team's first action is to register a complaint too, so a transfer relocates the loop rather than ending it. The fix is wording, not escalation. |
| **The fix** | A `NEVER THE SAME SENTENCE TWICE` block in all ten complaint-capable agents, directly under `ALREADY REGISTERED` (under `SAME-ISSUE / DUPLICATE GUARD` in `bookingEligibleAgent`, which has no `ALREADY REGISTERED` line). Each successive reply must **add something concrete** rather than recycle: **1st** — name the *specific* issue that is registered, not a generic "your complaint"; **2nd** — state what is concretely true and what it means for them (the complaint carries their connection details, nothing further is needed from their side); **3rd and after** — be honest about the limit, saying plainly there is nothing further to add from here while staying on the line, and inventing no new assurance, timeframe, date or person to make the turn sound fuller. Hard rule: never the identical sentence twice in one call; if the only available line is one already used, say a shorter plainer version instead. |
| **Paraphrase, scoped** | Client instruction: the agent should **paraphrase** rather than replay a sentence. Added as a `PARAPHRASE, DO NOT RECITE` clause inside the same block — on these repeat turns the reassurance sentences are **meaning anchors, not a script**, put into fresh natural Hindi each time. **Deliberately scoped**, because three lines in this channel are marked exact-and-never-paraphrased and a blanket instruction would erode them: the `callhangup` closing line, the complaint-tool technical-failure line, and the first-time registration-success line carrying the complaint number. The clause names those three as the carve-out explicitly. |
| **Empathy-ban interaction** | The block explicitly restates the CPL-05 ban (`समझ सकती हूँ`, `माफी चाहूँगी`, `खेद है`, `दुख हुआ`, `चिंता मत कीजिए`) because "acknowledge them better" is exactly the instruction that would otherwise pull a specialist agent into a banned empathy phrase. Acknowledgement here comes from being **specific and honest**, not from an empathy formula. |
| **Files (10)** | Every CRC agent holding `bpcl_create_complaint` — same list as CLS-01. |
| **CC** | Not yet ported, same as CLS-01. |

---

### XFER-02 · A no-transfer variant of `genericInfoComplaintAgent`, with the office number back on the complaint-failure path — CRC only, NOT DEPLOYED (2026-08-07) — **RETIRED by XFER-03 (2026-08-11): the file is deleted, see that row**

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Escalation. Client asked for a second version of the agent in which no consumer is ever routed to a person. |
| **Status** | **Variant file, not shipped.** `genericInfoComplaintAgent_noTransfer.txt` sits beside the deployed `genericInfoComplaintAgent.txt`; both now carry a `STATUS:` marker on line 1 (repo rule 6.6). Exactly one of the two can ship — the platform config decides, and nothing in the prompts can enforce it. The deployed file is unchanged apart from its marker. |
| **What it removes** | The whole `SENIOR TEAM TRANSFER` block and the T1–T4 moments; the `callTransferAgent` anchor line; `callTransferAgent` as a `switchagent` destination (`agentName` is now `routingAgent` and nothing else). A domain hand-off is the only reason it switches at all. |
| **What replaces it** | `THE COMPLAINT IS THE ESCALATION — THERE IS NO PERSON AT THE END OF THIS CALL`. A request for a person or a callback is answered by registering the complaint and saying the team will make contact; if it is already registered, by CLS-02's `NEVER THE SAME SENTENCE TWICE`, ending in honesty about the limit rather than a new promise. |
| **The failure path — reverts to CPL-07's outcome** | Client instruction, same session: on a `bpcl_create_complaint` FAILURE the variant gives **`{{crcOfficeNumber}}`** again, as the one real thing left. **Delivery differs from CPL-07 deliberately** — it uses this file's current `LONG NUMBER DELIVERY` (whole number in one go, English digit words, `" - "` between digits, three-piece split only on request), **not** the retired 3-3-4 dictation loop with per-piece check questions, which NUM-03 removed and which the rest of the file now directly contradicts. Guards kept from CPL-07: once per call, no retry, never promise the team will contact them (nothing was registered), never substitute the distributor's number, and no number at all if the variable is absent or brace-wrapped. |
| **The trap it is written against** | Removing the escape route without naming substitutes is what XFER-01 documented: the model fills the vacuum. The variant bans, explicitly, all five substitutes — claiming to connect/transfer/forward, naming a senior or team who will come on the line, narrating its own limitation as machinery (*"मैं आपकी call transfer नहीं कर सकती"*), inviting an office visit, and ending a registrable problem with *"थोड़ी देर बाद फिर call कीजिए"*. The `BANNED, AND WHAT I SAY INSTEAD` anchor menu is kept in full — it matters more here, not less. |
| **⚠️ Contradicts XFER-01** | XFER-01 deleted `{{crcOfficeNumber}}` channel-wide and `vaaniQA` **C5e** now scores *any* spoken number to reach us as a violation, in every agent. If this variant ships, C5e needs a carve-out for its failure path (the shape CPL-07's retired **C5e-FAIL** had) or every correct failure call is flagged. **Not done — the variant is not deployed.** |
| **⚠️ Platform dependency** | `{{crcOfficeNumber}}` was removed from this agent's injection set by XFER-01. It must be injected again for this file, or the failure path silently degrades to the failure line alone — exactly the calls it was written for. Same open dependency NUM-02/CPL-07 carried. |
| **Not ported** | The other 12 complaint-capable CRC prompts, `routingAgent`, and `Default` still route to `callTransferAgent`. A `Default` human-request still lands here expecting a transfer downstream and now gets a complaint instead — acceptable, but it means this variant is not a channel policy, only one agent's. |
| **CC** | Not applicable. CC has `calltransfer` inline and no `crcOffice*` variables. |

### NUM-04 · `getConsumerDetails_MobileVersion` is the deployed CRC entry agent; its office number is 12 digits with a country code — CRC only (2026-08-11) — **SUPERSEDED by XFER-03: that file is DELETED and `getConsumerDetails.txt` ships**

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Escalation. Client instruction: `calltransfer` is still not implemented on the platform, so this agent continues to hand out the office number instead of switching to `callTransferAgent`. |
| **Status** | **`getConsumerDetails_MobileVersion.txt` is DEPLOYED; `getConsumerDetails.txt` is not.** Both were unmarked until now; the deployed file gains a `STATUS:` line 1 marker (repo rule 6.6). **XFER-01 is ON HOLD for this agent** — its retirement of `{{crcOfficeNumber}}` and of the three-piece no-data dictation does not apply here until the platform ships `calltransfer`. The variable is live and injected for this agent, and this is the one live CRC prompt that speaks a number to reach us. |
| **The defect** | `{{crcOfficeNumber}}` arrives as `+` followed by **twelve** digits — country code 91 then the ten-digit number — but the prompt hard-coded a **ten**-digit shape in three places: the C2 split (*"first three, then the next three, then the last four"* = 10), all three C2 worked examples, and the C4 full read-back example. The model resolved the conflict between the template and the data by **discarding two digits, arbitrarily chosen** — reported live as "speaks only 10 of the 12, randomly". Not hallucination: it obeyed the prompt's structure over the prompt's value. |
| **Fixed by matching the shape to the data** | C2 is now **four pieces** — country code, then 3, 3, 4 — one per turn with the existing check question after each. All examples carry the country code (`"plus - nine - one"` as piece one), and C4 reads back all twelve. The `"+"` is spoken as the word *plus*, once, at the start; in Hindi digit mode it is `"plus - नौ - एक"`. |
| **The anchor rule** | `HOW TO SPEAK THE DIGITS` gains **SPEAK THE NUMBER EXACTLY AS THE VARIABLE HOLDS IT** — twelve digits, in order, add nothing, drop nothing, never shorten to ten, never "correct" it into the familiar ten-digit Indian shape, plus a self-check (*shorter than plus nine one and ten more digits → you dropped something*). This is the guard that survives if a future edit reintroduces a length-specific example. |
| **Why not strip the country code instead** | The rejected alternative was to speak the ten digits and tell the consumer to prefix `+91` if the call fails. That requires the model to **slice the variable** — the same operation already failing — and adds a conditional the consumer hears once and forgets while holding a pen. Reading the value through unchanged needs no arithmetic and no branch. |
| **New consumer-facing block** | `IF THEY ASK ABOUT THE plus nine one` — for *"यह plus nine one क्या है?"*, *"बारह digit का number होता है क्या?"*, *"plus कैसे लगाऊँ?"*. Answered once: it is India's country code, the full number is twelve digits with it, calls sometimes do not connect without it, so always dial with it in front of the ten digits. Then straight back to the dictation. |
| **The consumer may take ten digits — client instruction, 2026-08-11** | If they say they want only the ten digits (*"मैं सिर्फ़ दस digit लिखूँगा"*, *"plus nine one नहीं चाहिए"*) the agent **agrees** — no arguing, no repeated explanation. It gives the last **three pieces** (3-3-4), skipping piece one, and confirms those ten at C4 instead of twelve. The `+91` then rides as a one-time helpful aside, not a condition: *"अगर कभी call न लगे, तो number की शुरुआत में plus - nine - one लगा लीजिएगा।"* **Deliberately worded as "leave out piece one", never as "remove the first two digits"** — the agent reuses chunking it already has instead of slicing the variable, which is the operation this whole entry exists to stop. The three "never shorten to ten" rules (C2, C4, `HOW TO SPEAK THE DIGITS`) each carry the carve-out: the consumer asking is the only thing that shortens the number, the agent never chooses it. |
| **`IF THEY SAY THAT NUMBER DOESN'T WORK`** | Gains one check before the existing busy-team/timings reassurance: did they dial with the country code — asked once, skipped entirely if the plus-nine-one explanation was already given. The rest of that block (no second number, no complaint tool, no promise of a call) is unchanged. |
| **Scope** | The office number only. **STEP 1–4 are untouched** — the consumer's own number is still collected, confirmed and passed to `bpcl_fetch_all_api` as ten digits, with no country code anywhere in that path. Client instruction, explicit. |
| **NUM-03 adopted in part — the middle path (client decision, 2026-08-11)** | The full canonical shape (whole number in one go, no final read-back) was written, rejected, and reverted. What ships instead keeps this agent's dictation loop but removes the round trips inside it: **pen-and-paper opening turn → piece → wait → piece → wait → piece → wait → piece → wait → full read-back → "correct?" → wait.** A piece is delivered and the turn STOPS — **no check question attached to it**. The agent moves to the next piece on *any* signal ("आगे", "हाँ", "लिख लिया", a correct read-back), not a formal confirmation. `"क्या आपने note कर लिया?"` survives **only for silence**: if the consumer says nothing or is unintelligible after a piece, the agent waits once, then asks it as its own turn. It is never attached to a piece and never to a turn carrying no digits — named as a verbal tic. **The mandatory C4 read-back is kept**, deliberately against NUM-03: this number never arrives by SMS, so a mis-heard digit leaves the consumer unable to reach anyone. Deliberate divergence from the other 12 CRC files. |
| **Why the trigger is "silence", not "if unsure"** | The client's first wording was *ask if you are not sure they noted it*. Models cannot reliably judge their own uncertainty, so that trigger fires near-randomly and rebuilds the tic NUM-03 removed. Rewritten to an observable condition the agent can actually evaluate: the consumer's reply after a piece was silence or unintelligible. |
| **`SOURCE GATE` ported, adapted** | The canonical gate heads the closing: name the Result or the variable before speaking any number, or it was invented and is deleted. **The canonical sentence "there is no office number anywhere in my data" is deliberately removed here** — true in the other 12 agents, false in this one. Replaced with: the only acceptable answer for the office number is `{{crcOfficeNumber}}`; never a second number, alternate, helpline, email or app. |
| **Double-speak fixes ported from the XFER-01 sibling** | (1) `validatecontactno`'s Answer field is the tool talking to itself, never a directive — only the digits are taken from it; (2) a `HARD STOP CHECK` deleting anything after the first `"?"`; (3) the `CONFIRMATION GATE` widened from *no `bpcl_fetch_all_api`* to **no tool at all** on the confirmation turn, naming the re-called-`validatecontactno` path that makes the consumer hear the line twice; (4) the worked WRONG-turn example of the agent writing the consumer's *"हाँ"* for them; (5) *no reply text of any kind after the question mark*, plus the same widening in STEP 4's precondition. **Strengthened beyond the sibling at client request:** a new clause stating the agent's job on that turn is exactly two things — speak the number, ask for confirmation — and that the confirmation is the consumer's to give, out loud, covering the whole number. |
| **Tool blocker extended** | `bpcl_create_complaint` and `calltransfer` added to the forbidden list. No complaint tool (no record to attach one to, and it never offers to register one); `calltransfer` belongs to `callTransferAgent` — never called, named, or written, and the consumer is never told their call is being transferred or connected. |
| **`FAILED TURN` added** | Speaking a line that implies a tool ran, without invoking it that turn, is a failed turn — true of `validatecontactno`, `bpcl_fetch_all_api` and `callhangup` alike, because the platform never calls a tool for you. Own spoken line is never evidence; only a Result is. Recovery: invoke the tool first thing next turn. Same rule as CLAUDE.md §4 / CPL-03, scoped to this agent's tools. |
| **Verified, no change** | English digit words by default with a Hindi fallback only on consumer preference — the file already matched the canonical block and all 12 other CRC agents, so no divergence was introduced. |
| **CC** | Not applicable. CC has no `crcOffice*` variables and no office-number path — it transfers. |

### GCD-06 · A five-tool variant of `getConsumerDetails`, fetching each domain separately — CRC only, NOT DEPLOYED (2026-08-11) — **now the QA-environment tool-testing prompt per XFER-03**

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — data plumbing. Client asked for a version that calls the individual lookup tools instead of the single aggregate one. |
| **Status** | **Variant file, not shipped.** `getConsumerDetails_MultiToolVersion.txt` sits beside the deployed `getConsumerDetails_MobileVersion.txt`; both carry a `STATUS:` marker on line 1 (repo rule 6.6), as does the parked `getConsumerDetails.txt`. **Three files, exactly one ships** — the platform config decides and nothing in the prompts can enforce it. |
| **The only difference** | STEP 4. Instead of `bpcl_fetch_all_api` alone, it runs five tools one per turn in fixed order: `bpcl_get_consumer_details` → `bpcl_check_refill_status` → `bpcl_get_subsidy_details` → `bpcl_get_refill_history` → `bpcl_fetch_all_api`, all taking the same confirmed `contactNumber`. Everything else — number capture, the P1–P9 confirmation discipline, the `{{crcOfficeNumber}}` dictation closing, the plus-nine-one handling — is byte-identical and must stay that way. |
| **The turn-shape problem it solves** | Five tool calls means five turns. Left alone, the agent speaks on each one and the consumer hears five wait-lines for one lookup. The prompt fixes the shape explicitly: **one spoken line, on the first turn only** (`"जी, एक मिनट रुकिए…"`, which is a request to wait and therefore covers all five), and **turns two to five carry a tool call and no text at all**. It also bans narrating the sequence — no counting off, no naming a tool, no "one more check" — since that exposes machinery the consumer must never hear about, the same principle as invisible switching. |
| **The read problem it solves** | With one aggregate call, empty meant no record. With five, a registered consumer legitimately has empty results — no live booking, no subsidy, no refill history. STEP 5 is rewritten to read **all five Results together**: any real value anywhere means SUCCESS; only all five empty/errored means no record. Without this the agent would send real consumers to the no-data closing on the first empty Result. |
| **Tool blocker inverted** | The deployed file forbids these four by name and calls `bpcl_fetch_all_api` "the ONLY data-lookup tool". Here they are authorised, and the blocker instead fences *when*: STEP 4 only, in order, once each, never retried on error, never re-run later in the call. `bpcl_create_complaint` and `calltransfer` stay forbidden in both. |
| **⚠️ Open — needs the platform team** | Whether these four tools are actually exposed to this agent, and whether they accept `contactNumber` in the same shape `bpcl_fetch_all_api` does. If a tool is not registered the sequence stalls silently on that turn. Confirm before shipping. |
| **⚠️ Open — latency** | Five sequential round trips behind one "एक मिनट रुकिए" line. Not measurable from here. If the total wait runs long the consumer will speak into it, which the prompt handles (answer briefly, resume where it left off) but does not make pleasant. Worth timing before this variant is chosen over the deployed one. |
| **CC** | Not applicable. |
