### XFER-03 · `callTransferAgent` deleted; every routing-capable agent owns `calltransfer` itself — CRC only (2026-08-11)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Escalation. **Reverses XFER-01's central mechanism** — the dedicated transfer agent — while keeping everything XFER-01 decided about *when* a transfer is correct. Client instruction. |
| **Supersedes** | **XFER-01's topology**: the 16th agent, the *"`calltransfer` — NOT AUTHORIZED"* clause in every other prompt, the switch-to-`callTransferAgent` path, the platform's automatic invocation on switch, and the `handoffSummary`-carries-the-hours dependency. XFER-01's **four moments (T1–T4)**, its *never volunteer a transfer*, its *never re-ask someone who already asked*, and its complaint-first ordering are all **unchanged and still binding**. |
| **Change** | `calltransfer` is now held and invoked by **every agent that could previously switch to `callTransferAgent`**: the ten complaint-capable leaves, `newConnectionAgent_onHold`, `routingAgent`, and `getConsumerDetails`. There is no transfer agent, nothing to switch to, and no extra hop. `prompts/callTransferAgent/` is deleted. |
| **The transfer turn — one parameter, no spoken text** | `calltransfer` is invoked **exactly like `callhangup`**: the agent generates **no text of its own** on that turn and passes **exactly one parameter**, `preToolMessage: "आपकी कॉल ट्रांसफर की जा रही है।"`. Nothing else goes with it — no `agentName`, no `handoffSummary`, no `consumerQuery`, no `forwardingNumber`, no reason. Writing the line as text *as well* makes the consumer hear it twice; that is the specific bug the rule exists to prevent. |
| **The non-committal switch line is retired** | XFER-01 required a warm, non-committal line (`"जी बिल्कुल, एक मिनट।"`) on the switch turn precisely because the switching agent did not yet know whether the transfer would happen. There is no switch turn any more, so there is no line: the transfer turn is silent apart from the `preToolMessage`. `vaaniQA` **C5d** is rewritten from *"the switch line must not promise a transfer"* to *"the transfer turn carries no spoken text of her own"*. |
| **The `transfer` carve-out moves** | *"transfer"* was the one forbidden word `callTransferAgent` alone could say. It is now permitted **only inside that `preToolMessage`**, in every agent that holds the tool, and nowhere else in an agent's own speech. |
| **Who handles a failed transfer** | The calling agent, in place — the same two-outcome shape `callTransferAgent` had. **Success** → it produces **nothing**: no text, no goodbye, and **no `callhangup`** (the platform closes the call; hanging up there cuts the consumer off from the person they were just connected to). **Not success** — out-of-office-hours, platform failure, busy, no-answer → no tool that turn; it says the team could not be reached, states when they can be reached, and **does not close in the same breath as the bad news**. It waits, keeps answering, and calls `callhangup` only once the consumer accepts. |
| **Hours now come from the tool Result** | XFER-01 read the window out of `handoffSummary`, which no longer reaches anything on this path. The `calltransfer` **Result** carries the reason and, where there is one, the window; the agent converts it to Hindi words. **If the Result gives no window the agent names none** — *"अभी हमारी senior team से सम्पर्क नहीं हो पा रहा, कृपया कुछ समय बाद call कीजिए।"* Inventing a window would land the consumer on a line nobody answers. **This closes XFER-01's open platform dependency (3)** — nothing needs to write hours into `handoffSummary` any more. |
| **Still no number to give** | On the failure branch a consumer who asks for a number to call gets *"मेरे पास कोई number नहीं है, मैं सिर्फ़ आपकी call transfer कर सकती हूँ।"* No agent holds an office number; `{{crcOfficeNumber}}` does not exist in this channel. |
| **One attempt per call, never retried** | Unchanged from XFER-01, but now enforced per agent: `calltransfer` is called at most once in a call, and never a second time on the consumer's insistence. |
| **Topology exception removed** | XFER-01's recorded exception — *"every leaf may also switch to `callTransferAgent`"* — is gone. The shared-truth rule is restored in full: **every specialist is a leaf whose only `switchagent` target is `routingAgent`**, and no leaf switches to another leaf. Reaching a person is a tool call, not a switch. |
| **Who still never transfers** | `Default` (routes a human request to `genericInfoComplaintAgent` first, so the issue gets registered) and `emergencyAgent` (a live hazard outranks everything). Both keep an explicit *"`calltransfer` — NOT AUTHORIZED"* clause, reworded so it no longer points at a deleted agent. **XFER-02's `genericInfoComplaintAgent_noTransfer` variant is deleted** (2026-08-11, same session): it existed for a no-human config that this change moves decisively away from, and its one distinguishing fallback — the office number on a failed complaint — depended on `{{crcOfficeNumber}}`, which does not exist in this channel. XFER-02's own ⚠️ rows recorded that dependency and it was never resolved, so its failure path would have degraded to the bare failure line. Recoverable from git history if the requirement returns. |
| **`getConsumerDetails` deployment flip** | `getConsumerDetails.txt` — rewritten here to call `calltransfer` at C2 of its NO-DATA CLOSING — **is now the deployed file**. `getConsumerDetails_MultiToolVersion.txt` becomes the **QA-environment tool-testing prompt** (GCD-06's five-tool fetch). `getConsumerDetails_MobileVersion.txt` is **deleted** (NUM-04's twelve-digit dictation closing goes with it), as is `prompts/callTransferAgent/` — both live in git history only, and the CRC prompt tree is back to 15 agents. The remaining `STATUS:` markers rewritten. |
| **QA and analysis prompts** | `vaaniQA`: the data-line and **C1**, **C3c**, **C5d**, **C5h** rewritten off the transfer agent onto the inline tool; C5h is now *"after the `calltransfer` tool returns"* and reads the branch off the Result. `postCallAnalysisFlat`: the three `callTransferAgent` references rewritten; the `callTransferred` field and its `"yes"` = fully-succeeded rule are **unchanged**. |
| **⚠️ Open — needs the platform team** | **(1)** `calltransfer` must be exposed to all thirteen agents listed above, accepting `preToolMessage` alone — the `forwardingNumber` is now the platform's to supply, not the prompt's. **(2)** The tool's **Result** must reach the calling agent on the next turn, carrying success/failure and, on failure, a reason and — where one exists — the office-hours window; the failure branch is written against it. **(3)** The platform must close the call itself on a **successful** transfer, since the agent deliberately calls no tool there. **(4)** `callTransferAgent` must be de-registered as an agent. |
| **⚠️ Open — `getConsumerDetails_MultiToolVersion`** | As the QA prompt it still carries the `{{crcOfficeNumber}}` dictation closing, and that variable no longer exists. Its no-data ending needs porting to the `calltransfer` ending before QA can test the shipped flow. Flagged in the file's own `STATUS:` line, not fixed here. |
| **CC** | Entirely untouched. CC has always had `calltransfer` inline in ten agents; CRC's model is now **closer** to CC's, but not identical — CC passes `consumerQuery` and speaks a warm line via its own `preToolMessage`, and CC's failure path captures a callback slot, which CRC still has no concept of. |

---

### GCD-07 · The confirmation question fired twice across two turns, and '6' switched language mid-number — both channels (2026-08-17)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — shared truth (turn discipline + the TTS number rules). Landed in **CC and CRC together**, plus CRC's QA variant. |
| **What the live call did** | Two defects in `getConsumerDetails`, from two separate CRC calls. **(1)** The confirmation line was spoken twice, back to back, with nothing in between, and only then did the consumer answer: *"कृपया पुष्टि कीजिए, आपका मोबाइल नंबर eight - nine - … - eight है?"* twice, then *"हाँ, यह मेरा ही नंबर है।"* **(2)** A number came out language-mixed: *"seven - nine - nine - nine - eight - **छह** - five - five - **छह** - eight"*. |
| **Why the repeat happened** | The prompt **licensed it.** The confirmation-gate paragraph ended *"Wait, **or ask the one confirmation question again if it clearly was not heard**, and keep waiting"* — directly contradicting the rule ten lines above it (*"Never re-read the same confirmation … never re-confirm on silence"*). That escape branch fires in exactly the situation the transcript shows: the agent's turn came up before the consumer's reply had landed, so there was no affirmative to act on, and the prompt offered re-asking as a legal move. A ban and a permission that contradict each other resolve in favour of the permission — the same failure mode as CLS-03. |
| **Why nothing caught it** | Both mechanical checks in that file inspect **the current output only** — *"find the LAST `?` in your output, is there any text after it"* and *"does your output contain the confirmation sentence more than once"*. This failure spans **two turns**, each individually one clean sentence ending at `?`. No rule anywhere in either channel's copy was keyed on the agent's **own previous turn**. Note this is a different bug from the heavily-fortified self-answering one (question + fabricated *"हाँ, सही है।"* inside one output); that guard was working. |
| **The rule now** | The re-ask escape is **deleted**: no affirmative means wait, full stop, including when it seems the question was not heard — it was asked, it still stands, and the consumer is allowed to take their time. A **CROSS-TURN CHECK** is added above the existing hard stop: read back your own previous turn, and if it already carried the confirmation question for this number, you do not send it again whatever arrived (or did not arrive) since. Two turns each holding one clean confirmation is the same defect as one turn holding it twice. |
| **The silent turn is stated as legal** | *"Simply wait"* only works if the model believes producing nothing is an allowed output, and nothing in either file said so. It now does — with a fallback for a platform that requires text: one short, freshly worded waiting line (*"सुन रही हूँ।"*), never the confirmation sentence, never the digits, never the same waiting line twice. |
| **Why '6' switched language** | The rule was written as a **digit-level substitution** rather than a language choice for the whole number. CRC's copy carried *"- In Hindi, '6' is छह"* standing unconditionally in CRITICAL REMINDERS, with the conditional form 120 lines earlier. Generating an English sequence, the model hit a live rule saying *6 → छह* and applied it token-wise. Nothing in either file said a number is spoken in **one** language end to end. |
| **The rule now** | **ONE LANGUAGE PER NUMBER — NEVER MIXED**, chosen once before the first digit and held to the last. In an English number 6 is **"six"**, with no exception; `"छह"` belongs only to a number spoken entirely in Hindi digit words. The live broken string is quoted in the prompt as the named counter-example. Both the TTS block and the reminder line rewritten so neither can be read standalone. |
| **Files touched (4)** | CRC `getConsumerDetails.txt` (deployed) and `getConsumerDetails_MultiToolVersion.txt` (QA), CC `getConsumerDetails.txt`, plus this ledger. CC had no re-ask escape clause but carried the identical within-turn-only check and the identical digit rule, so it took both fixes. |
| **Still open — for the platform team** | Does the runtime allow the agent to return an **empty turn**, or does it require text every time it fires? If text is mandatory, no prompt wording fully closes the repeat: on a fragment turn the model must say *something*, and the nearest thing to hand is the line it just said. The fallback waiting line is written for that case, but the underlying question needs answering. |
| **Follow-up the same day — the trigger becomes the tool Result** | Client instruction: the confirmation sentence should fire **only** off `validatecontactno`'s Result. Implemented, and it is a stronger rule than the cross-turn check it now outranks — it converts the guard from **memory** ("did I already ask?") into an **event edge** ("has the one licensing event just arrived?"). Once the agent has asked, the newest item in the transcript is its own turn rather than a fresh Result, so on every later turn the trigger is simply absent; fragments, silence, timeouts and unclear replies all fire the model with no Result at the edge and therefore cannot produce the sentence. It interlocks with the existing never-call-`validatecontactno`-twice rule — one Result, one ask — and that interlock is now stated in the prompt, because a second call would manufacture a second trigger. The cross-turn check is kept but demoted to a **backstop**. The **corrected-number** path is stated explicitly as the one legitimate second confirmation: corrected digits go through `validatecontactno` again, that Result is a fresh trigger, and the sentence is not a repeat because the digits changed. |
| **Why the memory-based guard alone was not enough** | The model sees the text it generated; it does **not** see whether TTS finished playing it. On a barge-in or dropped audio, generated ≠ heard — so "did I already say this" infers an audio event from a text artefact. It also fails outright if two invocations race or the platform re-fires the same state, since the first output may not be in the second's context to inspect. **Known risk of the backstop, unresolved:** it can produce the opposite failure — the question was generated but never heard, and the agent now declines to ever ask again and waits indefinitely. STEP 3 has no dead-line escape (the three-nudge rule at C3 belongs to the NO-DATA CLOSING), so a never-heard confirmation can hang the call. Deliberately **not** patched by inventing a hangup path here; needs a decision. |
| **Not touched** | The `DIGIT WORDS` blocks in the other twelve CRC agents carry the same *"in Hindi '6' is छह"* phrasing, but inside a conditional sentence rather than standing alone — the same hazard, weaker. Not yet observed firing there. Worth a sweep if it shows up again. |

---

### XFER-04 · The transfer offer outlived the attempt — `getConsumerDetails` re-offered a connect after its own transfer failed (2026-08-17)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Axis 1 — escalation. **CRC only**; CC's failure path captures a callback slot and behaves differently, so this does not port as written. |
| **Amends** | **XFER-03's** once-per-call rule, which turned out to constrain only half of what needed constraining. |
| **What the live call did** | `getConsumerDetails` found no record, offered the connect, transferred on a yes, and the transfer failed. It then read the whole failure line back verbatim a second time when the consumer complained about being talked over, appended a close-check question it does not own, and — when the consumer moved on and asked about a delivery date — **offered the transfer again**: *"हमारी senior team आपकी सहायता कर सकती है — क्या मैं आपकी कॉल उन तक ट्रांसफर कर दूँ?"* |
| **Why the existing rule did not catch it** | Every agent forbade **calling** `calltransfer` twice. Only the ten complaint-capable leaves also forbade **asking** twice (*"I ASK THIS ONCE PER CALL. Having asked, I do not raise it again on later turns."*). `getConsumerDetails` had no such clause — and it is the one agent for which the connect offer is the **only** answer it has to any question, since it holds no data, no complaint tool and no phone number. A new query therefore routed straight back into the offer, by design rather than by accident. |
| **The rule now** | **The offer dies with the attempt.** Once `calltransfer` has been attempted, the C1 connect question is spent for the rest of the call — not in the same words, not softened, not as a different route to a person, not for a different reason, and **not because the consumer raised something new**. Offering a connect that can no longer be made asks the consumer to say yes to something that cannot happen, and forces a second refusal. |
| **A new question is not a new reason** | Explicit, because this is the shape the loop took: after a failed transfer the consumer usually drops it and asks something else. The answer is that Vaani has no record and the team could not be reached just now, freshly worded, with a request to call back later — never the connect question again. |
| **Two smaller defects from the same call** | **(1)** The failure line was read back **verbatim** on the next turn, window and all; it is now say-it-once, then acknowledge briefly and answer what was actually said. **(2)** `getConsumerDetails` asked *"क्या मैं आपकी कोई और सहायता कर सकती हूँ?"* — it holds **no close-check question** at all, and asking one only invites a query it will have to refuse. Its ending is C4. |
| **`newConnectionAgent_onHold` brought to parity** | It carried the bare *"ONE ATTEMPT PER CALL"* line with no guidance on what to say when asked again — the one gap `routingAgent` had already closed. It now carries the same tell-them-the-truth answer and the same new-question-is-not-a-new-reason clause. It never *offers* a transfer, so the offer half does not apply to it. |
| **Not touched** | The ten leaves already carry the ask-once rule and were left alone. `routingAgent` never offers on its own initiative and already handles the ask-again case. |
| **QA side** | `vaaniQA` **C5i** now scores the **re-offer** as a violation in its own right, independent of whether a second tool call followed — including when a brand-new query prompted it — plus the verbatim re-read of the failure line. |
| **⚠️ Unverified, worth checking with the platform team** | The failure line in that call named *सोमवार से शुक्रवार, सुबह दस बजे से शाम छः बजे*. Every prompt forbids speaking office hours from memory and requires the window to come from the tool's **Result** — and that window does not match this channel's documented hours (all weekdays, 9am–7pm). Either the Result carried it, in which case the two sources disagree, or the model invented it, which is a separate and more serious defect. Not fixable from the prompts without knowing which. |
| **Files touched (5)** | `getConsumerDetails`, `newConnectionAgent_onHold`, `vaaniQA`, plus this ledger — and `unregisteredComplaintAgent`, which exists on the UNREG-01 branch only and took the same offer-dies clause there. |

---

### CLS-03 · The close question fired on eleven consecutive turns, and the empathy ban left nothing to say instead — CRC only, CC pending (2026-08-11)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — shared truth. **CRC only** per client instruction this session; **CC pending**, same as CLS-01 and CLS-02. |
| **Source** | Live CRC transcript, 11-08-2026 12:04–12:11. Consumer's cylinder undelivered since Thursday; complaint registered correctly at 12:05:59, second complaint at 12:08:05. Over the next six minutes Vaani asked *"क्या कुछ और L P G से related help चाहिए?"* **eleven times** — 12:05:59, 12:06:17, 12:06:36, 12:07:05, 12:07:20, 12:07:31, 12:08:19, 12:08:32, 12:10:04, 12:10:45, 12:11:19 — at a man who was still describing his problem on every one of them. |
| **Why CLS-01's HARD GATE did not catch it** | CLS-01 already forbids the close question in the same turn as anything else, and most agents already carry that gate verbatim. It failed because the **positive** condition sitting three lines below it said a topic is fully resolved *"or a complaint is registered and the number shared"*. The number was spoken at 12:05:59, so by the prompt's own definition the topic was resolved in that instant and the close question was legal from the next turn onward. **A ban and a permission that contradict each other resolve in favour of the permission.** |
| **The fix — the gate is the consumer's last turn** | That clause is deleted from all four "fully resolved when" definitions. A topic is now resolved only when **the consumer's own most recent turn carried nothing further** — no question, no new fact, no fresh grievance. An acknowledgement or wind-down ("ठीक है", "अच्छा", "ok", "समझ गया", a thank-you, a goodbye) is what qualifies. Everything else means answer it, stop, wait. **A turn in which the agent answers something ends with a full stop, never with this question.** Stated as a `WHEN THE CLOSE QUESTION IS ALLOWED` block under the existing `ANSWERING IS NOT RESOLVING` paragraph in all twelve agents that hold a close question. |
| **A registered complaint is explicitly NOT a resolved topic** | Named as its own clause, because that is the exact inference the model made. Speaking the complaint number licenses nothing — the consumer has just been told their problem is on paper and almost always has more to say. |
| **If they never wind down, it is never asked** | Deliberate, and the client's call: there is no turn cap, no convergence ladder, and no forced close. The agent keeps answering and lets the consumer end the call. A call where the close question is never asked is CORRECT, and `vaaniQA` C16 says so explicitly so it is not flagged as a missing step. |
| **The empathy ban is retired (reverses CPL-05's blanket form)** | The same transcript shows Vaani saying "समझ सकती हूँ", "मुझे खेद है", "क्षमा चाहती हूँ", "आपकी नाराज़गी जायज़ है" throughout — **the ban was already dead in production**, and the model reaches for warmth regardless. What made the call hollow was not missing empathy but empathy carrying nothing: eight warm sentences with no fact in any of them. So the ban and its five-phrase list are deleted from every agent that carried them, and **no replacement script is given** — the wording is the model's own, freshly generated (client instruction). |
| **The one rule that replaces it** | Every warm sentence carries a fact, an answer, or a concrete next step with it. Warmth alone, on a turn where the consumer asked something, is worse than none. Acknowledge an issue **once**; never re-acknowledge the same feeling on later turns; never reuse an acknowledgement already spoken in the call. Never apologise on the distributor's behalf and never pass judgement on the distributor or their staff — stay with what happened to this consumer (client instruction, same session). |
| **Two rules scoped so warmth is possible at all** | *"I never repeat back what the consumer just told me"* now excludes naming, in the agent's own words, the specific thing that happened — that is acknowledgement, not echo, and being specific is what makes a person feel heard. *"Never start with जी / अच्छा"* now permits them where they genuinely fit; a blanket ban on the two words natural Hindi acknowledgement begins with was pushing the model into the flat register this row exists to remove. |
| **Post-call analysers** | Both `postCallAnalysisFlat` and `postCallAnalysisHuman` carried a CONTEXT paragraph reading *"There is no call transfer and no scheduled callback in this system — no senior team exists to hand the call to live"*, plus the deleted `{{crcOfficeNumber}}` dictation as the no-record path. False since **XFER-01** and self-contradictory in Flat, which describes `calltransfer` sixty lines further down. Both CONTEXT blocks rewritten for the XFER-03 inline transfer, and *"a CRC the consumer can visit in person"* corrected to the regional-head framing ESC-01 and the 2026-08-05 persona reversal already require. No CLS-03 scoring was added to Flat — Flat describes a call, it does not judge the agent. |
| **QA** | `vaaniQA` **C3j** inverted — empathy is no longer reportable as a phrase; only three things are: warmth with no fact on a turn where the consumer asked something, the same acknowledgement repeated, and blame placed on the distributor. **C16 added** for the close question, reporting same-turn gluing, asking while the consumer is still going, and repeat asks with a count. A12 scoped to the switch turn. Without this pass every improved call would be flagged as a violation. |
| **Files (15)** | All ten complaint-capable agents, `newConnectionAgent_onHold`, `Default`, both `getConsumerDetails` variants, and `vaaniQA`. The parked `newConnectionAgent.txt` is untouched per its DO-NOT-EDIT marker. |
| **Deliberately NOT changed** | The doubled closing line at 12:11:41 (`callhangup` text + `preToolMessage`), Vaani asking permission to end the call at 12:10:29, her refusal to state the delay in days, her refusal to note the delivery code, and the unbacked *"मैं इसे उसी में जोड़ रही हूँ"* at 12:10:12 — all reviewed with the client and left as-is this session. The cumulative-looking consumer transcript was confirmed to be a display artefact, not an ASR fault. |
| **CC** | Not yet ported. CC carries the identical close-question definition and the identical empathy ban, and is exposed to both. |

---

### PCA-01 · `callResult` gains a fifth value, `callTransferred`, and is decided at the end of the call — CRC only, CC pending (2026-08-11)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — post-call analysis. **Reverses XFER-01's deliberate no-new-enum-value decision.** Client instruction; client confirms there is no downstream config issue with a fifth value. |
| **Supersedes** | XFER-01's mapping of a transfer onto the existing four values, and its stated reason (*"no new enum value was added, deliberately, so nothing downstream breaks"*). The `callTransferred` **field** is unchanged and still `"yes"`/`"no"`. |
| **The new ladder** | `callTransferred` > `complaintRegistered` > `complaintFailed` > `issueResolved` > `unresolved`. A successful transfer **outranks a complaint registered earlier in the same call** — it is the only outcome where the call physically left the agent and ended somewhere else, so it is what the call ended as. |
| **Decided last, not first** | `callResult` is now explicitly settled **after reading the whole transcript end to end**, never on the first matching event, and never on an outcome that was later undone — a complaint claimed then reported failed is a failure, an answer later contradicted is not an answer, a transfer that did not go through is not a transfer. The `complaintRegistered` field already carried this instruction (*"let the last thing that happened win"*); `callResult` never did, and used a bare priority ranking instead. |
| **Why the ladder was kept rather than pure last-state-wins** | Literal "whatever happened last" loses the complaint signal: a complaint registered at two minutes, followed by an unrelated question answered cleanly at eight, would score `issueResolved` and the complaint would vanish from the disposition. The ladder is a ranking of **what the call ended as**, applied once, at the end. |
| **A failed transfer is never `callTransferred`** | The agent still has the call, so it ends however it actually ends — `complaintRegistered` if one was registered earlier, `unresolved` if the customer was left with nothing. Keeps `callResult` and the `callTransferred` field in permanent agreement: one is `"callTransferred"` iff the other is `"yes"`. |
| **`fcr` on a transferred call is always `"no"`** | Two reasons, both stated in the prompt. First contact resolution excludes calls handed to anyone else, by standard definition. And it is unobservable: the transcript stops at the moment the transfer succeeds, so `"yes"` would assert something the transcript cannot show. The existing *"immediate need was met"* instruction is about an answer versus a complete fix and explicitly does not license `"yes"` here. A failed transfer is `"no"` too. |
| **`csat` on a transferred call** | Bounded, not fixed — still predicted from observable tone. Default **3**; **4** when the customer stayed cooperative and the transfer came promptly on their first ask; **2**/**1** when they asked repeatedly, were angry, or repeated themselves many times first. **Never 5** — 5 requires a resolved issue, and nothing was resolved on this call. A **failed** transfer caps at **2**, **1** if they were angry. A transfer never raises the score on its own: it is a route, not a resolution. |
| **`followUpRequired` / `callbackPriority`** | A successful transfer is `"no"` / `"none"` — a human owns the call and nobody needs to call back a customer already speaking to a person — **unless** a complaint from earlier in the call is still open, which makes it `"yes"` / `"medium"`. A failed transfer is at least `"medium"`, `"high"` if they were angry. |
| **Files** | `postCallAnalysisFlat` only. `vaaniQA` is unaffected — it judges the agent, not the disposition. **`postCallAnalysisHuman` deliberately NOT given the value**: it has neither the `callTransferred` field nor any transfer mapping, and whether it needs them depends on the unresolved root-`CLAUDE.md` §2b question of whether it also runs on Vaani calls. |
| **CC** | Not ported. CC's `postCallAnalysisFlat` keeps the four-value enum. CC transfers inline from ten agents and has the same reporting gap, so this is worth porting with the CLS rows. |

---

### CPL-14 · A complaint and its number were fabricated in a single turn; `calltransfer` capped at one attempt and barred from switching after failure — CRC only, CC pending (2026-08-12)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — shared truth, three separate live defects from 12-08-2026 CRC transcripts. |
| **Defect 1 — the fabricated complaint** | `connectionServicesAgent`, 10:00:20, **one turn**: *"एक मिनट रुकिए, आपकी शिकायत दर्ज कर रही हूँ … आपकी शिकायत दर्ज हो गई है। आपका शिकायत नंबर है आठ - नौ - छह - सात - पांच - चार - तीन - दो - एक - शून्य।"* No tool ran. The whole registration was narrated and the number invented — note the digits run in a near-perfect descending sequence, and they were spoken in **Hindi** digit words, violating NUM-01 as well. |
| **Why every existing rule missed it** | CPL-03, CPL-11 and CPL-12 all attack the same failure from the same angle — *"speaking the line is not invoking the tool"* — and all of them assume the agent narrates the **registering** step and then stops. None of them said the thing that makes this instance impossible: **a Result cannot arrive on the turn the tool was invoked**, so the success line and the registering line can never share a turn. The prompts described the two turns separately but never stated that they are two. |
| **The fix — a structural test, not another prohibition** | New clause in all ten complaint-capable agents, directly under `THE TOOL TURN`: the registering line and the complaint number are **never in the same turn, and a turn containing both is proof no tool ran**. THIS turn: registering line + tool call, and nothing else — no number, no "दर्ज हो गई है", no SMS sentence, no "team संपर्क करेगी". NEXT turn, and only against a real Result: the success line. Plus the tell — *digits running in a neat ascending or descending sequence are the giveaway that they were generated rather than read*. |
| **Defect 2 — the agent wrote the consumer's reply** | `getConsumerDetails`, same day: *"कृपया पुष्टि कीजिए, आपका मोबाइल नंबर seven - zero - one … है? **हाँ, सही है।**"* — it asked for confirmation and then supplied the confirmation itself, then acted on it. The file's `HARD STOP CHECK` already forbade exactly this, naming "a हाँ" specifically, and it was violated anyway. **A rule that specific failing means placement and framing, not wording.** So it moves to the top of the prompt as an identity-level rule — *you invented a consumer who agreed with you, and then acted on it; this is fabricating consent* — with a purely mechanical pre-send test: find the last `?`, delete everything after it. Applied to both `getConsumerDetails` files. |
| **Defect 3 — a second transfer, and a switch after a failed one** | Live calls showed `calltransfer` attempted more than once, and the agent switching to another agent after a transfer failed. **Both now banned outright in all thirteen transfer-holding agents.** One attempt per call, full stop — once attempted the tool is finished for that call whatever the Result. On a repeat request the agent says she already tried and the team could not be reached, freshly worded, and never pretends to try again. |
| **Never switch after a failed transfer — the reasoning matters** | `switchagent` is not a recovery. The consumer has just been told they cannot have the person they asked for; handing them to a fresh agent that holds none of the conversation makes them start the entire story over, at the worst possible moment. The agent that attempted the transfer already has everything — it stays, answers what it can from that context, and registers anything registrable it has not already registered. |
| **QA** | `vaaniQA` **C17** (a number sharing a turn with the registering line is fabricated — highest severity, plausibility irrelevant) and **C18** (a second `calltransfer` anywhere in a call; any `switchagent` following a failed transfer; staying and continuing to help is correct and never reportable). |
| **Files (16)** | Ten complaint-capable agents (defect 1), thirteen transfer-holding agents (defect 3), both `getConsumerDetails` files (defect 2), `vaaniQA`. |
| **CC** | Not ported. CC's complaint path carries the identical CPL-03 framing and the identical gap, and CC transfers inline from ten agents with no one-attempt cap. |

---

### RTE-03 · `Default` classifies before it falls back, and probes once before routing a bare request for a person — CRC only, CC pending (2026-08-12)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — routing. Live calls were landing in `genericInfoComplaintAgent` almost regardless of what the consumer said. |
| **Two structural causes** | **(1)** `INTERRUPT B` routed *every* human request to `genericInfoComplaintAgent` **on the same turn**, with no attempt to learn what the problem was — a large share of calls by itself. **(2)** `genericInfoComplaintAgent` is defined as *"the unified agent for all generic information and complaints not owned by a dedicated agent"*, so any turn where triage was skipped or the intent was fuzzy landed there safely. A catch-all that accepts anything becomes the path of least resistance. |
| **The pattern already existed** | `STAGE 2B` is a refill triage that *"uses conversational questions to classify intent"* and is an explicit carve-out from Default's no-questions rule. This row generalises that pattern to the front of the call rather than inventing a mechanism. |
| **CLASSIFY BEFORE YOU FALL BACK** | New block above the STAGE 3 routing table: before routing anything to the catch-all, Default walks the owner list — booking failure, a problem with an actual delivery, money in any form, subsidy/DBTL, connection services, new connection, hazard — and routes to the first that fits. `genericInfoComplaintAgent` is reachable **only when none fits**. Stated with the reason, since a rule without one erodes: routing an owned query to the catch-all costs the consumer an agent that had their data and their flow, and makes them explain themselves again to get nowhere. |
| **One probe on a bare request for a person** | If the consumer has already described a problem anywhere in the call, Default does **not** ask — it classifies and routes. If it does not know the issue, it asks exactly **one** question and that question is the whole turn, no tool call. On the next turn it routes on whatever came back. **Never a second question**: if they named nothing placeable or declined ("बस connect करो"), it routes to `genericInfoComplaintAgent` immediately, because pressing twice is the stalling the never-re-ask rule exists to stop. Client's call on the budget of one. |
| **The constraint this is designed around** | Default must not gather facts — its own rules forbid collecting dates, days elapsed, amounts or quantities before a switch, and `vaaniQA` **C11** reports exactly that. So the probe is scoped to the **topic** and nothing else: what the problem is, never a date, an amount, a booking number or a complaint number. Default finds out WHO should help; the specialist gathers the case. Without that scoping this row would have built the behaviour C11 flags. |
| **Distributor details are treated as a symptom** | A request for the distributor's name, address or number gets the same single probe first — it is usually a cylinder that has not come, a booking that failed, or money taken. If a problem is named, it routes to the agent that owns it; only a genuine bare contact-detail request goes to the catch-all. `Default` still holds none of that data (DATA-01, unchanged). |
| **`routingAgent` deliberately unchanged** | Client's call. Mid-call it usually has a `handoffSummary` and more context, so it misclassifies less, and it is meant to be the silent re-router. It will still send ambiguous queries to the catch-all — a known, accepted gap, not drift. |
| **QA** | `vaaniQA` **C19** (an owned query routed to the catch-all — not reportable when the query is genuinely generic or the consumer declined to describe it) and **C20** (a bare request for a person routed or escalated with no probe; a second probe after they answered or declined; asking for specifics on the probe turn, which is already a C11 violation). |
| **Files (2)** | `Default.txt`, `vaaniQA.txt`. |
| **CC** | Not ported. CC's `Default` has the same catch-all pull and the same same-turn escalation route. |

---

### RTE-04 · `genericInfoComplaintAgent` answers from `{{customerStatus}}` before it registers; `Default` stops advertising the catch-all — CRC only, CC pending (2026-08-12)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — routing and complaint discipline. Follows RTE-03, same reported symptom: calls landing in the catch-all, and the catch-all acting on the consumer's sentence without ever looking at its own data. |
| **Amends CPL-04** | CPL-04's grievance carve-out let a grievance skip the whole `RESOLUTION LADDER` and register directly. Because nearly everything reaching this agent *sounds* like a grievance about something that already happened, the carve-out fired every time and step 2 — *"can I resolve it from my own data?"* — never ran. **The carve-out no longer skips the data.** |
| **Same defect class as the 2026-08-05 decision** | The `activeDeliveryAgent` case (grievance carve-out vs Check 4, complaint registered on turn one while the backend said delivery was in process) was diagnosed and the fix **declined** as rare. It has now been reported as widespread in a different agent. **Client scoped this fix to `genericInfoComplaintAgent` and `Default` only** — the other nine agents keep the existing carve-out, so the August decision stands where it was made. |
| **The new order in `genericInfoComplaintAgent`** | Read `{{customerStatus}}` before acting on anything — before a complaint, before a transfer, before answering. Give the **data-backed answer first**, in plain Hindi. Stop, let them respond. Register only if they still want it, if they say the record is wrong, if the record confirms the problem, or if the record holds nothing useful. **The same order applies before a transfer**: never move a consumer to a person on a problem that was never looked up. |
| **What did NOT change** | Where the record adds nothing, a grievance still registers directly — no stalling, no interrogation, no making them ask twice. The change is that the agent *looks* first, never that it refuses to register. Stated explicitly in the prompt, because the obvious way to over-correct this is to start withholding complaints. |
| **`Default` stops advertising the catch-all** | RTE-03 made Default check owners first but left `genericInfoComplaintAgent`'s routing entry at six lines of *"Routes:"* — generic booking queries, generic delivery queries, equipment, complaints, escalations. **That list was itself the attractor**: it reads as a match for almost anything. It is now two lines that describe no topics at all — reached only by elimination, with an explicit *NOT this agent, ever* list naming every owned domain. You cannot make an agent unattractive while enumerating everything it accepts. |
| **The name is the other half, and is out of reach** | "generic" invites everything, but `agentName` must match the platform registry, so renaming is a config change, not a prompt one. Not attempted. |
| **Deliberately not done** | An owner list inside `genericInfoComplaintAgent` so it bounces misrouted queries — client's call: its existing routing rule stays, and the data instruction carries the weight instead. |
| **QA** | `vaaniQA` **C21** — a complaint or transfer on the first turn against a stated problem, with no data-backed answer and no sign the record was read. Not reportable when the record genuinely holds nothing, or when the consumer asked for a complaint outright. |
| **Files (3)** | `genericInfoComplaintAgent.txt`, `Default.txt`, `vaaniQA.txt`. |
| **CC** | Not ported. CC has no `{{customerStatus}}` equivalent in this agent and its own catch-all pull is unexamined. |

---

### CPL-15 · The complaint number is what decides success or failure — two outcomes, never a third — CRC only, CC pending (2026-08-12)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — shared truth. Closes the hole CPL-14 left open. |
| **The report** | Another fabricated registration, from a 13:38 transcript on 12-08-2026 — a delivery-OTP complaint narrated end to end in one turn: *"एक मिनट रुकिए, आपकी शिकायत दर्ज की जा रही है … आपकी शिकायत दर्ज हो गई है। आपका शिकायत नंबर है पाँच - सात - आठ - नौ - दो - एक - चार - तीन - दो - एक।"* Registering line, success line and a ten-digit number in a single breath, in **Hindi** digit words again (NUM-01). |
| **What CPL-14 already covers** | This exact turn shape. CPL-14 landed at 16:24 the same day and the transcript is from 13:38, so it is **pre-fix evidence**, not a survival of the fix. Its structural test — *the registering line and the number are never in the same turn, and a turn containing both is proof no tool ran* — makes this output impossible. Note the digits here run `5 7 8 9 2 1 4 3 2 1`, no tidy sequence: CPL-14's "neat ascending or descending run" tell would have missed it, and the turn-shape test would not. **The tell is a secondary signal; the structure is the rule.** |
| **What was genuinely missing** | The prompts had two branches — success → speak the number, failure → the technical-problem line — but never said **what sorts a Result into one or the other**. Success was left as an unstated property of the Result, and nothing covered a Result that comes back looking successful while carrying no readable number: field absent, empty, curly-braced, or a placeholder. The success gate said *"if I cannot point to it there, I do not have one and I speak none"* — correct, and it stops there. It tells the agent what **not** to say and never what to say instead, and a model standing in that gap with a consumer waiting fills it. |
| **The fix — the number is the test, and there is no third state** | New clause in all ten complaint-capable agents, directly under the CPL-14 clause. **A real number in the Result is SUCCESS; no number is FAILURE** — and "no number" covers every way it can be missing: the tool errored, no Result came back, the field is absent, empty, blank, curly-braced, or a placeholder. It is failure **whatever else the Result says, including the word success**. The two branch labels now name the test rather than an abstract outcome: *On SUCCESS — a real complaint number came back in the Result* / *On FAILURE — no complaint number came back, for any reason*. Nothing new to route to: the failure branch is the existing one, unchanged. |
| **NO NUMBER MEANS NO REGISTRATION** | Banned explicitly alongside inventing a number: reusing one from earlier in the call or from something the consumer said, reading out a partial one, and — the quieter failure the old wording permitted — **dropping the number but claiming the registration anyway** ("दर्ज हो गई है" with no number, the S M S line, "team संपर्क करेगी"). Speaking none was already required; saying nothing in its place was not. |
| **Why failure, when a complaint may exist** | It may. Telling the consumer registration hit a problem costs them nothing and is true of everything Vaani can see; handing them a number that does not exist sends them away believing they have a complaint on record, and they discover otherwise only when they try to use it. The failure branch's existing no-retry rule keeps the honest answer from creating a duplicate. |
| **The `FAILURE-LINE GATE` moved with it** | That gate let the technical-failure line be spoken only against *"a FAILURE Result"*, which under the new test would have locked the agent out of the very branch it is now told to take. It now reads on the same axis — the line is spoken when a call of the agent's own came back **without a number**, an explicit failure or a numberless Result alike. Its own purpose is unchanged: a frustrated consumer, a repeated question or a confusing turn is still not a tool failure. |
| **QA** | `vaaniQA` **C22** — provenance, where C17 is turn shape. Every spoken number must match a visible Result digit for digit; report a number matching none, a number reused from earlier in the call, and a registration claimed successful with no number behind it — that last is the same defect as an invented number, not a lesser one. Taking the failure branch is correct behaviour and never reportable. |
| **Files (11)** | Ten complaint-capable agents, `vaaniQA`. |
| **CC** | Not ported. CC's complaint path has the identical two-outcome gap, and CPL-14 is still unported there too. |

---

### TOOL-01 · Named tool blockers in every prompt; nobody but `getConsumerDetails` fetches or asks who the consumer is — CRC only, CC pending (2026-08-12)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — shared truth. Client instruction: *every agent already has all its data and must not call a tool or do anything to help the consumer beyond what its prompt instructs.* |
| **The call** | 12-08-2026, 13:30. Consumer calls from their registered number about a delivery OTP. Routed to `bookingNonEligibilityAgent` — an agent whose whole job is explaining why booking is blocked, with no delivery flow. Its injected context said the refill was **delivered 27-07-2026 at 10:34**. It never read it. It asked for the registered mobile number, called a lookup tool, got nothing back, asked when they had booked, accepted "8th" over its own record, then merged a registering line, a complaint-failure line and a transfer offer into one turn, transferred, and the transfer failed. |
| **Root cause — a ban with no names in it** | `bookingNonEligibilityAgent`'s entire prohibition was one abstract sentence: *"You have three tools… You never call any data lookup tool."* A model does not reliably map "any data lookup tool" onto `bpcl_get_refill_history` when the platform has handed it that tool and the consumer is asking for exactly that. **And the sentence was visibly wrong about its own tool list** — "three tools", omitting `calltransfer`, which XFER-03 gave it and which the next paragraph explains how to use. A prompt that is wrong about itself is not an authority the model defers to. |
| **How wide it was** | **Nine of fifteen prompts named no tools at all**: both booking agents, all four delivery agents, `paymentAgent`, `genericInfoComplaintAgent`, `emergencyAgent`. All eight complaint-capable ones among them carried the same stale "three tools" sentence and the same abstract ban. Only `getConsumerDetails` (×2), `Default`, `routingAgent`, `subsidyAgent`, `connectionServicesAgent` and both newConnection files named anything. |
| **A second contradiction in the same sentence** | All eight also said *"I never connect the consumer to another person"* — false since XFER-03, which gave them `calltransfer`. Replaced with the gated truth: reaching a person is `calltransfer`, invoked directly, only at the four SENIOR TEAM TRANSFER moments, never volunteered. |
| **The fix** | Every prompt now carries a `[TOOL BLOCKER — MANDATORY]` naming **both lists by exact tool name** — what it may call, and what it may never call. `bpcl_fetch_all_api`, `bpcl_get_consumer_details`, `bpcl_get_refill_history`, `bpcl_get_subsidy_details`, `bpcl_check_refill_status` and `validatecontactno` are **forbidden by name in all fourteen prompts other than `getConsumerDetails`** (client decision: nobody else fetches; all data is pre-injected). `validatecontactno` was missing from `subsidyAgent`, `connectionServicesAgent` and `newConnectionAgent_onHold`'s lists and was added. |
| **The clause that carries the weight** | *NOT KNOWING SOMETHING IS NEVER A REASON TO CALL A TOOL.* Naming the tools stops a casual call; it does not stop the one that actually happens, where the agent reaches the edge of its data, feels the gap, and goes looking. So the blocker says there is nothing out there to find, and names the three legitimate exits — an unknown said out loud, a query routed, or a complaint registered. |
| **The no-ask rule** | *NEVER ASK THE CONSUMER FOR THEIR MOBILE NUMBER, THEIR CONSUMER ID, OR ANY IDENTIFIER*, in all fourteen. The consumer was identified before the agent activated — that is why it holds a record — so asking is both pointless and the reliable tell that a forbidden lookup is coming next. Framed as a diagnosis rather than a prohibition: if you are about to ask, the real problem is that you do not have an answer, and a number will not produce one. `newConnectionAgent_onHold` gets a variant, since its caller genuinely has no record: it has no tool to run a number through and no record it would open. |
| **QA** | `vaaniQA` **C23** — a lookup tool invoked by anyone but `getConsumerDetails`, and any other agent asking for a mobile number or identifier. Both are the same defect wearing two faces. Worst variant called out separately: the answer sitting in the injected record while Vaani asks for a number. |
| **Not in this pass** | Routing ("OTP not received" on an existing booking belongs to the delivery family, not booking) and extending RTE-04's read-the-record-first rule beyond `genericInfoComplaintAgent`. Both still open. |
| **⚠ Platform, not prompts** | On the same call the transfer-failure Result arrived **as a consumer turn**, in English: *"This is an internal instruction: Transfer failed after dialing… do not mention internal transfer details, provider errors, B-legs, routes, or tools."* Tool Results must reach the agent as Results. Delivered through the consumer channel, anything a caller says in that shape can steer the agent. No prompt change addresses this; it is with the platform team. |
| **Files (15)** | Fourteen live prompts, `vaaniQA`. `getConsumerDetails_MultiToolVersion` (QA-env) and the parked `newConnectionAgent.txt` untouched. |
| **CC** | Not ported. CC has the same abstract bans and the same exposure. |

### PCA-02 · `postCallAnalysisHuman` is now genuinely the human-agent analyser, and gains four flat dashboard scores — CRC only (2026-08-13)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — this is a **CRC-only analyser**, scoring calls the automated agent never touched. No CC counterpart is planned. |
| **What closed** | The subject-framing question left open by the 2026-08-11 CONTEXT pass (root `CLAUDE.md` §2b, CRC `CLAUDE.md` open items). **Client decision 2026-08-13: this file analyses a conversation between two humans** — a CRC staff member and the customer. There is no Vaani in its transcripts and no AI behaviour to score. |
| **Four new root fields** | `riskEscalationIndex` ("yes"/"no"), `customerEffortScore`, `conversationQualityScore`, `overallScore` (each "1"–"10"). Placed immediately after `notes`, so the flat block a dashboard reads sits together at the top and the nested advanced-QA objects stay untouched below it. **The nested objects remain authoritative** for monitoring (root `CLAUDE.md` §2b); these four are the QA dashboard's layer. |
| **All four are strings** | Including the scores — `"8"`, never `8`. Client instruction: types are handled at platform level, so the prompt emits one consistent shape. |
| **No arithmetic anywhere** | The first design used a weighted formula (quality ×0.4 + ease ×0.3 + FCR ×0.3). **Rejected — an LLM cannot compute a weighted mean reliably, and any later reweighting would silently invalidate every historical score.** Replaced by **first-match lookup tables**: `overallScore` reads down six ordered rows keyed on `riskEscalationIndex` and `fcr`; `conversationQualityScore` rates eight dimensions good/weak/poor, counts the weak and poor ones, and reads the count off a table. Selecting and counting are things the model does consistently; multiplying is not. |
| **The cap is the row order** | A call with `riskEscalationIndex` "yes" can never exceed `"5"`, and `fcr` "no" can never exceed `"6"`, because those rows come first and matching stops there. This is what stops a compliance-failure call scoring 8/10 and losing the QA team's trust in the number. |
| **Score direction is uniform** | All three scores are **high = good**, including effort — `"10"` means the customer barely had to try. Effort is naturally "high = bad", so the dashboard would have had one silently inverted column. The definition states the direction explicitly and separates effort from outcome: bad news delivered quickly and clearly is a **low-effort** call. |
| **`riskEscalationIndex` is a closed list, not a judgement** | Seven named triggers, any one of which forces "yes". **Trigger 6 narrowed 2026-08-13:** it fired on *any* angry customer, and since the row order caps a "yes" call at `"5"`, an agent who absorbed the anger and fully resolved the call could never score above 5 — the flag tracked how hard the call was rather than whether anything needs attention. It now fires only where the anger **went unaddressed** — the customer left still angry, still unheard, or with nothing; anger the agent defused is recorded in `customerSentiment` and `csatScore` instead. Two explicit non-triggers, both of which a naive reading gets wrong: **a correctly-handled escalation is the process working, not a risk**, and **a registered complaint is the resolution in this channel** (ESC-01), never a failure. Without those, the metric silently tracks complaint volume. A `riskReason` companion string was proposed and **declined by the client** — the nested `riskObservations` carries the detail. |
| **No transcript → `"NA"` across the whole payload** (revised 2026-08-13, client instruction — supersedes the original `""`-for-four-fields rule). The four dashboard fields were the only ones the empty-transcript rule covered, so every other field fell back to its normal default and an unreadable recording arrived on the dashboard as `greeting` "no", `fcr` "no", `callResult` "unresolved" — a reviewed-and-failed call. Now **every string in the output returns `"NA"`, every number returns `0`** (the two fixed values, `qaScore.total` 9 and `csatAnalysis.maxScore` 5, keep theirs), **every array returns empty**, and the five `riskObservations` **booleans return `false`** — they are typed booleans, so `"NA"` in them would either fail a strict parse or read as truthy and flag five risks. `notes` keeps its own sentence, "No transcript available for this call.", because it is the field a manager reads to learn why the row is blank. `"NA"` means unreviewable; `"no"` still means reviewed and clean. A short but genuine call is scored normally. |
| **De-Vaani'd (pass two, same session)** | CONTEXT rewritten for two humans — all tool language gone (`calltransfer`, `preToolMessage`, "only a Result proves registration"): a person uses a CRM and a phone. Agent referred to as **they/them**, never inferred from a name. `complaintRegistered` detection now judges **meaning, not phrasing** — the old rule hunted Vaani's exact scripted Hindi lines, which a human never says, so it would have under-reported badly; the completed-vs-intended and later-failure-cancels tests are unchanged. The ZIP/new-connection-hold sections stay as **policy the human must follow** — the hold is real, ZIP's facts are the facts — with the scripted soft-unknown line replaced by the behaviour it described. |
| **Staff latitude, stated once and enforced twice** | Client instruction 2026-08-13: a human agent **may** share a contact number, invite the customer to the office, quote a price they know, and offer to call back — none of these is a risk, and both `riskObservations` and `Compliance and Commitment Control` say so explicitly. Still wrong: a reopening date, a guaranteed delivery time, or a refund figure they do not have. **This is a real divergence from ESC-01**, which bars all of it for Vaani; it is scoped to what a person may do and does not touch any live prompt. |
| **Ninth checkpoint** | `evaluations[]` gains **Interruptions and Talk Balance** (`qaScore.total` 8 → 9, and → 10 later the same day, see below). It exists only because the subject is human — an AI never talks over anyone. It duplicates dimension 8 of `conversationQualityScore` by design, with an explicit instruction that the two must never contradict. |
| **Two more root fields, and `csat` renamed** (2026-08-13) | `greeting` ("yes"/"no" — did the agent open with a clear भारत पेट्रोलियम / Bharat Gas brand reference) and `containment` ("yes"/"no" — did the agent who answered handle it themselves, or did the call pass to another person). Root `csat` is renamed **`csatScore`** throughout. `greeting` is tied to the `Call Opening and Greeting` checkpoint and to dimension 1 of `conversationQualityScore` — the three judge the same moment and may not contradict. `containment` "no" is explicitly **not** a mark against the agent: passing to a senior after a real attempt is the correct process here (ESC-01), so it never drives `riskEscalationIndex`, a checkpoint FAIL, or a lower `overallScore`. |
| **Internal-consistency ties added** (2026-08-13) | The nested objects could contradict the flat fields, since nothing bound them. Now: `passedCriteria` / `failedCriteria` / `qaScore.earned` are all **counted from the `evaluations` array** and must sum to its length; the checkpoint list is **exactly nine, no more and no fewer** (a checkpoint that did not arise is a PASS saying so, never a dropped entry) so `qaScore.total` can stay 9; `csatAnalysis.score` equals `csatScore` and its sentiment agrees with `customerSentiment`; and `summary.resolutionStatus` follows `fcr`. Also fixed: `overallScore` rows 5 and 6 left a gap — `fcr` "yes" with quality `"7"`/`"8"` and no slips matched no row at all — so the perfect case is now row 5 and row 6 catches everything else. |
| **Three authoring leftovers removed** (2026-08-13) | A stray *"2. Add this section after your existing FIELD DEFINITIONS"* line shipped inside the prompt body; an orphan rule told the model to match `reason` against predefined values and fall back to `"others"`, when this output has no such list and no `"others"` value anywhere (**the same orphan line still sits in `postCallAnalysisFlat.txt` in both channels — unfixed**); and the `issue` examples included `"escalation request - complaint registered"`, whose hyphen violates that field's own no-special-characters rule. The `fcr` hold clause also named only Bharat Gas Mini as the alternative offered; ZIP-02 offers **Mini and लाइट ज़िप together**, as the `evaluations` section already said. |
| **Built for aggregation** (2026-08-13) | Client context: one object per call, **hundreds then summed and averaged onto a manager's dashboard**. A `TYPES AND RANGES` block now heads the field definitions and fixes one type per field — root fields all strings (scores quoted), numbers only inside `overallVerdict`/`qaScore`/`csatAnalysis` and real numbers there, `riskObservations` real booleans, arrays of plain strings. **The scales stay native and are not reconciled**: CSAT is 1–5 because that is what CSAT means, the three QA scores are 1–10, and nothing downstream should average across them. **`qaScore` is a fraction, not a third scale** — `earned` out of a fixed `total` of 10 is the audit trail, `percentage` (0–100) the comparable number, `overallScore` the 1–10 headline. `passedCriteria`/`failedCriteria`/`earned` are counts that always sum to 10, and where **0 is a legitimate reviewed value** (all ten can fail). |
| **Denominators are constants — client decision 2026-08-13** | `qaScore.total` is **10** and `csatAnalysis.maxScore` is **5** on *every* call, no-transcript rows included. Sending them to 0 on an unreviewable call was proposed and **rejected by the client**: a denominator states what a call is scored out of, which does not vary with what happened on the call, and the human scorer works against a fixed maxScore. The aggregation risk this was meant to dodge is already handled by the `overallScore` gate — the dashboard drops unreviewable rows before it sums, so a constant denominator costs nothing. Only the *earned* numbers go to 0. |
| **Tenth checkpoint: Customer Identification and Verification** (2026-08-13) | `evaluations[]` goes 9 → **10** and `qaScore.total` 9 → 10, so QA scores out of a round ten. Client asked for a tenth of the analyser's choosing; this was the genuine gap — `Accuracy of Information Shared` judges whether what the agent *said* was true, and nothing checked they had established **whose connection** they were discussing before handing out booking history, delivery status, subsidy credits, payment detail or KYC status. PASS on confirming consumer number / registered mobile / name and address first; PASS where the call never touched account-specific detail (a general how-to needs no verification); FAIL only where account detail went out with no identification at all. Failing to *find* the record after asking properly is a PASS — the asking is what is scored. **`conversationQualityScore` stays at eight dimensions** — this is a compliance checkpoint, not a conversation-quality one, and only `Interruptions and Talk Balance` is deliberately duplicated across the two lists. |
| **`overallScore` is the reviewability gate** | A dedicated `analysisStatus` field was proposed and **declined by the client** — reviewability is inferred from the sentinel instead. The prompt therefore designates **`overallScore` alone** as the field the dashboard filters on: `"NA"` excludes the row, `"1"`–`"10"` means every other field in it is trustworthy. It may never be `"NA"` on a call that had agent turns. Ingest must key off this one field, not off a scatter of `"NA"` checks. |
| **Grouping columns closed, absent values normalised** (2026-08-13) | Fields a dashboard groups by were free text and would have produced hundreds of distinct strings: `callDataCapture.issueType` is now **the same closed list as `departmentArea`** and must equal it; `csatAnalysis.sentiment` is the same four values as `customerSentiment` and must equal it; `summary.callLanguage` is a four-value list. `issue` is explicitly labelled a readable label, **not** a grouping key. Formats pinned: `deliveryDelayDays` whole days as digits, `amountMentioned` digits only (no ₹, no words), `referenceOrConfirmationNumberGiven.value` digits only. **Behavioural change to flag on ingest:** `bookingDateMentioned` / `deliveryDateMentioned` / `amountMentioned` now return **`"NA"` when absent, not `"no"`** — `"no"` in a date column is not a date, and it collided with the yes/no fields' vocabulary. Free-text fields (`customerName`, `agentName`, `actionTakenByAgent`, the four `conversationInsights` strings) return `"NA"` rather than an invention when the transcript does not show them. Arrays are capped at three entries and are empty rather than padded. |
| **Cross-object contradictions closed** | `overallVerdict.result` now follows the array (FAIL iff any checkpoint failed), and `recommendedCoachingPriority` follows the scores ("High" at risk "yes" or overallScore ≤ 5, "Low" at ≥ 8 with nothing to coach). Previously each was judged independently and could contradict the numbers beside it on the same row. |
| **Divergence created** | This file now differs sharply from `postCallAnalysisFlat.txt` (same channel) and from CC's copies. That is intended: those score the AI, this scores a person. Do not reconcile them. |

### KYC-01 · eKYC is always recommended, whatever the "Latest eKYC" date says — CRC only (2026-08-17)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — a **domain-policy** instruction, client-given, scoped to CRC `connectionServicesAgent`. CC is **not** changed: it keeps the date-as-fact-only behaviour until the client says otherwise. |
| **Source** | Client instruction, 2026-08-17: *"In CRC connectionServices always recommend consumer to complete their eKYC. Even if the Latest eKYC date is present. Just reference it as this is your last eKYC date and you should do your eKYC."* |
| **The change** | §7's eKYC-date rules previously let a populated `Latest eKYC` date **close** the topic — the worked example was *"आपकी eKYC ... पूरी हो गई थी"*, which reads as "nothing more is needed". Now the date is spoken **only as when their last eKYC was** (*"आपकी last eKYC [date] को हुई थी"*) and every eKYC turn ends with the recommendation to get it done, followed by the C5 route (KYC form, P O I, P O A — online or the distributor). |
| **What did NOT change** | The **never-compute-validity** rule (§7) stands unaltered. Recommending eKYC is explicitly **not** a verdict that the existing one expired, lapsed, or is overdue — no arithmetic against the nine-month domain fact, no deadline, no due date. If the consumer asks whether theirs is still valid, that detail is still "not available with me", and the recommendation stands beside that answer rather than answering it. The date-format rule (§9 Rule 4, YYYY-MM-DD read day-first, time never spoken digit by digit) is untouched. |
| **Files (2)** | `bpcl_showroom_crc/prompts/connectionServicesAgent/connectionServicesAgent.txt` (§7 eKYC bullets + C5), and this ledger. |
| **⚠️ Open** | Whether the standing recommendation should also fire from the other CRC agents that mention KYC (`genericInfoComplaintAgent`, the delivery family's KYC-block path) — not instructed, deliberately not done. And whether CC should follow; §6 rule 4 asks the question, the client answer is CRC-only for now. |

---

### CPL-19 · The registering sentence becomes a fixed tool parameter — `bpcl_create_complaint` gets a `preToolMessage` — CRC only, CC pending (2026-08-18)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**, and a deliberate change to the §4 tool-turn rule **for one tool only**. Landed in **CRC only**, in all ten registering agents plus `vaaniQA`. |
| **Why now** | Client report: happening **in production, regularly**, and the worst-impact defect on the channel. The model is fixed — Flash Lite stays, it is the right tier for low-latency voice — so the fix had to be structural rather than another prohibition. |
| **The evidence** | Ten ledger entries on this one tool in sixteen days (CPL-03, 08, 09, 11, 12, 13, 14, 15, 16, 17), every one a prose fix, every one followed by a recurrence. Meanwhile `callhangup` and `calltransfer`, whose spoken line lives **inside** the call, have **zero** narration reports in the whole history. CPL-11 spotted the reason and set it aside: each *"names a single **fixed literal** rather than a value the model must compose."* |
| **The mechanism** | The old turn had **two independent parts** — speak a Hindi sentence, separately invoke the tool. A fluent Hindi sentence is a complete, self-terminating output, so the model produced the half that ends naturally and dropped the half nothing enforced. All four observed modes are one cause at different stopping points: **A** silent no-op (CPL-03/09), **B** the call typed out as text (CPL-11/12/17), **C** an invented number (CPL-13/14/15), **D** false completion (CPL-13/16). |
| **The shape that shipped** | On a registering turn the agent speaks **its own short text** — an OPTIONAL restatement of the problem, then **`"एक मिनट रुकिए।"` exact and always last** — and invokes `bpcl_create_complaint` whose `preToolMessage` is **one fixed sentence, never composed and never varied: `"मैं आपकी शिकायत रजिस्टर कर रही हूँ।"`**. The consumer hears the restatement and wait cue, a sub-two-second gap, then the fixed sentence. |
| **Why the split, and not everything in the parameter** | A pure-`preToolMessage` version was built first and rejected on **latency**: nothing can be spoken until the *entire* call is generated, including `feedbackDescription`, so every registration gained a silent gap at the front. Keeping a short text means audio starts streaming immediately and the composing happens behind it. Client confirmed the residual gap is under two seconds and, crucially, **the consumer has already been told to wait before it arrives** — which is exactly why the wait cue is last. |
| **What the fixed sentence buys** | It cannot drift into the completed past (CPL-16's entire subject), cannot carry a complaint number (CPL-14/17), cannot have a clause bolted onto it (CPL-08), and **cannot be spoken at all unless the tool is invoked**. Four recurring defects become unrepresentable for that sentence rather than merely forbidden. |
| **The failure mode inverts — the real win** | If the tool does not fire, the consumer hears a restatement and *"एक मिनट रुकिए"*, then nothing. They were asked to wait; **they were never told a complaint exists.** Today that same failure tells them it is registered when it is not. Silence is detectable and alarmable; a false confirmation is not. |
| **Deliberate client decision — registration verbs NOT banned from the agent's own text** | The safest version bans `दर्ज` / `रजिस्टर` / `register` from the agent's own text on that turn, so the promise can only come from the tool. **Client declined** (2026-08-18): the agent may say them, and a restatement using them is not an error. **Known consequence, recorded on purpose:** if the agent does announce the registering in its own text and the tool then fails to fire, the false-promise path is open again for that turn. The design still holds by default — every prompt teaches restatement-then-wait-cue and states that the tool announces the action — but it is a convention now, not a guarantee. |
| **What it does NOT fix** | Modes **C** and **D**. An invented complaint number, or *"register हो गई है"* with no Result, happen **after** the tool returns and are about reading the Result. CPL-15's two-outcome rule and CPL-16's tense gate still own those. |
| **Companion fix — we were teaching the bug** | CPL-11 established by controlled comparison that **code-shaped syntax in the prompt produces narration**. CPL-17 (filed 2026-08-18) then added a detection checklist naming the literal tokens and characters — **putting the demonstration into ten files in order to warn against it.** Reverted. Every such list is now a **whitelist**: *there is exactly one kind of thing you produce — a natural Hindi sentence a person could say out loud; if any part of the draft is written for a machine to read, it is not speech.* CPL-08's older self-check and every surviving `parameter = "value"` form (including `routingAgent`'s two switchagent-retry lines and `connectionServicesAgent` §84) got the same treatment. |
| **Rules reworded to match** | The **TENSE GATE** now says the fixed sentence is in-progress by construction and aims the gate at the agent's own text instead. **REGISTER LINE IS TERMINAL** became **THE WAIT CUE IS TERMINAL** — `"एक मिनट रुकिए।"` is the last thing in the agent's own text, and the only thing that may follow it is the tool's sentence. The tool-turn preambles no longer group the complaint tool with `callhangup`/`calltransfer`: those two speak *only* a `preToolMessage`; the complaint tool carries a fixed one **alongside** the agent's own text — a third case, stated as such. |
| **`vaaniQA` updated in the same pass** | Left stale it would have flagged correct behaviour and spiked the dashboards. **C3g** rewritten to describe the new two-utterance turn and to state that the pair is correct, not repetition. **C3g-TAIL** re-aimed at the wait cue, since the terminal position moved. **C17** reworded for either registering sentence. Two new checks: **C25** — a *reworded* registering sentence standing where the fixed one belongs means the tool's sentence never played, with an explicit carve-out that Vaani's own restatement may use `दर्ज`/`register` freely; **C26** — a wait cue that leads nowhere is the new signature of a missed call, benign-sounding and therefore easy to miss on a casual listen, reported at C3g severity. |
| **Platform support — CONFIRMED 2026-08-18** | The platform accepts `preToolMessage` on `bpcl_create_complaint` and speaks it while the backend call runs. One QA item stands: confirm it plays **in parallel with** the call rather than after it returns — served late it fills no gap and becomes a post-hoc announcement. |
| **Deliberately NOT changed** | `switchagent` keeps the old contract (own text, no `preToolMessage`) — client scoped this to the complaint tool only, and wants to watch it first. `getConsumerDetails` holds no complaint tool and keeps its two-exception wording. `feedbackDescription` stays an **English summary authored by the agent**, not the consumer's verbatim words — client confirmed. |
| **Accepted trade-offs** | Two complaints in one call means `"एक मिनट रुकिए।"` is heard twice, identically — accepted, `callhangup` already behaves that way. The consumer no longer hears the issue summary *inside* the registering sentence; the optional restatement covers it, and the summary the team reads is `feedbackDescription`. |
| **Files (12)** | The ten registering agents — `activeDeliveryAgent`, `postDeliveryAgent`, `eligibleDeliveryAgent`, `notEligibleDeliveryAgent`, `genericInfoComplaintAgent`, `paymentAgent`, `bookingEligibleAgent`, `bookingNonEligibilityAgent`, `connectionServicesAgent`, `subsidyAgent` — plus `postCallAnalysis/vaaniQA.txt` and `routingAgent` (assignment forms only). |
| **⚠️ Open** | **CC unported** (§6 rule 4) — CC keeps the old two-part contract and every mode A/B failure with it. Port alongside BAN-01, CPL-08, CPL-09, CPL-16, CPL-17, CPL-18, OTP-01. Still unmeasured: the true production rate, with no denominator behind any of the nineteen entries. |
| **Numbering** | The 2026-08-17/18 entries filed as CPL-10/11/12 collided with the 2026-08-07 entries; renumbered to **CPL-16 / 17 / 18**. |
