### CPL-16 · The registering line spoken in the completed past — narration instead of registration — CRC only, CC pending (2026-08-17)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth**, a direct follow-up to CPL-03 and CPL-09 (this entry was filed as CPL-10 and renumbered to CPL-16 on 2026-08-18, the original CPL-10 being the 2026-08-07 bookingNonEligibilityAgent entry). Landed in **CRC only**; CC carries the same gap. |
| **Source** | Two live `genericInfoComplaintAgent` transcripts (client-reported, 16-08-2026). One: registering line → a content-free **"Uhh..."** turn → complaint number spoken in **Hindi digit words**, against NUM-01's English-digit default. The other: *"आपकी शिकायत **दर्ज हो चुकी है** ... — एक मिनट रुकिए।"* spoken on the turn `bpcl_create_complaint` was being invoked — the consumer is told the complaint **already exists** before any Result. |
| **Root cause — a second, weaker procedure competing with Tool 1** | `genericInfoComplaintAgent` §8, `postDeliveryAgent` §7 and `eligibleDeliveryAgent`'s equivalent each carried a `COMPLAINT REGISTRATION FLOW` section that restated registration in two sentences — *"When the consumer confirms … I call `bpcl_create_complaint` immediately"* and *"**After registering, on success** I read the complaint number …"*. That pair says nothing about speaking the registering line as text, nothing about `preToolMessage`, and collapses the two turns into one continuous action — directly against Tool 1's `THE REGISTERING LINE AND THE COMPLAINT NUMBER ARE NEVER IN THE SAME TURN`. The model executes the section it is standing in, not the tool block hundreds of lines above. `activeDeliveryAgent` has **no such section**, which is why it never drifted — the "reference" agents were only accidentally correct. |
| **The Hindi-digit tell** | The same §8 sentence's own template read `[complaint Number digit by digit in Hindi]` while its prose said *"(English digit words by default)"* — an unported **CC** leftover (CC keeps Hindi words by design, NUM-01). The live call followed the template, which is how we know §8 and not Tool 1 was driving the turn. Present in three CRC files; all three fixed. CC's copies are correct as they stand and were not touched. |
| **Fix 1 — TENSE GATE (all ten complaint-capable agents)** | The registering line is **in-progress only**: `"दर्ज कर रही हूँ"` / `"दर्ज की जा रही है"`, never `"दर्ज हो चुकी है"` / `"दर्ज हो गई है"` / `"register हो गई है"`. A completed-sounding line is false at the moment it is spoken, and it is the shape of a turn that **narrates** the registration instead of performing it — once the sentence sounds finished, the tool stops being called. It also traps the agent: on a tool failure she has already promised something that never happened. Sits directly under `FAILED TURN` / `REGISTER LINE IS TERMINAL FOR THE TURN`. |
| **Fix 2 — NO FILLER TURN (same ten)** | `"umm"`, `"uhh"`, a throat-clear, or a re-spoken hold line in the gap while the Result comes back is banned — a filler turn there is the **audible signature of a turn that had no tool call behind it** (the same tell CPL-09 and GCD-05 each observed independently). If the consumer speaks while waiting, answer what they said; never re-speak the registering line. |
| **Fix 3 — the competing section rewritten (three agents)** | `genericInfoComplaintAgent` §8 is now an explicit **TURN ONE / TURN TWO** procedure that states Tool 1 wins on any conflict, spells out line-as-text + no `preToolMessage` + terminal line + tense gate + no filler, and routes a missing Result into Tool 1's `RECOVERY`. `postDeliveryAgent` and `eligibleDeliveryAgent` get the compact form of the same. |
| **Files (11)** | The ten complaint-capable CRC agents, plus this ledger. |
| **Not changed** | Whether the delivery-irregularity complaint on that call should have been `genericInfoComplaintAgent`'s at all — RTE-02's GATE A hands back a booking that exists whose cylinder never came. Not re-examined here. |
| **⚠️ Open** | **CC unported** (§6 rule 4): CC's `genericInfoComplaintAgent`, `postDeliveryAgent` and `eligibleDeliveryAgent` carry the same two-sentence section and the same collapse risk, and no CC agent has the tense gate or the filler ban. Port before the next CC complaint-path change, alongside BAN-01, CPL-08 and CPL-09. |

---

### CPL-17 · The second registration gets typed out instead of invoked — CRC only, CC pending (2026-08-18)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth** (§4, *the tool call is the action*). Landed in **CRC only**, in all ten complaint-capable agents. CC carries the same gap. |
| **Source** | Client report, 2026-08-18: happening **in production, on a regular basis**. `postDeliveryAgent` registered one complaint correctly — real tool call, real Result, real number — and then later in the same call emitted: *"आपकी परेशानी को समझते हुए, मैं आपकी शिकायत फिर से दर्ज करने की कोशिश कर रही हूँ, कृपया एक मिनट रुकिए।"* followed by `[bpcl_create_complaint(feedbackDescription="Delivery boy took back the full cylinder…"` — **the call typed out as spoken text**. Nothing registered. The consumer heard a sentence, then machine text, then silence, and believed a complaint was being filed. |
| **Why the existing rule did not hold** | `OUTPUT-SHAPE SELF-CHECK` (CPL-08) already forbids exactly this and already names the symptom — *"the tool's NAME and then a bracket and parameter text"* — and `postDeliveryAgent` carries it. It failed for two reasons. ① It is the **last sentence of a ~400-word paragraph** about a different topic (failed turns generally), so it reads as a footnote to the first registration rather than a standing check. ② It describes the shape but gives **no account of when or why** the model produces it, so there is nothing to recognise the moment by. |
| **Root cause — the narration is downstream of a forbidden retry** | The give-away is the word **"फिर से"**. The agent was not registering; it was **re-registering**, which `CALL ONCE, NEVER RETRY` and `SAME-ISSUE / DUPLICATE GUARD` both forbid outright. So the turn is a conflict: a consumer pressing for it again, and a rule saying the tool may not be called. **Typing the call out is what that conflict resolves into** — it feels like acting while obeying the ban on acting. It is the one output that both disobeys the rule and fails the consumer. This is why the failure is always **late** in a call and never on the first complaint. |
| **Second driver — the real call is the template** | Once a genuine `bpcl_create_complaint` invocation sits in the context, it is the most available example of "what registering looks like". Reproducing its **shape** as text is not repeating what worked; it is producing a picture of it. The new block states this directly: having called the tool successfully already makes the agent **more** likely to type the next one, not less. |
| **Fix — one new standalone block, five parts** | ① The failure named by its real shape and its real timing (*late in the call, after a successful one*). ② Why it happens, with the correct resolution spelled out — **always a sentence, never a typed-out call**: the already-registered line, or the technical-failure line plus the senior-team offer. Both are complete, honest turns that need no tool. ③ The prior-success-as-template trap. ④ **THERE IS NO SUCH THING AS RETRYING A COMPLAINT** — *"फिर से दर्ज कर रही हूँ"*, *"दोबारा try कर रही हूँ"*, *"दर्ज करने की कोशिश कर रही हूँ"* banned as line shapes; **"कोशिश" has no place in a registering line at all**, because on a legitimate registering turn the tool is being invoked, not attempted. The line itself is the warning sign. ⑤ A **literal character check run on every turn**, not only complaint turns: `[`, `(`, `=`, English text in double quotes, and the strings `bpcl_create_complaint` / `switchagent` / `calltransfer` / `callhangup` / `feedbackDescription` / `reason` / `agentName` / `preToolMessage`. Any hit → delete back to the last natural Hindi sentence. Safe to run blind, and the block says why: **no valid Hindi turn contains any of those characters**. |
| **Files (10 + ledger)** | All ten CRC complaint-capable agents: `postDeliveryAgent`, `activeDeliveryAgent`, `eligibleDeliveryAgent`, `notEligibleDeliveryAgent`, `genericInfoComplaintAgent`, `paymentAgent` (first person), `bookingEligibleAgent`, `bookingNonEligibilityAgent`, `connectionServicesAgent`, `subsidyAgent` (second person — the block is voiced to match each file). |
| **⚠️ Open** | **CC unported** (§6 rule 4) — CC's complaint agents carry CPL-08's self-check with the same structural weakness. Port alongside BAN-01, CPL-08, CPL-09, CPL-16, CPL-17, OTP-01. Also worth watching: whether the same typed-out shape appears for `switchagent` on a routing turn, which the literal check now covers but which has not yet been observed in a transcript. |

---

### CPL-18 · "Complaint" is an action word, not an intent — the unclassified escalation handoff — CRC only, CC pending (2026-08-18)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth** (§4 complaint discipline + the `CLASSIFY BEFORE YOU FALL BACK` routing rule). Landed in **CRC only**; CC's `Default` and `genericInfoComplaintAgent` carry the same two gaps. |
| **Source** | Live CRC transcript, 18-08-2026 09:24. Consumer's first utterance: *"यह gas cylinder की complaint करनी है ma'am."* `Default` routed on that turn to `genericInfoComplaintAgent` with `handoffSummary` = *"Intent: gas cylinder complaint. Context: consumer wants to register a complaint regarding a gas cylinder. Please help consumer register their complaint…"*. `genericInfoComplaintAgent` then spoke a **registering line and a clarifying question in the same turn** — *"आपकी शिकायत दर्ज कर रही हूँ कि आपको गैस सिलेंडर से संबंधित समस्या है — एक मिनट रुकिए। क्या आप मुझे बता सकती हैं कि सिलेंडर के साथ ठीक क्या समस्या आ रही है…?"* — and when the consumer answered (*booked 9 August, 18 August today, nine days, no cylinder*), registered it. |
| **What went wrong** | Three separate failures, in sequence. ① **`Default` never classified.** A booking that exists whose cylinder has not arrived is `activeDeliveryAgent`'s, named explicitly in `CLASSIFY BEFORE YOU FALL BACK` and in STAGE 2B's `ACTIVE DELIVERY PROBLEM`. `Default` never got there because it read the word *complaint* as a finished intent. ② **The handoffSummary locked the answer in.** *"Please register a complaint"* is the **escalation** shape, reserved for INTERRUPTS B/C/D/E. ③ **`genericInfoComplaintAgent` registered blind**, against a query it was simultaneously asking the consumer to explain. |
| **Root cause 1 — the word is not the topic** | `Default`'s `IF THE QUERY IS TOO VAGUE TO PLACE, ASK ONE QUESTION FIRST` rule was already there and correct, but nothing said that *"complaint करनी है"* **is** that vague case. With a product noun attached (*"gas cylinder की complaint"*) it reads like a placed intent, and every agent in the routing map deals in cylinders, so the noun classifies nothing. |
| **Root cause 2 — `NO PING-PONG` outranked `GATE A`** | `genericInfoComplaintAgent` §3 already holds the exact case: *"A BOOKING THAT EXISTS WHOSE CYLINDER HAS NOT ARRIVED"* → hand back to `routingAgent`, restated as **GATE A** in §7 with the note *"THIS IS THE CASE I MOST OFTEN KEEP BY MISTAKE"*. It never fired, because the clause immediately after it — *"An escalation that arrives here for registration is NEVER handed back, whatever its topic"* — matched first. The handoffSummary said *register*, so the agent read itself as an escalation and switched off its own gates before the consumer had said a word. **The clause written to stop ping-pong became the clause that guaranteed the misroute.** |
| **Fix 1 — `Default`** | New block under `CLASSIFY BEFORE YOU FALL BACK`: **THE WORD "COMPLAINT" IS NOT A TOPIC** — *"complaint करनी है"*, *"शिकायत दर्ज करनी है"*, and the same with any product noun attached, are the **action**, not the problem; treat as too vague to place and ask the one topic-only question as the entire turn, then route on the answer. Named as *the single most common way an owned query reaches the catch-all*. Paired with **A CONSUMER SAYING "COMPLAINT" IS NOT AN ESCALATION**: `COMPLAINT ESCALATION` applies to INTERRUPTS B/C/D/E only, and an escalation `handoffSummary` is never emitted for an unclassified query. Restated at the `COMPLAINT ESCALATION` section itself, where the *"never ask them to describe their problem"* line had been the thing being over-applied. |
| **Fix 2 — `genericInfoComplaintAgent`, `NO PING-PONG` narrowed** | An escalation *"arrives for registration"* only when the `handoffSummary` **CONTEXT names a real described problem**, or names a request for a person / callback / language / non-LPG product. A summary that asks for registration but describes **no incident** — *"consumer wants to register a complaint regarding a gas cylinder"* — is an **unclassified query wearing a registration instruction** and exempts nothing. Explicit: **the "Please register a complaint" line in a handoffSummary never overrides GATE A or GATE B** — whoever wrote it did not know the problem either; they were passing on the word *complaint*, not a decision. |
| **Fix 3 — the clarifying turn is clean** | RESOLUTION LADDER step 1 now states that the clarifying question is the **whole turn and carries no registering language** — no *"दर्ज कर रही हूँ"*, no *"एक मिनट रुकिए"*, no tool. The two sentences contradict each other outright: one says the complaint is being filed, the other admits the agent does not know what it is. Plus: **"they have a gas cylinder problem" is not a clear query** — a topic word with no incident attached fires step 1. |
| **Fix 4 — unrendered `{{customerStatus}}`** | Platform-side defect on this call (client is fixing it separately), but the prompt had no branch for it: §7's `I READ {{customerStatus}} BEFORE I ACT` assumed the block renders. Now — an empty / `null` / brace-wrapped block is **NO RECORD AT ALL**, which is not the same as a record that says nothing. Never imply a check happened, never state a delivery or booking position. Order: **hand back to `routingAgent` first** if the problem belongs to another family (that family holds its own record, which is precisely why the hand-back exists); only if the problem is genuinely this agent's, say honestly the details are not to hand and register on the consumer's own words. *A missing record makes reading the consumer carefully more important, never a reason to skip to the tool.* |
| **Files (3)** | `Default/Default.txt`, `genericInfoComplaintAgent/genericInfoComplaintAgent.txt`, plus this ledger entry. |
| **⚠️ Also seen, not fixed here** | The success turn spoke the complaint number as **Hindi** digit words (*"एक - दो - तीन - चार - चार - पाँच - सात - शून्य"*), against **NUM-01** (English digit words by default in CRC), and the tidy ascending run is the exact pattern `HARD STOP CHECK` calls the giveaway of an invented number. Worth checking against the backend Result for that call before treating it as a fabrication. |
| **⚠️ Open** | **CC unported** (§6 rule 4). CC's `Default` has the same catch-all routing map and CC's `genericInfoComplaintAgent` the same `NO PING-PONG` clause. Port alongside BAN-01, CPL-08, CPL-09, CPL-10, OTP-01. |

### OTP-01 · The delivery O T P is correct process, not a grievance — CRC only, CC pending (2026-08-17)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — a **domain fact** plus the complaint-discipline consequence that follows from it. Shared truth by nature (§4 LPG domain facts); landed in **CRC only** because the client scoped it there. CC carries the identical gap. |
| **Source** | Client instruction, 2026-08-17, with a live CRC transcript (17-08-2026, 09:33). Consumer: *"Booking थी, लेकिन delivery agent के द्वारा deliver नहीं किया गया, OTP माँग रहा था."* Vaani (`activeDeliveryAgent`), after an `"Umm..."` turn: *"असुविधा हुई … आपकी शिकायत दर्ज कर रही हूँ कि बुकिंग होने के बावजूद डिलीवरी एजेंट ने ओ टी पी की माँग की और सिलेंडर डिलीवर नहीं किया"* — she filed a complaint against the delivery person for doing exactly what the process requires. |
| **The fact** | Every refill delivery is confirmed with an O T P. It reaches the consumer by **S M S on their registered mobile number**, and the delivery person hands the cylinder over **only after** the consumer tells it to them. Asking for it at the door is correct behaviour — not a demand, not a refusal to deliver, not misconduct. |
| **Root cause** | No prompt in either channel held the fact. The only O T P text anywhere was the **PII/fraud** clause (*"never share an O T P"*, *"if someone asked for an O T P to process a change, treat it as possible fraud"*), which points the opposite way, and the RESOLUTION LADDER's **grievance carve-out** — a cylinder not delivered registers directly, no ladder — matched the consumer's sentence perfectly. Both pushed toward registering. |
| **The rule** | An O T P having been asked for is **never** a complaint and never reaches any reason list; the grievance carve-out explicitly does not apply, because nothing went wrong. Vaani **explains**, and the explanation is the resolution. She never says the request was wrong, never apologises for it, and never promises it will not happen again. |
| **The four branches** | ① They did not know why it was asked → the explanation is the whole answer, no complaint. ② They never received the O T P → check the S M S on the registered number; if that number is stale, it is a **mobile-number update** and routes. ③ They **gave** the O T P and the cylinder still did not come → a genuine **non-delivery** grievance, registered under a non-delivery reason phrase, never one describing the O T P. ④ Money demanded alongside → the money is a **payment** matter and routes; the O T P part is still normal. |
| **The PII carve-out** | The delivery O T P is the **one** O T P a consumer is meant to share — at the door, with the delivery person, and nowhere else. Vaani still never asks for it, repeats it or records it, and stops a consumer who starts reading it out (*"यह O T P मुझे नहीं चाहिए"*). Bank / payment / K Y C / account-change O T Ps are unchanged: never shared with anyone, and a request for one is still possible fraud. |
| **Files (16)** | Four delivery agents (`activeDeliveryAgent` — full block + `Path H`; `postDeliveryAgent` — full block + a PATH 2 entry; `eligibleDeliveryAgent` — full block; `notEligibleDeliveryAgent` — a route-instead-of-register variant, since it owns no delivery reason phrases), `genericInfoComplaintAgent` (full block + a `Delivery O T P` entry in its resolution inventory), both booking agents (compact route-instead-of-register form), `Default` (a `Delivery O T P` FAQ line in the KNOWLEDGE BASE's GENERAL RULES & ROUTING block, beside `Door Delivery`, explicitly *never* an escalation), `routingAgent` (O T P signals added to the `activeDeliveryAgent` row + a classification note that it is a delivery-family query, never a complaint route), `paymentAgent` (separates the O T P from the cash demand inside FLOW 5), `connectionServicesAgent` / `subsidyAgent` / `newConnectionAgent_onHold` (the carve-out appended to their §11/§10 fraud clause), `vaaniQA` (the fact added to PART B, plus new check **C24**), plus this ledger and the CRC channel contract. |
| **Not changed** | `newConnectionAgent.txt` (PARKED, do not ship — §6 rule 6) and `emergencyAgent` / `getConsumerDetails`, neither of which handles delivery queries. |
| **⚠️ Open** | **CC unported** (§6 rule 4): CC's delivery family, `genericInfoComplaintAgent`, `Default` and `routingAgent` hold no O T P fact either, and CHANNELS.md CPL-14 already records a CC call where a delivery-O T P query landed in `bookingNonEligibilityAgent` and produced a fabricated registration. Port alongside BAN-01, CPL-08, CPL-09, CPL-16. Also unresolved: whether the O T P is re-sendable and by what route — nothing on our side holds that, so no prompt claims it. |

### HOL-01 · `{{crcHolidayList}}` — the office holiday calendar, CRC only (2026-08-18)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — a **new CRC-only runtime variable**. CC receives no equivalent, so a CC prompt without it is intended, not an unported fix. |
| **Source** | Client, 2026-08-18. A new variable arrives on CRC calls carrying the Bharat Petroleum holidays falling on working days over the **next thirty days**. |
| **The shape** | Plain sentences, one per holiday, each naming the occasion, the weekday and the date — e.g. `"<occasion> holiday on <weekday> <date> <month> <year>, …"`. Not a structured list; the prompts describe the shape and explicitly mark any example occasions as illustrative, so the model never treats a sample date as data. |
| **Whose holiday it is** | **Both offices.** It is a Bharat Petroleum holiday, not this CRC's private calendar, so the same value answers "will the office be open" for **this CRC and the consumer's own distributor alike**. This is the one office fact exempt from `genericInfoComplaintAgent`'s DISTRIBUTOR ↔ CRC NON-SUBSTITUTION rule, and that exemption is stated inline where the rule lives, so the two do not read as contradicting each other. |
| **Absence is "I don't know", never "we're open"** | The list covers thirty days. A festival further out, a date outside the window, or an occasion the list does not name → *"जी, इसकी जानकारी अभी मेरे पास नहीं है।"* The agent never derives a holiday from its own festival-calendar knowledge, never estimates a date, and — the trap this rule exists for — **never inverts absence into the opposite answer** by telling the consumer the office will be open that day. Not being in the list is evidence of nothing. Empty / `null` / brace-wrapped is broken data and gets the same answer, never *"कोई छुट्टी नहीं है"*. |
| **Delivery** | Occasion plus date, day-first, in Hindi words — never raw digits, never the year-first `{{system.current_date}}` form; year dropped unless asked. One festival asked, one festival answered: the whole list is never read out and a holiday nobody asked about is never volunteered. A holiday question is **information** — nothing is registered for it. |
| **Placement — two agents, not fifteen** | `Default` (owns inline general FAQ, where the question lands at the front of the call) and `genericInfoComplaintAgent` (the catch-all leaf, and already `routingAgent`'s target for a general-information question raised mid-call). The query is ~0.01% of calls; the existing route already carries the tail case from any leaf, and fifteen copies of one fact is fifteen places to drift. |
| **Files (3)** | `Default/Default.txt` (RUNTIME VARIABLES, beside the office-timing line), `genericInfoComplaintAgent/genericInfoComplaintAgent.txt` (SECTION 3 DATA I HAVE, beside the office-timing line), plus this ledger entry. |
| **⚠️ Open** | Whether the variable ever carries a **half-day** or a distributor-only local closure — today's prompts treat every entry as a full closure of both offices. `vaaniQA` has no check for a fabricated or inverted holiday answer yet. |

### GCD-08 · The mobile confirmation was spoken twice in one breath — two instructions, one sentence's worth of room — both channels (2026-08-19)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — shared truth (turn discipline). Landed in **CC and CRC together**, plus CRC's QA variant. Amends **GCD-07**, whose event-edge trigger this keeps and sharpens. |
| **What the live call did** | One agent turn, the identical sentence back to back with nothing between: *"कृपया पुष्टि कीजिए, आपका मोबाइल नंबर eight - five - … - two है?कृपया पुष्टि कीजिए, आपका मोबाइल नंबर eight - five - … - two है?"* |
| **Why it happened** | `validatecontactno`'s Result carries its own confirm instruction — `Next: confirm once, then call bpcl_fetch_all_api`, and an `Answer` field reading *"Mobile number captured: 8585990252. Please confirm once before I check the consumer details."* The prompt **also** told the agent to confirm. Two live mandates, so two confirmations. GCD-07's answer to this was to declare the Result's text a non-directive and make the prompt the sole source — which leaves the tool's instruction standing and unexplained, and the model obeys what it is given. |
| **The rule now — one instruction, split across two sources** | There is ONE confirmation, and the two sources do different jobs: **the Result decides WHEN** (it is the only trigger, per GCD-07's event edge) and **the prompt decides HOW IT IS WORDED**. Neither is ignored and neither is obeyed twice. What the agent takes from the Result is the cue and the digits — never its wording: `"Mobile number captured…"`, `"Please confirm once…"` and any `Next:` note are the tool talking to the agent, never a line for the consumer. |
| **Files touched (4)** | CRC `getConsumerDetails.txt` (deployed) and `getConsumerDetails_MultiToolVersion.txt` (QA), CC `getConsumerDetails.txt`, plus this ledger. |
| **Client instruction** | Follow the tool's confirm instruction, not the prompt's — the prompt only teaches the sentence. That is exactly the split written above. |

### XFER-05 · The spent transfer did not survive a switch, and the failure path re-offered it — CRC only (2026-08-19)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Axis 1 — escalation. **CRC only**; CC's failure path captures a callback slot and does not port. |
| **Amends** | **XFER-03's** once-per-call rule and **XFER-04's** offer-dies-with-the-attempt rule, both of which were written per agent and therefore did not bind after a `switchagent`. |
| **What the live call did** | Complaint tool failed → T3 offer → consumer said yes → `calltransfer` → failure (out of hours, with a window). The consumer raised the next issue; the agent **registered again** (*"मैं इस समस्या को दर्ज करने का एक और प्रयास कर रही हूँ"*), failed again, **offered a transfer again**, and **ran `calltransfer` a second time** (busy). It then circled four more turns on *"कुछ समय बाद call कीजिए"* in fresh words each time, never closing. |
| **Why the existing rules did not catch it** | Every once-per-call rule was phrased in the first person against *this* agent's own turns — and the call had switched agents. A freshly activated agent reads a `handoffSummary`, not a spent-tool ledger, so nothing in front of it said the transfer was already gone. **The limit is a property of the call; the prompts made it a property of the agent.** |
| **The rule now (three clauses, in all thirteen routing-capable agents)** | **(1)** *The attempt belongs to the call, not to me — it survives a switch.* Before invoking `calltransfer`, read back through the whole call for a transfer line or a transfer Result, including turns from before this agent became active; if either is there, the tool is finished. **(2)** *No second offer once the transfer is spent.* T3 exists because there is nothing left to give — once a transfer has been attempted that is no longer true, so a later complaint failure gets the failure line and nothing else. A yes the agent cannot act on is worse than not asking. **(3)** *When there is genuinely nothing left, say it once and close.* Never in the same breath as the bad news — but repeating "call again later" in fresh words three and four times is stalling, and the correct ending is an honest acknowledgement and `callhangup`. |
| **Files touched (14)** | The ten complaint-capable leaves, `routingAgent`, `newConnectionAgent_onHold` (clause 1 only — it never offers), plus this ledger. `getConsumerDetails` already took the offer-dies rule at XFER-04 and is first in the call, so it was left alone. |
| **⚠️ Same open item as XFER-04** | The failure line again named *सोमवार से शुक्रवार, सुबह दस बजे से शाम छह बजे*, which still does not match this channel's documented hours (all weekdays, 9am–7pm). Either the Result carries those hours or the model invents them; unresolved, and not fixable from the prompts. |

### CPL-20 · The complaint tool's failure did not survive a switch either — CRC only, CC pending (2026-08-19)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — complaint discipline (§4 shared truth), landed CRC-first alongside XFER-05, which is the same defect in the transfer path. |
| **What the live call did** | After `bpcl_create_complaint` had already failed once, the agent announced *"मैं इस समस्या को दर्ज करने का एक और प्रयास कर रही हूँ"* and called it again — the exact wording the prompt bans (*"कोशिश"*, *"फिर से"*) and the exact behaviour the tool-is-down rule forbids. |
| **Why the existing rule did not catch it** | `THE TOOL IS DOWN FOR THE REST OF THE CALL ONCE IT HAS FAILED` was, like the transfer rules, scoped to the agent that made the failing call. A fresh agent reads a fresh `handoffSummary` and treats itself as a fresh attempt. |
| **The rule now** | One added clause under that heading in all ten complaint-capable agents: *the failure belongs to the CALL, not to the agent that made it* — a failure anywhere earlier in the conversation, including before this agent became active, means the tool is down from this agent's very first turn. **A fresh agent is not a fresh attempt.** |
| **Files touched (11)** | The ten complaint-capable leaves, plus this ledger. |

### BNE-01 · `bookingNonEligibilityAgent` ignored the delivered-status line sitting in its own `handoffSummary` — CRC only (2026-08-19)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — a grounding/scope defect in one agent. |
| **What the live call did** | `handoffSummary` carried *"LPG refill was booked on 2026-08-07 … Your Refill has been delivered on 11-07-2026 at 09:58 AM."* alongside the eligibility verdict. The consumer's actual complaint was that the cylinder never came. The agent answered with a **booking-gap explanation**, told them the next booking was *"पंद्रह दिन बाद"* — a figure in no field of the summary — and never once mentioned that the record showed the refill as delivered. |
| **Why it happened** | The prompt's delivery-status paragraph described only the **pending** shape of that sentence (*"refill will be delivered to you shortly"*). The **completed** shape was undescribed, so it read as background text rather than the answer to the question actually being asked. |
| **The rule now** | The paragraph names both shapes, and the completed one is not optional to notice: on *"मेरा सिलेंडर नहीं आया"* or any question about the existing refill, the agent answers from that sentence **first**, before eligibility, gaps or next-booking dates. Where the record says delivered and the consumer says it never came, the consumer is believed — that is the consumer disputing system data, which is inside this agent's own complaint scope (reason `others`) and a grievance about something that already happened, so it registers here rather than routing, with the consumer's own account in `feedbackDescription` and never the system's delivery date. Stating the record once is information; stating it twice is contradicting them. A third clause restates that no gap, day-count or next-booking date may be **derived** from that sentence — the invented *"पंद्रह दिन"* is exactly that failure. |
| **Files touched (2)** | `bookingAgent/bookingNonEligibilityAgent.txt`, plus this ledger. |
| **Not touched** | The date shapes in that summary disagree (`2026-08-07` year-first for the booking, `11-07-2026` day-first for the delivery — a delivery apparently preceding its own booking). The existing DATE FORMAT rule already reads each field's shape independently; whether the backend is emitting one of them wrongly is a platform question. |

### NUM-05 · CRC's `bookingEligibleAgent` dictated the booking numbers in Hindi digit words, and one of them was a digit short — both channels (2026-08-19)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — NUM-01 compliance in CRC; a factual data fix in CC. |
| **What the live call did** | *"एक आठ शून्य शून्य दो दो चार तीन चार चार पर बुकिंग मेसेज भेज दीजिए"*, then three more Hindi-digit turns dictating it in pieces. NUM-01 has made English digit words the CRC default since 2026-08-03, and this agent's own `LONG NUMBER DELIVERY` block says so — but the two booking numbers were **written out in Hindi digit words in the prompt itself**, in seven places, so the model was copying what it was given. A prompt that contradicts its own rule loses to its own examples. |
| **The second defect** | That Hindi spellout is **ten digits for an eleven-digit number** — `18002243344` spoken as *एक आठ शून्य शून्य दो दो चार तीन चार चार* drops a `तीन`. Any consumer who wrote it down got an unusable number. **The same broken spellout is in CC**, where Hindi digit words are correct per NUM-01, so CC keeps the language and takes the missing digit. |
| **The rule now** | CRC: both numbers written out in English digit words with `" - "` (`one - eight - zero - zero - two - two - four - three - three - four - four`, `seven - seven - one - eight - nine - five - five - five - five - five`), plus a clause saying they are spoken exactly as written and never re-translated into Hindi digit words. Also added: the three-piece repeat split is **shaped by the number** — three-three-four for ten digits, four-three-four for an eleven-digit toll-free — because the fixed 3-3-4 wording had no shape for the number it was most often used on. |
| **Files touched (3)** | CRC `bookingAgent/bookingEligibleAgent.txt`, CC `bookingAgent/bookingEligibleAgent.txt` (digit fix only — Hindi retained per NUM-01), plus this ledger. |

### PCA-03 · `customerSentiment` collapsed into neutral/negative on almost every call, and nothing recorded the agent's tone — new `agentTone` field — CRC only (2026-08-19)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — this is the **CRC-only human-agent analyser** (PCA-02). No CC counterpart is planned, and `postCallAnalysisFlat` is deliberately untouched. |
| **What the dashboard showed** | Client observation: almost every call came back `"neutral"` or `"negative"`, and the distribution was plainly wrong — those calls were not all neutral and not all negative. `"positive"` was effectively unreachable. |
| **Four causes, all in two lines of prompt** | The whole definition was *"The customer's overall tone: cooperative or happy, factual or calm, dissatisfied or frustrated, hostile or shouting"* — adjectives with no decision procedure. (1) **Topic bleed** — every LPG call is *about* a problem, so the model read the subject as negative and labelled the customer negative. (2) **`"positive"` was written as "cooperative or happy"**, and an LPG caller is almost never *happy* — they say "ठीक है", "थैंक यू", and hang up — so the value almost never fired. (3) **Section 4's `UNCERTAINTY RESOLVES DOWNWARD`** is a facts rule, but the model applied it to a judgement field: no clear evidence of delight → take the safer lower bucket → `"neutral"`. (4) **One flat label for the whole call**, which the model anchored on the opening turns — where the customer is stating their problem. A caller who arrived upset and left satisfied was scored `"negative"`. |
| **The fix — a procedure, not adjectives** | `customerSentiment` keeps its name, its position and its four values (client decision: **no new sentiment fields, no `start`/`trend` split** — a single field, decided better). Its definition is now: an explicit **"what it is not"** (the problem being serious is not negative sentiment; a disappointing but true answer — the new-connection hold — is not either); a **middle-and-end weighting rule**, since the opening always reads worse than the call was and the closing turns carry the most weight; **five signals** to read off those turns (cooperation, acceptance, escalation language, courtesy, direction of travel); and a **first-match ladder** ordered `angry` → `negative` → `positive` → `neutral`. |
| **`neutral` is last on purpose** | It is the bucket everything was collapsing into, so it now sits **below** `positive` in the ladder and is labelled *the narrow bucket, not the default* — reached only by checking all five signals and genuinely finding nothing either way, never by being unsure which other row applied. Correspondingly `positive` is stated to be **common and meant to be**: an ordinary courteous close *is* positive, delight is not required, and a call that improved from a sharp opening to a settled close lands there. |
| **Carve-out added to section 4** | `customerSentiment` and `agentTone` are **exempt from `UNCERTAINTY RESOLVES DOWNWARD`**, stated at the rule itself. That rule governs facts — a date, a number, an event that did or did not occur. Applying a downward default to a behavioural judgement is what drove the collapse. |
| **New root field: `agentTone`** | `"empathetic"` \| `"professional"` \| `"impatient"` \| `"rude"`, placed immediately after `customerSentiment`. **Four rungs of one ladder, not four independent traits**, read first-match downward: `rude` → `impatient` → `empathetic` → `professional`. |
| **Named `agentTone`, not `agentSentiment` or `agentBehaviour`** | Industry splits the two words and the first draft was on the wrong side of the split. **"Sentiment"** means the positive/neutral/negative *scale* — Contact Lens, NICE and Verint all score "agent sentiment" on exactly that three-value scale. **"Tone analysis"** is the separate technique that returns *descriptive labels* — professional, friendly, empathetic, cold, direct — which is what this field holds. Calling it `agentSentiment` would have told the dashboard team it shares a scale with `customerSentiment`, which it does not, inviting a comparison or an average that means nothing. `agentBehaviour` was rejected for the opposite reason: "behaviour" reads as *actions*, and actions are `conversationQualityScore`'s job — the whole point of this field is that it is scoped to **manner only**, so naming it behaviour would reopen the overlap the firewall below exists to close. |
| **The fifth value is gone — there is no `flat`/`robotic`/`cold` rung at all** | It went `flat` → `robotic` → `flat` → **deleted**, and the history is kept so it is not re-litigated. `robotic` and `cold` are the standard tone-analyser labels, so `robotic` was tried; **client rejected it on the plainest ground — the agent is a person, and a person's tone is not "robotic"**, a word that describes a machine and reads as a category error on a human-QA dashboard (doubly so in a repo whose other analysers score an actual bot, and whose live prompts already use "robotic" to mean *Vaani*). `cold` was declined separately — it implies *unkindness*, which drifts into `rude`. `indifferent` and `disengaged` were set aside as descriptions of **attitude** rather than tone. The rung was then **dropped entirely**, and the real reason was never the wording: **the `professional`/`flat` boundary was not decidable from a transcript** — "courteous and businesslike" against "correct but gave the bare minimum" is a distinction two runs would split on, which is exactly how a dashboard column turns to noise. |
| **The cold call needs no value — it is `professional` without `empathetic`** | Deleting the rung costs nothing, because the cohort it was meant to catch is already findable. An agent who worked correctly and acknowledged nothing is `"professional"` here, and **dimension 7 (Empathy) of `conversationQualityScore` is where the missing warmth is recorded** — a manager filters for professional calls whose empathy dimension was weak and gets exactly that group. Stated in the prompt so the gap is not "helpfully" refilled: never stretch `impatient` or `rude` to cover mere coldness, and never withhold `professional` as though it were a mild criticism. |
| **Rungs, not traits — the co-occurrence fix** | Client observation: *"the tone will be empathetic and professional at the same time"* — correct, and a defect in the first draft, which listed the five as independent adjectives so a warm, correct agent genuinely matched two values and the pick came down to a coin flip. The values are now **four rungs of one ladder with `professional` as the baseline**, each defined against it: `empathetic` means *professional **and** warm*, a superset rather than a competitor, so "both at once" resolves to `empathetic` **by rule**. First-match downward, adverse rows first — `rude` → `impatient` → `empathetic` → `professional` — mirroring `customerSentiment`'s ladder, so an otherwise warm agent who rushed the customer reads `impatient`, which is the part worth seeing. |
| **`professional` / `unprofessional` / `rude` was proposed and declined** | The client's own suggestion for shortening the list. Rejected for three reasons, all of which would have cost more than the brevity bought: `unprofessional` and `rude` **overlap almost completely** (rude *is* unprofessional), making it a three-point severity scale wearing label clothes; it **deletes the positive end**, so a genuinely warm agent becomes indistinguishable from a merely correct one — and finding that difference is half the reason the field exists; and `unprofessional` is a **verdict rather than an observation** — careless, casual, distracted and dismissive all collapse into it, so a manager cannot coach it without relistening to the call. The ladder gets the same brevity (four values) while keeping both ends. |
| **Why not just use `conversationQualityScore`** | Considered and rejected. That score asks *did the agent do the right things* against our process standard and returns a **number**; `agentTone` asks *how did they sound* and returns a **label**. An agent can score `"10"` — greeted properly, understood the issue, read numbers digit by digit, closed correctly — and still sound flat throughout, and today that agent is invisible. A manager also cannot filter a 1–10 column to find the sharp calls; they can filter a label. Industry practice keeps QA score and sentiment as separate columns for exactly this reason, and treats **the rows where the two disagree** as the coaching signal. |
| **`professional` is the healthy default** | Stated explicitly, because the same downward pull that broke `customerSentiment` would otherwise make `"flat"` the residual. `"flat"` requires positive evidence of disengagement; absence of warmth alone is `"professional"`. `"rude"`, `"impatient"` and `"flat"` are adverse judgements and section 4 binds — each must trace to a moment in the transcript, and where none does the value is `"professional"`. Never marked down for the flavour of the agent's Hindi, for brevity, or because the customer disliked an answer that could not have been different. |
| **It moves no score — deliberately** | `agentTone` drives nothing: never raises `riskEscalationIndex`, never fails a checkpoint, never changes `conversationQualityScore` or `overallScore`. **`conversationQualityScore` keeps all eight dimensions, including 7 (Empathy) and 8 (Interruptions and talk balance).** Pulling tone out of the quality score was considered and rejected — it would change what an existing dashboard column means mid-stream and break comparison with historical rows. The mild overlap is handled by a consistency check instead. |
| **Consistency checks added** | `agentTone` `"rude"`/`"impatient"` cannot sit beside checkpoint 10 PASS and dimension 8 rated good. `customerSentiment` must agree in direction with `csatScore` — `"positive"` not beside a `"1"`/`"2"`, `"angry"` not beside a `"4"`/`"5"`. And explicitly: **no check binds `agentTone` to any score** — a correct-but-flat call is a real row and the disagreement is left standing, because that disagreement is the whole point of the field. |
| **Two downstream corrections** | §5.3 no-transcript: both sentiment fields return `"NA"`, and `"NA"` overrides their closed value sets — `agentTone` is never `"professional"` on a call with no agent turns to judge. And `riskEscalationIndex` trigger 6 said *"anger the agent absorbed calmly is recorded in `customerSentiment`"*, which under the new ladder is wrong — anger that subsided is not `"angry"`. It now points at `agentTone` and `notes` instead. |
| **`csatAnalysis.sentiment` unchanged** | Still mirrors `customerSentiment` exactly. Nothing was renamed, nothing removed, no field changed type — the change is one added key and a rewritten definition. |
| **Files touched (2)** | CRC `postCallAnalysis/postCallAnalysisHuman.txt`, plus this ledger. |

---

### PCA-04 · `postCallAnalysisHuman` learns the transcript format — IVR preamble, ASR artefacts, and system text wearing a speaker prefix — CRC only (2026-08-19)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — the **CRC-only human-agent analyser** (PCA-02, PCA-03). No CC counterpart. |
| **What prompted it** | The client supplied a real transcript. The prompt had never been told what its input physically looks like — it described the *conversation* in §2 and jumped straight to fields. Every rule below is a defect visible in that one sample. |
| **The format, now stated** | Plain lines, each prefixed `agent:` or `customer:`, no timestamps, no turn numbers, no names. **The prefix is the only speaker evidence** — never re-attribute a line from its tone, language or content. New section **1.1**, placed before §2 so it is read before anything is scored. |
| **🚨 The IVR preamble was about to break `greeting` on every call** | The call opens with recorded audio the transcriber attributes to `agent:` — a brand welcome (*"नमस्कार भारत गैस customer care में आपका स्वागत है"*), the quality-and-recording disclaimer, and **hold music transcribed as speech** (*"घूमती जमीन पे जरा घूम के देखें…"* — song lyrics arriving as ordinary agent lines). **The recorded welcome names भारत गैस**, so a careless read returns `greeting` `"yes"`, checkpoint 1 PASS and dimension 1 good on *every call ever made*, whatever the human said — three columns silently worthless. The rule now: ignore the block entirely, it **ends at the first agent line addressed to this caller**, and `greeting` / checkpoint 1 / dimension 1 are judged on that turn and nothing before it. Stated once in 1.1 and again at all three judging sites, because a rule about the top of the transcript is not where anyone looks when scoring a greeting. Also: the song lyrics are **not** the agent rambling or being unprofessional — `agentTone` is untouched by them. |
| **🚨 System text carries a speaker prefix too** | The sample's transfer failure arrives as **`customer:` The explicit call transfer did not connect. This is an internal instruction…** — formal English, addressed to the agent, describing platform internals, sitting on the customer's channel in a Hindi call. Read as customer speech it would set `summary.callLanguage` to English, could be quoted verbatim into `outcomeEvidence` as a **fabricated customer quote**, and would be scored for sentiment. The rule: such a line is **event evidence, never speech**. It is legitimate evidence for `containment` and `callResult` — a transfer was attempted and failed — but it is not a turn, is never quoted, sets no language, contributes nothing to `customerSentiment`, `agentTone`, `csatScore` or `customerEffortScore`, and **an instruction telling the agent what to say is not the agent having said it**. The agent is judged on the turn they speak *after* it. |
| **ASR partials were about to wreck `customerEffortScore`** | The sample shows *"Booking हो गई है confirmation message"* immediately followed by *"Booking हो गई है confirmation message भी आ गया है."* — a partial and its completion, i.e. the customer saying it **once**. `customerEffortScore` explicitly scores how often the customer repeated themselves, so counting these would mark nearly every call high-effort. Now: **consecutive same-prefix lines are one turn**, and a near-duplicate from the same speaker is a transcription artefact. Only a genuine restatement — said again because the agent did not grasp it — counts. |
| **Backchannel is not a turn** | Standalone `agent: hmm` lines appear throughout. They are never an interruption, never talking over anyone, and never evidence of "bare minimum words" — no effect on checkpoint 10, dimension 8 or `agentTone`. |
| **ASR noise is nobody's fault** | The sample has *"Splendor नहीं मिल रहा है"* where the customer said सिलेंडर, and a garbled opening turn. Neither the customer (unclear, uncooperative, high-effort) nor the agent (failed to understand) may be marked down for transcription error, and **an agent asking a clarifying question after a garbled line is doing the right thing**, not failing checkpoint 3. Unreadable content is absent — `"NA"` — never guessed, and never quoted as evidence. |
| **Code-mixing is normal speech** | Hindi in Latin script and Hindi–English mixing — *"Booking हो गई है confirmation message भी आ गया है"* — is a **Hindi** turn. The everyday English words (booking, delivery, complaint, cylinder, message, transfer) are not a language violation by the agent and do not change `summary.callLanguage`. |
| **Nothing else changed** | No field added, renamed or retyped; no rubric, checkpoint or JSON shape touched. Section 1.1 is input-handling only, and every rule in it exists to stop an artefact being scored as behaviour. |
| **✅ Closed — the sample was a format specimen only** | The reference transcript is unmistakably **Vaani**: it self-identifies (*"मेरा नाम वाणी है"*), speaks CRC's scripted lines verbatim (*"क्या कुछ और L P G से related help चाहिए"*, *"आपकी कॉल ट्रांसफर की जा रही है"*), and carries a `calltransfer` tool Result — so it was raised as a possible routing error. **Client confirmed 2026-08-19: `postCallAnalysisHuman` is routed only human-answered calls.** The sample was supplied to show the transcript *shape*, not the subject. §2's *"There is no AI agent anywhere in this transcript"* therefore stands, and every rule in 1.1 applies unchanged — the IVR preamble, the ASR artefacts and the prefixed system text are properties of the platform's transcript format, not of who answered. Recorded because the risk it would have carried is specific and easy to miss: §3.2's staff latitude — a phone number, an office visit, a quoted price, an offered callback, all fine for a person — would **wrongly exonerate Vaani**, for whom each is an ESC-01 violation. If that routing ever changes, this row is the reason it cannot change silently. |
| **Files touched (2)** | CRC `postCallAnalysis/postCallAnalysisHuman.txt`, plus this ledger. |

---

### PCA-05 · The transcript-format rules reach `vaaniQA` and `postCallAnalysisFlat`; `vaaniQA`'s stale "no transfer" line removed; filler list cut to two — CRC only (2026-08-19)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither. **CRC only by client instruction** — CC is explicitly not to be touched in this pass, unlike the usual "CC pending". |
| **Why it followed PCA-04** | Section 1.1 landed in `postCallAnalysisHuman` only, but **all three CRC post-call prompts read the same platform transcript**, so all three were exposed to the same artefacts. Client direction: guide Vaani's analysers too. |
| **🚨 `vaaniQA` contradicted itself about transfers** | PART C opened with *"THERE IS NO TRANSFER IN THIS CHANNEL… There is no senior desk and no one to hand the call to live."* — pre-XFER-01 text that survived both XFER-01 and XFER-03, while **lines 45, 47, 138 and the whole of C5 in the same file correctly describe the transfer**. A reviewer following the stale line would report every correct transfer as a violation. Rewritten to complaint-first / transfer-second, naming XFER-03 and pointing at C5. Found while checking what `calltransfer` needed, not by looking for it. |
| **`calltransfer` needed nothing** | Proposed and withdrawn. `postCallAnalysisFlat` already judges `callTransferred` **entirely from spoken evidence** — the transfer line `"आपकी कॉल ट्रांसफर की जा रही है।"` with nothing following it is success, the line followed by the team-unavailable explanation is failure, no line is no attempt. That is correct **because the line is `calltransfer`'s own `preToolMessage`, so it exists only if the tool ran**. An earlier suggestion to teach the analysers how tool Results appear was **wrong and dropped — client confirms PCA sees no tool calls and no tool results**, and no rule may assume otherwise. |
| **The IVR block is OPTIONAL, on every channel** | Client correction: Vaani's calls may or may not carry the recorded preamble. All three prompts now say so explicitly, `postCallAnalysisHuman` included (it had described the block as always present), with the added warning not to mistake a genuine first turn for a recording merely because it opens the transcript. |
| **Vaani's first line is fixed, and is the boundary** | `"नमस्ते, मेरा नाम वाणी है। मैं आपकी किस प्रकार सहायता कर सकती हूँ?"` — identical on every AI call. Given to both AI analysers as the marker separating recording from agent, which is a cleaner test than the human file's "first line addressed to this caller" heuristic. |
| **What `vaaniQA` got** | Speaker prefixes are the only attribution evidence; consecutive same-prefix lines are one turn; **near-duplicate adjacent lines are ASR partials, not repeats** — stated emphatically because several of its rules turn on Vaani repeating or re-asking, and a real repeat has the consumer speaking in between; **Latin-script and code-mixed Hindi is not an English turn**, since the everyday English words are *required* by Vaani's own style rules; and the IVR block, whose hold-music lyrics would otherwise read as Vaani rambling, going off-topic or breaking her sentence limit. |
| **What `postCallAnalysisFlat` got** | The same block, plus the **`customerSentiment` rewrite from PCA-03** — it carried the identical two-line definition and therefore the identical collapse into neutral/negative. Same five signals, same middle-and-end weighting, same first-match ladder `angry → negative → positive → neutral`. Also the system-text rule, which protects its `outcomeEvidence` quote field. |
| **`agentTone` deliberately NOT ported** | Client instruction, and correct: Vaani's tone comes from her prompt, not from the call, so every row would carry the same value and the column would score the prompt rather than an agent. `customerSentiment`'s fix applies everywhere; `agentTone` is human-only. |
| **Filler allowance cut from four to two** | `"जी"`, `"अच्छा"`, `"hmm"`, `"umm"` → **`"अच्छा"` and `"hmm"` only** (`"हम्म"` still counted as `hmm`). Client instruction. `"जी"` and `"umm"` now fall under the normal rules and are reportable if a listed rule is broken. |
| **…and the same cut made in the live prompts, to stop a contradiction** | **CLS-03's carve-out permitted Vaani to "open naturally with `जी` or `अच्छा`"** in eight agent prompts. Narrowing only `vaaniQA` would have made QA report an opener the agent was explicitly told it could use — a false violation on every warm call. The carve-out now reads **`"अच्छा"` alone** in all eight: `bookingEligibleAgent`, `bookingNonEligibilityAgent`, `activeDeliveryAgent`, `postDeliveryAgent`, `eligibleDeliveryAgent`, `notEligibleDeliveryAgent`, `paymentAgent`, `genericInfoComplaintAgent`. **This narrows CLS-03**, which is otherwise unchanged — warmth is still allowed, still in the model's own words, still carrying a fact. |
| **Files touched (12)** | `postCallAnalysis/vaaniQA.txt`, `postCallAnalysis/postCallAnalysisFlat.txt`, `postCallAnalysis/postCallAnalysisHuman.txt`, the eight agent prompts above, plus this ledger. |

### NAME-05 · CRC's `promptQA.txt` is renamed `vaaniQA.txt`; CC keeps the old name — CRC only (2026-08-19)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — a **filename change only**. Not one byte of prompt content differs because of it. |
| **What changed** | `bpcl_showroom_crc/prompts/postCallAnalysis/promptQA.txt` → **`vaaniQA.txt`**. Client rename: the file reviews **Vaani's** conduct on a call, so "vaaniQA" says what it is, where "promptQA" suggested it reviews prompts. It sits beside `postCallAnalysisHuman.txt`, which reviews the *human* agent — the pair now reads as the two subjects it actually has. |
| **CC is NOT renamed** | Per the same session's instruction that CC is not touched, `bpcl_contact_center/prompts/postCallAnalysis/promptQA.txt` keeps its name. **This is the recorded difference; without this row it would read as drift.** Rename CC's copy whenever CC is next opened, and retire this row when both match. |
| **Reading older ledger rows** | Every `promptQA` reference dated **before 2026-08-19** — QA-01, QA-02, PCA-01, ESC-01, ZIP-01/02, TTS-01/02, NUM-01/04, CPL-*, RFL-01 and the rest — refers to **this same file** under its old name, in whichever channel that row names. Those rows were rewritten to `vaaniQA` where they are CRC-scoped and left as `promptQA` where they name CC's copy or a historical filename. |
| **Three over-reaches from the rename sweep, corrected** | A repo-wide find-and-replace caught more than it should have, and all three would have made the ledger describe files that do not exist. **(1) CC-scoped rows** — QA-01's *"landed in CC `promptQA.txt`"* and TTS-02's *"3 CRC + CC `promptQA`"* had been rewritten to `vaaniQA`, naming a CC file that does not exist; restored. **(2) `bpcl_contact_center/README.md`** described CC's own folder as running `vaaniQA.txt`; restored to `promptQA.txt`. **(3) Historical filenames** — DRIFT-01 and QA-01 name two CRC candidate variants that existed at the time, `postCallAnalysis_promptQA_merged.txt` and `postCallAnalysis_withPromptQA.txt`; the sweep mangled them into `..._vaaniQA_merged` and `...withvaaniQA` (the second with broken casing), rewriting the record of files that were never called that. Both restored, in `CHANNELS.md` and `bpcl_showroom_crc/docs/POSTCALL_STATUS.md`. |
| **⚠️ Platform config** | Nothing in the prompts points at this filename, so no prompt edit is needed — but **whatever loads the post-call prompt by path must be repointed**, exactly like the `newConnectionAgent_onHold` case. If it is missed, CRC's QA analyser silently stops running. |
| **Files touched (6)** | The renamed prompt, `CHANNELS.md`, root `CLAUDE.md` (naming-defects list), `bpcl_showroom_crc/CLAUDE.md`, `bpcl_showroom_crc/docs/POSTCALL_STATUS.md`, `bpcl_contact_center/README.md`. |

### GCD-09 · `getConsumerDetails` read a rejected mobile number back and called it wrong in the same breath — CRC only, CC pending (2026-08-21)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — shared truth (number capture). Amends GCD-08's confirmation-trigger fix, which only covered the success Result. |
| **What the live call did** | `validatecontactno` returned `invalid_mobile_number` (12 digits captured against a 10-digit expectation). The agent's next turn read the rejected digits back in the STEP 3 confirmation template *and* declared them wrong in the same output: *"कृपया पुष्टि कीजिए, आपका मोबाइल नंबर nine - zero - … - one है?क्षमा कीजिए, यह मोबाइल नंबर गलत है। कृपया अपना १० अंकों का registered मोबाइल नंबर सही से बताइये।"* Three clauses — a confirmation question, a rejection, a re-ask — stacked in one turn, on a number that was never valid to begin with. |
| **Why it happened** | The prompt had a `PARTIAL NUMBER BEHAVIOR` section for an incomplete number, but nothing at all for a *rejected* one. STEP 3's trigger was worded around "a complete, successful number," which an invalid Result technically satisfies (it is complete, just wrong) if the model isn't told otherwise — so it fell through into the confirmation template with no separate handling. |
| **The rule now** | New `INVALID NUMBER BEHAVIOR` section: an `invalid_mobile_number` / `not_10_digits` Result is never the STEP 3 trigger. The agent speaks the "no" alone, in one short line ("यह नंबर सही नहीं लग रहा, कृपया दोबारा बताइए।"), and stops — no digit read-back, no confirmation template, no stacked re-explanation. STEP 3's trigger definition now explicitly excludes partial and invalid Results. |
| **Files touched (2)** | CRC `getConsumerDetails/getConsumerDetails.txt`, plus this ledger. CC's `getConsumerDetails.txt` has the identical gap (confirmed by grep) and is unfixed — port before the next CC number-capture change. |

---

### RTE-05 · `connectionServicesAgent` answered a delivery-status question with distributor details instead of routing it — CRC only (2026-08-21)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — a domain-boundary leak. §14's router already sent booking/delivery questions to `routingAgent`; this is the same live call, not a new rule. |
| **What the live call did** | After registering an unrelated complaint, the consumer asked where their already-booked cylinder would reach them. The agent treated it as a C12 distributor-lookup and read out the distributor's name, address and phone number — proactively repeating the number three times, including one clearly wrong readback — instead of switching to `routingAgent` for what was, in substance, a delivery-status question. |
| **Why it happened** | §1's own framing — *"assume the query belongs to you unless proven otherwise… helping is your default"* — has no scope limit in the prompt, so it read as license to answer anything reachable from injected data, including a question that only sounded like a location question because it was wrapped in "distributor का number दे दीजिए ताकि पूछ सकूँ." |
| **The rule now** | The default-helping bias is now scoped explicitly to C1–C12 topics. A carve-out spells out that booking, delivery, payment, subsidy and new-connection questions are never assumed as this agent's, however they're phrased — including a delivery-status ask wrapped in a request for the distributor's number — and a matching note sits at the C12 module boundary itself, where the agent actually reached for the wrong tool. |
| **Files touched (2)** | CRC `connectionServicesAgent/connectionServicesAgent.txt`, plus this ledger. |

---

### NUM-06 · The distributor's number and the complaint number need different dictation defaults, and one shared rule was giving both the same one — CRC only (2026-08-21)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — shared truth (TTS/number delivery), amending NUM-01/NUM-03's `LONG NUMBER DELIVERY` block, which treated every long number the same way. |
| **What the live call did** | The same call from GCD-09/RTE-05 showed `connectionServicesAgent` reading the ten-digit distributor number in one continuous Hindi-word breath, losing track partway, restarting, and mishearing a piece back (पांच दो चार vs पांच दो सात) before landing it — the exact failure mode a ten-digit number invites when it isn't broken up. |
| **First pass, then a correction** | The first fix made *every* long number — complaint number included — pieced from the first turn. The client's instruction on review: the complaint number is short, unlike a ten-digit mobile-shaped number, so it should stay whole-in-one-turn by default, same as before; only the **distributor's number** (and the SMS/missed-call/WhatsApp numbers, which are also ten-digit-shaped) needed the piece-by-piece-always fix. |
| **The rule now, in the ten complaint-capable agents' `LONG NUMBER DELIVERY` block** | The distributor's contact number and the SMS/missed-call/WhatsApp numbers are delivered in three pieces (3-3-4, ten digits, no country code) from the very first turn, always — piece, then STOP, no question attached, no final "क्या आपने पूरा नंबर नोट कर लिया?" round-up once the pieces are done. The complaint number is the one named exception: spoken whole, in one turn, the first time, exactly as before — it switches into the same three-piece pattern only once the consumer asks to repeat it, slow down, or note it down. `connectionServicesAgent`'s own distributor-number rule (§9 Rule 2) and its C12 `TURN 1` examples were restructured so the number is never folded into the same sentence as the name/address/timing. `vaaniQA.txt` (C5f/C5g) was rewritten to score both defaults as correct rather than flagging either as a violation. |
| **Files touched (14)** | The ten complaint-capable leaves, `Default` and `newConnectionAgent_onHold` (piece-default only — neither holds a complaint number), `postCallAnalysis/vaaniQA.txt`, plus this ledger. CC was not touched — its `LONG NUMBER DELIVERY` block carries the same undifferentiated rule and should be ported on the same terms before the next CC number-delivery change. |

### PARAM-01 · `bpcl_create_complaint`'s two parameters are renamed, and the parameter set is now closed by rule — both channels (2026-09-02 / 2026-09-03)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Axis** | Neither — **shared truth** (§4 complaint discipline, the CPL-01 `PARAMETERS` clause). Landed in **both channels in the same session**, all twenty complaint-capable agents, per §6 rule 4. |
| **Part 1 — the rename (2026-09-02)** | The backend renamed the two agent-supplied parameters: `feedbackDescription` → **`complaintSummary`**, `reason` → **`complaintReason`**. A prompt still naming the old fields emits parameters the tool no longer accepts. `feedbackDescription` was renamed everywhere it appears. `reason` was renamed **only where it names the parameter** — assignments, "I pass only complaintSummary and complaintReason", "never in complaintReason". Three things deliberately keep the word: **CC's `calltransfer` has its own unrelated `reason` parameter**; the ordinary English sense (*"never a reason to transfer"*, *"no complaint number came back, for any reason:"*); and the vocabulary phrases **"reason list" / "reason phrase"**, which name the closed value set the parameter draws from, not the parameter itself. |
| **Part 2 — the source of the extra keys (2026-09-03)** | Client report: the agent was passing **`caseId` and `caseNumber`** into `bpcl_create_complaint` alongside the two real parameters. |
| **Root cause — the list read as the boundary** | Every one of the twenty files stated the contract as an **enumeration**: *"Everything else (consumer id, mobile number, BillingState, DistrictName, name, feedbackDate) is filled automatically by the system."* Six named fields, and nothing saying the list was examples rather than the whole of what the platform owns. A key not on that list — `caseId`, `caseNumber` — therefore read as **unclaimed**, and inventing it looked like helping. The clause forbade six things where it needed to permit two. |
| **Second driver — outputs re-entering as inputs** | `caseId` and `caseNumber` are shaped like what comes **back** in the Result. Once a successful registration sits in the context, its response fields are the most available example of "what this tool's data looks like", and they get composed into the next request. The new clause names this directly: **a value that comes back in a Result is an OUTPUT, never an input** — passing it in means inventing something the platform has not created yet. |
| **The rule now** | `PARAMETERS — EXACTLY TWO, AND THE SET IS CLOSED.` The agent passes `complaintSummary` and `complaintReason` and no third key — not one that looks useful, not one seen in an example, not one that came back in an earlier Result. **Closed by rule, not by list**: any key that is not one of the two belongs to the platform, whether or not it is named. The field names are kept, explicitly relabelled as examples, with `caseId` / `caseNumber` / `complaintNumber` added to them. Closes with a self-check — *if I catch myself composing a third key, I delete it; the call is correct with exactly two and wrong with three.* |
| **Files (22)** | The twenty complaint-capable agents (ten CC, ten CRC), voiced first- or second-person to match each file, plus CRC's `CLAUDE.md` complaint section and this ledger. Also corrected in passing: CC's `eligibleDeliveryAgent` said *"only these **three** and nothing else"* above a two-parameter list — a stale count from before the parameter set shrank. |
| **Not changed** | The historical ledger rows above still say `feedbackDescription` and `reason`; they are the record of what was written at the time and are deliberately not rewritten. CPL-17's literal character check still lists the **old** parameter names among the strings that betray a typed-out tool call — harmless as written (it is a list of forbidden substrings, and both old names remain good tells), but worth adding the new names to on the next pass through that block. |

## 3. Confirmed defects — not intended differences

Fix these; do not record them as policy.

| ID | Where | Defect |
|---|---|---|
| **NAME-01** | Both | `deliveryGenericAgent` is a `switchagent` target in `routingAgent` (`routingAgent.txt:63`, `:132`) with a full routing block. **No such prompt exists.** A generic delivery question reaching `routingAgent` dead-ends. |
| **NAME-02** | Both | `refillSupportAgent` is named as a routing destination in `connectionServicesAgent.txt:224/237` and `newConnectionAgent.txt:2027`. **No such prompt exists.** Mitigated in practice — both agents may only switch to `routingAgent` — so it is misleading guidance rather than a live break. |
| **NAME-03** | Both | CC folder `genericInfoComplaintAgent/` vs CRC folder `genericInfoComplaint/`; `Default.txt:113` and `routingAgent.txt:64` disagree on the name. Canonical: `genericInfoComplaintAgent`. |
| **NAME-04** | Both | `bookingEligibleAgent.txt:1` is titled "(NON-ELIGIBILITY BLOCKER CASE)" but its body is the eligible flow. |
| **CS-01 (fixed 2026-07-28)** | CRC | `connectionServicesAgent.txt` spoke "जी, अभी मेरे पास **आपके distributor** की यह जानकारी उपलब्ध नहीं है" and used the same third-party framing in the C12 Turn-1 examples/pacing script — violating D-03/D-05. Rewritten to name the distributor factually ("distributor का नाम ... है") instead of possessively. |
| **DEF-03 (fixed 2026-07-29)** | CRC | **An unported CC fix — the first item pulled out of the §4 `Default` backlog.** CRC's STAGE 2B listed `"कितने दिन हो गए booking को?"` as a delivery triage anchor (`Default.txt:284`) and demonstrated it in Example 3C (`:337`), directly contradicting its own ABSOLUTE GUARDRAIL at `:30`, which names "कितने दिन हुए" as a violation. CC had already fixed this at `Default.txt:294`; CRC never received it. Confirmed live: an agent asked the day count, welded a second ungrounded question ("क्या आपने इसके बारे में पहले कहीं शिकायत की है?") onto the same turn, and used neither answer. Ported CC's ban line verbatim, adopted CC's merged single-question delivery anchor, and replaced Example 3C with CC's (which routes after one question). Also added a general test to TRIAGE RULES — *a triage question must be able to change which agent you route to; if every answer lands on the same agent it is fact-gathering and is banned* — which kills the whole class rather than one phrase, and reformatted the pipe-delimited anchor lists as numbered "pick ONE" menus, since a single line of `"A" \| "B" \| "C"` reads as a script to deliver rather than a menu to choose from. Nothing landed in CC. |
| **DEF-02** | CRC | 9 "senior team" hits. Reviewed: **8 are legitimate** — recognition lists for detecting the consumer's request — and `Default.txt:89` is a correct prohibition. No action, recorded so the next audit does not re-flag them. |
| **GCD-01 (stale — 2026-07-28)** | CRC | Previously recorded a `getConsumerDetails_v2.txt` beside `getConsumerDetails.txt` with no marker for which is live. That v2 file no longer exists in this workspace — only `getConsumerDetails.txt` is present. Leave this row until a future pass confirms it stays gone, then delete. |
| **DOC-01** | CRC | [agent-workflow.md](bpcl_showroom_crc/docs/agent-workflow.md) §7 links to `customerStatus-spec.md`, which does not exist in the CRC workspace. |
| **DOC-02** | CC | [ORCHESTRATION.md](bpcl_contact_center/docs/ORCHESTRATION.md) §7 records one open question: the exact `ConsumerDetails` field name carrying urban vs rural distributor category. Until answered the 25/45-day gap split cannot be computed and the gate defaults everyone to 25 days. |
| **STRUCT-01** | Both | Only 6 of 15 prompts use `SECTION n:` headers; the rest are flat prose. Inconsistent skeleton makes safe machine-editing much harder. |

## 4. Unclassified divergence — the audit backlog

Line-level divergence between the channels' shared agents. Each file's differences are **not yet
classified** as intended policy (D-0x above) vs. an unported fix. This is the main body of work.

| Agent | CC lines | CRC lines | Differing lines | Classified? |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| `newConnectionAgent` | 1706 | 2186 | 930 | ❌ |
| `subsidyAgent` | 180 | 261 | 316 | ❌ |
| `bookingNonEligibilityAgent` | 219 | 282 | 261 | ❌ |
| `paymentAgent` | 217 | 216 | 257 | ❌ |
| `activeDeliveryAgent` | 179 | 234 | 257 | ❌ |
| `Default` | 463 | 441 | 256 | ⚠️ partial — D-02, D-03 confirmed |
| `postDeliveryAgent` | 285 | 350 | 247 | ❌ |
| `connectionServicesAgent` | 309 | 249 | 226 | ⚠️ partial — CS-01 confirmed |
| `eligibleDeliveryAgent` | 209 | 257 | 202 | ❌ |
| `notEligibleDeliveryAgent` | 171 | 197 | 190 | ❌ |
| `routingAgent` | 275 | 302 | 135 | ❌ |
| `getConsumerDetails` | 120 | 103 | 120 | ❌ |
| `bookingEligibleAgent` | 169 | 148 | 96 | ❌ |
| `emergencyAgent` | 265 | 266 | 34 | ✅ persona only — the control case |

**Counts are stale for two rows as of 2026-07-29.** `Default` and `getConsumerDetails` were both
edited this session (DEF-03 and GCD-02 above) and their differing-line counts have not been
recomputed. `Default`'s divergence went down; `getConsumerDetails`'s went down by the size of the
shared block. Everything else is untouched.

**Read this table as a risk ranking.** `newConnectionAgent` at 930 differing lines and a 480-line
size gap cannot plausibly be explained by escalation and persona alone — the channels have almost
certainly accumulated independent domain content there. `paymentAgent` is the more insidious case:
near-identical length (217 vs 216) with 257 differing lines means the two were edited in place,
separately, and no size signal will ever reveal it.
